The bald man was certain that the black mist figure would die if he punched it.

He is a Cup-path superhuman, the type with enhanced physical abilities. Even with only Sequence 9 enhancement, his own enhancement is still quite impressive. A Moth superhuman at a low Sequence level would have no chance of winning if he got close to him!

I won!

The bald man saw his fist easily pierce through the black mist man's head.

But he suddenly froze.

Is the human brain like this?

He felt as if his punch had encountered no resistance at all, as if he had simply hit a cloud of black mist!

The man in black mist slowly turned around, and the hole in his head where his fist had pierced through it shifted position as he turned.

Only when he turned around completely did the bald man see a pair of blood-red eyes, which now held a hint of surprise.

How dare this guy touch me directly? Is he that bold?

But that surprise had another meaning in the bald man's eyes.

This man in the black mist must not have expected to be unharmed by so many people, which means he had no backup plan!

The advantage lies with me!

With this in mind, the bald man felt more confident. Although he didn't know why the black mist man couldn't hurt him, he was certain that the black mist man couldn't hurt him either!

He quickly took a few steps back, keeping about five steps away from the man in the black mist, and asked sharply, "Who exactly are you? I don't think I've offended you, have I?"

"Since neither of us can do anything about the other now, let's have a good talk. I don't think there are any irreconcilable conflicts between us."

Su Mu looked away and continued mixing drinks.

Although he didn't actually know how, he just thought it was fun to shake a cocktail bottle, especially with human hands... well, not quite human hands.

The man frowned and continued, “Sir, although we are both extraordinary beings, there is no conflict of interest between us. I am the ‘Cup’ and you are the ‘Moth’, and the spiritual energy we need is different. Or do you need my resources? I admit that I have some extraordinary materials related to the ‘Moth’. If you need them, we can talk.”

"You didn't really intend for this girl to pay enough protection money, did you?"

Alice paused in her milk-drinking motion, clearly not expecting Su Mu to suddenly bring her up. She glanced at Su Mu furtively before looking away.

The Lord of the Mist is standing up for me...

This is what Su Mu learned from Ryan's memories.

However, Ryan didn't seem to know the true purpose of this man named Remo.

Perhaps Ryan didn't think this guy's real purpose was important.

It didn't matter to Su Mu; she didn't care about his purpose and just enjoyed the feeling of playing cat and mouse.

Remo glanced at Alice, his expression darkening.

What's going on? How come Alice, this country girl, knows someone with superhuman abilities?

That's impossible, she's never left Hall Village in her entire life...

Could it be someone her parents know?

Remo didn’t know much about Alice’s parents, only that they died in a fire.

What bad luck! This village girl actually has a superhuman protecting her.

"So you've come for Miss Alice. I admit, I did initially intend to use Miss Alice, but since she's under your protection, I won't bother with her anymore. However, you've killed so many of my men as soon as you arrived. Shouldn't you give me some explanation?"

Remo had thought it through clearly: neither he nor his opponent could defeat the other, but the black-haired man had deliberately exposed his weakness.

His mind immediately started working. "I can't kill you, but I can certainly take down this village girl you're protecting. I won't let this village girl leave alive without giving her an explanation."

Alice sensed Remona's undisguised malice and instinctively moved back a little.

Remo was completely unconcerned about her little tricks, and even looked down on them.

What's the point of moving this little distance? Unless you run out of this bar right now, I won't even need a second to catch you.

He turned his gaze back to Su Mu.

"What do you think, sir? I'm not greedy. As long as you can give me something with the spirit of the [Cup], I can pretend that nothing happened today."



Chapter 12 Different Paths to Supernatural Power


“If you can compensate me with some items imbued with the spirit of the [Cup], I'll pretend today's events never happened, and I can guarantee that Miss Alice will not be harmed in my territory in the future.”

“The gangs in this town are just things I created in my spare time. I also have a secret society behind me. I think you should know what that means, right?”

Remo wasn't lying. He did indeed have a secret society behind him, but he wasn't actually qualified to join. He just wanted to use his backer to make the Black Mist Man think twice.

He had already made enough concessions.

Considering that the Black Mist Man had only appeared now, and hadn't appeared when Alice was struggling in the past few years, Remo speculated that he probably didn't live in Hall Town.

Although he didn't know if the Black Mist Man had come to take Alice away from the town, making an enemy of a secret society was certainly not something any ordinary superhuman could accept.

Moreover, [Moths], due to their chaotic and arrogant nature, generally don't organize any secret societies.

How could a wild superhuman dare to fight against a half-step Organization Realm expert like himself...?

Remo stared at the black-mist man, awaiting his answer.

As expected, the black-mist man shifted his gaze from the cocktail bottle to him, his blood-red pupils filled with...joy?

That shouldn't be the case. Someone on one side should at least feel some apprehension upon hearing that the other has powerful backers, even if they're not afraid. Has this guy lost his mind?

“A substantial fortune, though I don't feel I need it, hoarding it still makes me quite happy.”

?

What is this guy saying?

By the way... is this guy speaking Plitvish?

Before Remo could figure out what Su Mu was babbling about, he saw the black mist around the latter suddenly begin to spread, threatening to envelop him.

Damn it, why is this [Moth] so difficult to communicate with!

Remo cursed inwardly, his body immediately taking action.

His target was Alice.

The black mist moved faster than he expected; the moment he moved, it blocked his path between him and Alice.

"A wisp of black mist thinks it can stop me? It underestimates the Cup."

Remo scoffed inwardly, his movements unwavering.

Regardless of the mist's strangeness, he didn't believe his [Cup] pathway power could be stopped by it; he simply thought this moth lacked common sense.

Thump!

The scene he had envisioned—easily passing through the black mist and controlling Alice—didn't occur. Instead, his body felt like it had crashed into a solid wall. He felt excruciating pain all over, and the immense recoil caused him to fall to the ground.

How could this be?

Remo stared in astonishment at the still-spreading black mist.

The mist possessed all the characteristics of fog he knew: hazy, drifting with the wind, and with clear boundaries.

But this "fog wall," which he should have easily passed through, had just blocked him and even repelled him?!

Su Mu silently assessed the situation as he watched Remo being repelled.

"Hmm, although the strength has decreased, it should still be enough to stop a superhuman."

Having made a rough assessment, Su Mu decided to stop toying with his prey and directly controlled the black mist to completely envelop Remo.

Remo struggled fiercely as he was enveloped in the black mist, but he quickly realized with despair that no matter how hard he tried, the mist could hold him firmly, rendering him immobile.

"Wait, wait! You can't kill me! The Holy Communion Church is coming soon! If they find me dead, they'll definitely retaliate! Think it through! Retaliation from a secret society! You can't escape unscathed alone! If you kill me, they'll hunt you down for the rest of your life!"

About a minute later, Remo fell silent, and the black mist seeped into his body and disappeared.

Alice, sitting to the side, watched everything with a sense of感慨 (gan3 kai3, a complex feeling of mixed emotions, including regret and awe).

Although Remo's sudden outburst was clearly aimed at her, and she hadn't even had time to react,

the almighty Lord of the Mist would help her.

Since having this thought, she seemed to have lost her initial fear of these gang attacks.

Thank you for the protection of the Lord of the Mist...

Alice prayed silently.

Meanwhile, Su Mu briefly reviewed the major events in Remo's life.

Similar to Ed, he also bought a book about the "Cup" at a high price on the black market, but the rituals recorded in his book differed greatly from Ed's.

Ed's book involved cannibalism to perform rituals and accumulate spirituality, while Remo's involved mixing drinks.

This piqued Su Mu's interest.

In other words, the same spirituality doesn't exist through only one path.

Furthermore, judging from Remo's memories, this world doesn't only have one type of spirituality.

Sure enough, Ed was inept, spending all his time thinking about cannibalism.

In Remo's memories, there were at least three types of spirituality.

First, the "Cup," representing desire, senses, and consumption.

Second, the "Moth," representing chaos, change, and lies.

Third, the "Heart," representing life, continuity, and cycle.

Moreover, a crucial point in his memories was that the proper path required drinking a potion to enter, while Ed's path, which didn't require potions, was a heretical method!

Furthermore, to advance to the next rank, a transcendent being needs to completely digest the residual spiritual energy of the potions within their body; otherwise, they might turn into a monster or a corpse due to the backlash from the potions during the advancement.

Additionally, transcendent beings themselves are [potions]. After death, the potions consumed by any transcendent being in life will be released due to a rejection reaction; some might be chunks of flesh, others might be eyeballs.

These released substances are the purest potions, requiring no further preparation; consuming them directly is equivalent to drinking the potion of that rank.

The higher the rank of a transcendent being, the more similar their behavior and pathways become, and higher-ranking transcendent beings seem to receive more divine favor.

In Remo's memory, he had witnessed a high-ranking bishop of the Holy Communion Church successfully summon an evil god and receive a blessing.

Seeing this, Su Mu turned his gaze to Alice and couldn't help but ponder.

If this brat becomes a transcendent being, will his sphere of activity expand even further?

Alice felt a little nervous being stared at by Su Mu without saying a word.

"Oh no, why is the Lord of the Mist suddenly looking at me like that? There's a hint of ill intent... No, no, how could the Lord of the Mist have ill intentions towards me? It must be my imagination!"

To reflect on her mistaken thoughts, Alice straightened her back, while cautiously stealing glances at Su Mu a few times.

"Oh no, the Lord of the Mist seems to be able to read minds. Did He know about my disrespectful thoughts just now?!"

"I really didn't mean to!"

Alice sat even straighter.

Putting aside the idea of ​​making Alice choose between the werewolf and the bartender, Su Mu continued to sift through Remo's memories.

"The extraordinary path is a serious matter; it can't be taken lightly. This is currently his only follower, and judging from Remo's memories, both paths are somewhat perverse—one involves cannibalism, and the other uses blood to make drinks. He doesn't want his little follower to become such a pervert."

The Church of Holy Communion seems to possess extraordinary methods related to the "heart." I'll ask them to lend it to me sometime.

..................

............

......

After reading Remo's memories, Su Mu confirmed one thing.

This Byd Remo actually dared to lie to me!



Chapter 13 Director


This Byd Remo isn't a member of the Holy Communion Church at all; he's only met some of its members.

This Remo wanted to join the Church for protection but was rejected because he didn't meet their standards.

So his earlier warnings about being hunted down and the Church sending people were just empty threats to scare him.

Turns out I have to find them myself.

Su Mu had originally planned to control someone from the Church to check their memories for potions and ritual methods related to the "Heart" pathway, but now he's told he has to find them himself.

Dex City.

Su Mu mentally noted the city where the Holy Communion Church was located and decided to go check it out later; Ed happens to have a house there.

Now the main thing is to inventory the loot.

First, there's nearly 300,000 in cash assets. These gangsters are really rich; this bar seems to be entirely Remo's property.

Normally, this bar is just used as a base. The town is mostly populated by elderly people who wouldn't frequent a place like a bar, and besides, Remo's gang is notorious in town; nobody dares to come here.

However, Su Mu could now use this bar as his new base, letting the idle werewolves in the forest come and clean it up.

While thinking this, Su Mu controlled several werewolves waiting around the forest crevice to move towards the bar.

As for the corpses in the basement… he should bury them. Although werewolves can accumulate spiritual energy by eating corpses, and it wasn't him eating them himself.

But the thought of controlling werewolves to eat humans still made Su Mu feel disgusted.

As for the other drinks, they were some special cocktails Remo had mixed. Most of the drinks in the bar were homemade by Remo, and they all contained traces of his abilities.

Although Su Mu, being a cloud of black mist, couldn't drink alcohol, for the sake of potential future customers, he decided to seal them away.

Su Mu felt a pang of regret at losing it, even though it seemed useless; what if it happened?

Under Su Mu's control, Remo began tidying up the mess the bar had made.

He righted the tables and chairs, swept away the bottles and jars, acting like a professional housekeeper.

At this moment, Remo looked up at Alice, who sat beside him with a surprised expression.

“Don’t sit there. What time is it? Why aren’t you home?”

“Ah? Oh! Okay… Lord of the Mist.”

Alice hadn’t expected Remo to suddenly speak to her and didn’t react for a moment, but quickly guessed that Su Mu was controlling Remo to talk to her.

So she immediately stood up and ran out of the bar.

The money Su Mu had given her was left on her seat.

—

The next day, eight o’clock in the morning.

Alice got out of bed, groggily washed up, made breakfast, and yawned as she prepared to go to work.

“Ha, back to work again…”

Work?

The sudden voice in her head startled Alice, instantly snapping her back to reality.

"Ah, Lord of the Mist, you may not understand human society very well. Humans need to work to earn money to..."

Alice was interrupted before she could finish explaining.

"Didn't I give you money last night?"

"Ah, I forgot!"

"...Never mind, after you resign, you can come to the bar."

—"

Chief Cross was in a good mood today.

He carefully tidied his appearance in front of the mirror before leaving the police station.

He was 34 years old, in his prime, but his excessive drinking had made him somewhat overweight.

He had just received notification that due to his excellent management, the crime rate in Hall Town had dropped significantly, and he was being recalled to Dexterity.

Cross had long wanted to leave this godforsaken town.

Besides old men and women and a troublesome gang, there was nothing here.

He was a chief, after all; staying in a place like this would only bury his talents!

Fortunately, even in such a place, he could still find ways to advance his career, thanks to his clever mind.

By directly cooperating with the gang, turning a blind eye to their extortion, he could still get a share of the profits.

Then, he would use the "favor money" from the gang to bribe his superiors, and soon he secured this promotion.

However, he couldn't be too happy yet. His superiors said someone would be inspecting the place this afternoon, and he needed to coordinate with Remo beforehand to make him behave himself, lest their dealings be discovered.

Kross arrived at the bar's entrance, finding it empty and quiet inside, which puzzled him.

Usually, there would be two doormen standing guard at this time; why weren't they here today?

But Kross didn't think much of it, assuming they were all inside drinking.

He pushed open the door, entered the lobby, and instinctively shouted, "Ryan! A bottle of whiskey, get Remo out here."

...

Wait a minute.

Kross's voice suddenly stopped.

He hadn't noticed before; he hadn't heard a thing since entering!

In any other bar, it would be understandable, since there weren't many customers during the day, but this was a gang-owned bar!

Not only had he not heard anything, but he couldn't even see the usual gang members, usually dead drunk!

What was going on?

Did Remo know someone was coming and run away beforehand?

Just as Kross was bewildered, the door behind the bar opened.

Remo stepped out.

Those lifeless eyes looked at Kross.

"Welcome, what would you like to drink?"

Kross didn't know how to describe the feeling Remo gave him.

Those eyes were dull and lifeless, like a soulless walking corpse.

His voice was completely flat, devoid of emotion, and even had some pronunciation problems, sounding as if he were possessed by something that wasn't used to a human body.

"Whiskey, by the way, where are your men?"

Kross forced himself to step forward and sit at the bar.

Although this was strange, what era was this? He didn't believe in those old charlatans.

How could there be gods and demons in this world?

He was still puzzled, though. Remo was usually elusive, only appearing briefly and rarely seen; he could only find him through other gang members. How could he see him so easily today, and where were the others?

"Oh, they've all...gone out."

Something was wrong.

Remo definitely didn't mean "out."

Internal strife?

Or is it going to be a shootout with another gang?

Wait a minute, there's only one gang in Hall Town. Where would they get the chance for a shootout? And surely internal strife wouldn't leave them with just one leader, right?

Besides, there seemed to be a strange smell in the air after Remo came out…

Kross fell into deep thought, while Remo, or rather Su Mu, was also a bit of a headache.

He had just cleaned up the bloodstains below when a man in a police uniform appeared.

And he hadn't seen anything related to this man in all the memories he'd reviewed.

This shouldn't be happening. I absorbed all the sound with black mist last night, how could the police, especially the chief, be coming to my door?

Su Mu knew the man's identity through Remo's memories and also knew about their deal, so he didn't think there was any danger. Besides, what did it matter to Su Mu if Remo's identity was in danger?

But he didn't understand why he'd been targeted. Was the bribe last time insufficient?

Or had his ability been so severely weakened that he couldn't even absorb a few gunshots?



Chapter 14 Going to School?


Su Mu pondered whether he should simply kill Remo and feed the resulting potion to Alice, quickly testing if it could enhance his own stats.

Otherwise, with his current stats significantly reduced, he felt somewhat vulnerable.

While thinking, Su Mu controlled Remo to retrieve a bottle of whiskey from the liquor cabinet behind him, opened it, poured a glass, and handed it to Kross.

"Here."

Kross took the drink and took a sip, then noticed something amiss.

Remo usually practically mixed drinks every day, so why did he pour it for him without doing anything this time?

Remo was acting very strangely today; everything about him seemed off.

Kross felt there was something wrong, but also nothing; after all, everyone has those occasional unusual behaviors.

Perhaps he was frustrated, or perhaps he hadn't slept well.

Kross tried to convince himself, but he couldn't shake the feeling of unease.

He felt he was missing a crucial piece of evidence...

But Kross didn't want to think about it anymore. He didn't care what happened to Remo today, or where the rest of his gang had gone. He just wanted to avoid causing any trouble this afternoon that might hinder his promotion.

Kross picked up his glass, took a sip, and stated his purpose for being there.

"This afternoon, inspectors from Dexterity will be coming. I hope you and your men behave yourselves. If our scheme is exposed, no one will escape unscathed."

Remo glanced at Kross, then nodded, his movements swift and efficient, his body motionless, like a pre-programmed machine.

"Okay, I'll be careful."

Su Mu breathed a sigh of relief. Thankfully, he hadn't been completely humiliated.

Kross finished his drink, put down his glass, and turned to leave.

"That's good. I'll take care of the others in town, making sure they don't dare mention your protection money collection."

Kross had only taken a few steps when Remo suddenly called him back.

"You haven't paid yet."

He paused, turning back with a "What the hell are you talking about?" look.

He never expected Remo to dare ask him for money.

Did this guy want to stay in Hall Town?

Kross quickly understood Remo's meaning.

This bastard was trying to extort money from him using the afternoon disciplinary meeting as an excuse, the damned ingrate!

Kross's face darkened.

"Mr. Remo, you should think carefully. Are you sure you want to charge me for my drinks?"

Remo remained expressionless, simply pointing with his index finger to the price list hanging on the wall behind the bar.

Whiskey: 30 sq.m./glass

Kross felt a vein throbbing on his forehead.

If the higher-ups discovered the gang's activities here, they would definitely send more people. Remo's life wouldn't be easy either. He'd either have to go to jail for a few years or escape somewhere else. Kross didn't believe Remo would dare to do anything reckless.

But he didn't dare gamble.

Remo's acting very strangely today. If he really goes berserk and jumps out, my promotion will definitely be ruined.

Kross returned to the bar with a grim face, took out his wallet, pulled out a 100-dollar bill, and slammed it on the bar.

He said in a low voice, "Keep the change, Remo. I'll say it again, if your scheme is exposed, it won't benefit any of us."

Withdrawing his hand and putting the wallet back in his pocket, Kross left the bar.

"Just you wait," he thought. "Once I get this promotion and leave this wretched town, I'll send men to wipe out your gang. I'll get back all 100 coins I lost today, with interest."

Thinking of his future revenge plan, Kross rudely opened the door, only to find a girl standing outside.

"Alice? What's she doing here?"

Kross recognized Alice; after all, she was the town's famous beauty.

Kross had even considered proposing to her, especially since they were sisters... But considering her parents' death and his own promotion plans, pursuing a minor would severely impact his career.

Alice was about to enter when the door opened, and she looked at the person who opened it with some confusion.

Then she immediately took a few steps back warily.

Alice still remembered Cross, though not well.

Cross had visited her home shortly after her parents' death. Given it was a small town and they had died fighting a fire, it was normal for Cross, as the police chief, to come and pay his respects.

But Cross's motives were impure. Even before entering the house, he looked at her with greedy eyes, and even at her sister!

Moreover, while claiming to be visiting the families of heroes, he repeatedly hinted that she should become his adopted daughter.

When she refused several times, he even threatened her with the property.

If it weren't for the lawyer's intervention, she wouldn't know what to do.

Therefore, Alice had a very poor impression of the police chief.

The chief, seeing the girl's wary demeanor, didn't intend to say anything. Having just been extorted by Remo, seeing the girl only annoyed him more.

"Hmph, little brat."

Cross left without lingering.

Alice entered the bar after Kross left.

Alice hurried to the bar and cautiously asked, "Lord of the Mist, why is Director Kross here? Did last night's events get exposed?"

Remo handed Alice a bottle of milk as usual, briefly explaining their conversation.

Alice nodded, drinking her milk.

Su Mu, remembering something, moved the money Kross had left on the table to Alice's side, then pointed to a small leather suitcase beside her.

"Here you go, along with what you forgot yesterday."

"Ah, thank you, Lord of the Mist."

Alice happily accepted the money, already fantasizing about how to use it to buy her younger sister something nice to eat.

"By the way, don't you need to go to school at your age?"

Su Mu had been wondering about this for a long time. Since he possessed Alice, he hadn't seen her go to school.

"Ah, going to school is for rich people... and there aren't any schools in our town."

Upon hearing this, Su Mu felt something awaken within him.

As he possessed more and more people, some of his forgotten memories from the past returned, though not much; he only knew a little bit now.

Damn it, how can anyone escape the hardship of going to school!

"Then go home and pack your things. I'll take you and your sister to school in Dex City."

No, the thought of her follower not being able to go to school made Su Mu feel terrible.

As the old saying goes, nothing is more difficult than education.

She absolutely had to be enrolled in school!

"Eh?! Really?"

Alice clearly hadn't expected that an evil god would offer her a place to go to school.

She was momentarily excited; it was school! She'd heard that schools offered access to advanced knowledge, and that there were all sorts of noble ladies there...

But Alice quickly calmed down.

Could someone like her, a poor person, really go to school?

She'd heard that the school's admission standards were very strict, and it had to be in Dex City. What about her parents' house?

Where would they live once they got there?



Chapter 15 Inspector


"Um, Lord of the Mist, if we go to Dex City...where will we stay?"

Alice felt it necessary to ask beforehand. After all, the Lord of the Mist was an evil god and might not understand human society.

"I have a house there."

Ed's.

"But am I really qualified to go to school?"

"I'll take care of it."

Based on my understanding of this era, isn't school just a matter of paying money?

Especially in this capitalist society.

As for not having enough money...isn't there the Holy Communion Church?

However, Su Mu couldn't pinpoint the location of the Holy Communion Church's stronghold.

Old Ed's understanding of the supernatural relied entirely on gatherings, and those gatherings had recently stopped. His diary entries suggested he was laying low.

Alice felt her eyes welling up.

When her parents were alive, they had said they would save enough money to take them to school in the city, but due to unforeseen circumstances, that plan fell through. But she had thought about saving money to send her younger sister to school someday.

And now, the Lord of the Mist had so casually arranged everything for her, even ensuring she could go to school.

The Lord of the Mist... was like a mother to her.

"Okay, okay, thank you, Lord of the Mist!"

Alice wiped her eyes with the back of her hand to stop herself from crying.

Then, as if making a decision, she nervously said, "Um... Lord of the Mist, could I tell my sister about you...? She's very tight-lipped, she definitely won't tell anyone."

In Alice's eyes, Su Mu was being too good to her, which gave her the idea of ​​recruiting followers for Su Mu.

However, Alice wasn't the type to betray her sister on impulse; she had made this decision after careful consideration.

She had seen how good Su Mu was to her, so she wanted to try introducing him to her sister as well.

After all, Su Mu was currently only good to her, and although he had said he would protect her sister, she wanted to try and let Su Mu develop a relationship with her sister as well.

Just in case something happened to her someday, leaving her sister without anyone to care for her.

Alice even considered having Lorina offer her soul as well. After all, she hadn't encountered any problems after offering her own soul; perhaps the so-called soul offering was just a test by the Lord of the Mist?

However, it was always better to be cautious about anything involving souls, and besides, Su Mu didn't seem interested in Ryan's declaration of offering his soul...

Could it be that I really have a special connection with the Lord of the Mist?

Alice sometimes had such fantasies.

————

At noon, Alice took Lorina to a bar.

“Sister, do we really have to go in? Isn't this where the gangsters hang out?”

Lorina stood at the entrance, clutching Alice's clothes fearfully.

Alice patted her head. “It's okay, there are no gangsters here anymore.”

Lorina looked at her sister's relaxed expression and relaxed a little as well, entering the bar with her.

The lobby was clean and bright, and there was no trace of the mess from last night.

Lorena was also visiting this place for the first time, her eyes filled with curiosity. After finding no one around, she couldn't help but look around.

Alice, watching Lorena's lively and curious appearance, smiled. Only after Lorena had finished looking around and returned to her side did Alice take a deep breath and say seriously, "Lorena, I have something to tell you now. Don't be afraid."

Lorena, infected by Alice's serious expression, nodded earnestly.

Her eyes, the same bluish-green as Alice's, were filled with tension and seriousness.

Then, Lorena's mouth gaped open, her eyes fixed on the black ring that appeared behind Alice's head.

Next, a figure formed of black mist appeared beside them.

"Sister, sister, what is this?"

Lorena was frightened by the supernatural thing before her, looking at Alice helplessly.

A few minutes later, Alice briefly recounted the events of the past two days.

She selectively omitted the more cruel and disturbing parts, so as not to leave Lorena with any trauma or bad impression.

"So, it was the Lord of the Mist who protected my sister?"

Lorina nodded, seemingly understanding, then stood up and bowed deeply to Su Mu.

"Thank you, Lord of the Mist."

Su Mu was somewhat surprised.

Is a 16-year-old really this quick-witted?

She's quite polite too.

"You're welcome, it was nothing."

After all, your sister gave me her life.

After thanking him, Lorina immediately looked at Su Mu with curious eyes and asked, "Is the Lord of the Mist really an evil god?"

This little brat is so outspoken.

Su Mu thought to himself, but he didn't sense any malice or fear from the girl, only undisguised curiosity and a hint of gratitude and admiration.

"I forgive this little brat. I'll put a lot of pressure on you to study when you start school."

!

Lorina, what are you saying?!

Alice felt like the sky was falling.

She immediately covered the girl's mouth, glared at her, and then looked at Su Mu with some timidity.

"I'm sorry, Lord of the Mist! She...she's still young...please don't take it to heart."

"That's right, I am the Evil God. I'll find you a school to attend. If you don't get full marks on your exams, I'll take your soul."

"Eh, I have to go to school too? I'm a girl."

Lorrina clearly didn't think education was a good thing like her sister, and offered a reason very much in line with the times.

"Even if you were an armed helicopter, you'd still have to go to school."

Watching Lorina banter with Su Mu so skillfully, Alice felt a subtle sense of relief.

Thankfully, the Lord of the Mist wasn't angry with Lorina over the form of address. But what was an armed helicopter?

—

Afternoon, East Road Police Station, Hall Town.

A man who looked to be in his twenties or thirties got out of a car, straightened his clothes and hat, and glanced at the police station entrance.

He wore a tall, dark blue cork helmet with a gleaming brass badge set in the center of his forehead. The helmet's leather chin strap clung tightly to his shaved, bluish chin, maintaining a professional, upright posture at all times.

A crisp, dark blue, fitted long coat clung tightly to his broad chest, its double-breasted brass buttons gleaming from the collar to just above his knees. A white, stiff turtleneck held his neck in place, the number "32" clearly etched on the left lapel, standing out starkly against the uniform.

A revolver peeked out from under his short cloak at his right hip.

Glenner wasn't in a good mood today.

He should have been sipping tea and reading the newspaper at the police station in Dexterity, not end up in this small town after a bumpy ride.

Although he was only a squad leader in the station, a squad leader his age was considered exceptionally talented.

He was just one case away from a promotion; becoming deputy chief, if not chief, was a realistic possibility.

But now, he'd been suddenly sent here to inspect the town's atmosphere.

Moreover, his own bureau chief even hinted that he should relax the standards, which clearly indicated that he had accepted a bribe.



Chapter 16 Bar


A bunch of scum, how can a police station be run properly with these people?!

But Glenner could only put up with it.

He was still working for them, there was nothing he could do.

He could only hope that one day a case would come up that he could solve, and then get a promotion.

This wasn't just a baseless fantasy.

Glenner had absolute confidence in his crime-solving abilities.

The reason was his extraordinary ability inherited from his ancestors.

[The Lamp]

Its characteristics of revealing, enlightening, and illuminating the hidden allowed him to see all sorts of concealed secrets.

Although he was currently only a ninth-level [Scene Recorder], his intuition for "anomalies" allowed him to immediately discover things that others couldn't.

Glenner also used this almost cheat-like intuition to jump from a novice to a squad leader in just one year.

However, recently there hadn't been any major cases, so he hadn't had many opportunities to showcase his abilities.

The reason for sending him to investigate was obvious: to use his 100% case-solving rate to endorse the man who had bribed his chief.

Glenner picked up his pocket watch and glanced at the time.

1:40 PM.

Glenner suppressed his displeasure and walked into the police station.

As soon as he entered, a smiling man with a beer belly approached him.

"Welcome, welcome. You must be the inspector from Dexter City. Please come in."

Cloth smiled and immediately told the other officers on duty to make tea, intending to build rapport first.

Glenner didn't want to establish a connection with him. Although he didn't know what the man had used to bribe his chief, even if he had, he was just a minor official in a remote town. His presence was merely out of respect for the chief.

However, he wouldn't make things difficult for this guy. Although he could easily disqualify him according to strict standards, it was his chief's arrangement. Helping him would make his future promotion easier—a win-win situation.

"No need, let's begin the inspection now."

Kross, not embarrassed, immediately echoed, "Yes, yes, let's set off now. By the way, I haven't asked your name yet. I'm Kross."

"Glenner."

Glenner walked ahead, with Kross following behind on his left, acting as an introducer.

"Our Hall Town is indeed a small place, and the crime rate was high a few years ago. But after I arrived, I immediately started working, leaving no stone unturned in dealing with those criminal gangs. Now Hall Town is much more peaceful. Look, that's Mr. Chris's shop. See how leisurely he's been these past few years? A few years ago, he wouldn't have been so relaxed."

Kross secretly patted himself on the back as they walked.

Although the decrease in crime rate had little to do with him—mainly because all the young people had moved to the city in recent years, and a bunch of old men and women wouldn't cause any major trouble—the fact remained that the crime rate had dropped since he arrived!

Glenner walked around, looking at everyone, his brow furrowing deeper with each glance.

Something was wrong. His intuition told him that these people weren't as relaxed as they seemed.

Although these elderly people chatted and laughed in small groups, even smiling and waving to Klose, presenting an image of harmonious relations between officials and civilians.

But he sensed something was amiss.

The residents wore smiles, but their eyes held no joy; their gazes towards him—or rather, towards Cross—were filled with anger and fear.

His extraordinary abilities, granting him intuition, allowed him to immediately pinpoint the source of this unease.

Clearly, the relationship between the officials and the people of Hall wasn't as harmonious as it appeared; in fact, it was rather tense.

But that didn't matter. The chief's implication was clear: as long as it wasn't an obvious problem, turn a blind eye; as long as he didn't see it, he could pretend it didn't exist.

This was just a formality.

Glenner frowned and glanced at his pocket watch again.

2:07 AM.

"Um, Mr. Glenner, is there... a problem? You see, the relationship between the police and the residents of Hall is excellent."

Seeing Glenner's furrowed brow, Cross felt a pang of worry. Although the chief had said in his reply that it was just a formality, he was still afraid of giving himself away.

"It's alright, harmony between officials and the people... very good."

"Is that so? Haha, it's nothing much. Thanks to the policies I implemented before, the residents are now more willing to trust us police officers, and even the number of calls to the police has decreased significantly. The residents are living and working in peace!"

Seeing the "anomaly" but not being able to point it out made Glenner feel somewhat irritated and uncomfortable, but he suppressed it.

He should just finish this quickly and go back.

For the rest of the time, Glenner silently looked around, while Cross boasted and introduced things, and the residents performed their clumsy act.

As a superhuman of the [Lamp], not being able to expose such a clumsy performance was too torturous. Glenner spent the entire afternoon in agony, checking his pocket watch 74 times to distract himself, and briefly jotting down his observations in his notebook.

The logbook would be handed to the chief upon his return, who would then forward it to headquarters. After review, Cross's long-awaited promotion would be secured, and Glenner had indeed been quite lenient with the entries.

He didn't write down a single anomaly he observed.

He omitted things like anger towards the police, or complaints about poverty despite the low prices in the small town.

But it was still agonizing. After all, the nature of [The Lamp] was to seek the truth, and this was clearly a problem he couldn't investigate.

The good news, however, was that only one place remained to be inspected.

Glenner took out his pocket watch.

5:32 AM.

His gaze fell upon a bar without any sign.

The very existence of this bar was unusual to Glenner.

In a town where 74% of the population was over 50, there was a building clearly catering to young people.

Furthermore, judging from its exterior, this bar doesn't look like it's been abandoned for a long time; on the contrary, it looks quite new, clearly built in recent years.

Even if one could explain it as different from other bars in the city, primarily catering to the elderly, that wouldn't make sense.

It's a little past 5 PM now, close to dinnertime. Those who ate early would probably have already finished dinner. If it were offering services, there should be a few pedestrians around here by now.

The bars in town don't open until after 9 PM, but the old man clearly wouldn't be up that late, so this bar's opening hours must be much earlier.

It would be strange if this bar were unconventional, opening in the morning or early evening.

As the only bar in town, he hadn't heard a single person mention it after walking all over town.

Of course, even that could be explained by the bar's poor performance, but the main reason Glenner was certain there was something wrong with this bar was the unusual expression on Cross's face behind him.

"Um, Mr. Glenner, you see this bar isn't open yet, and we've been walking around all afternoon, how about... we end it here? I'll take you to the best restaurant in town for a meal."

Cross was implying to Glenner that this was the end of the day.

Glenner could tell from Cross's expression.

He was nervous.

Glenner felt his instincts as a "Light" officer were flaring up, but considering his promotion opportunities and the possibility of a lenient inspection, he suppressed his curiosity.

"Fine, I'll just pretend I didn't see anything. What secrets could a small-town bar hold? At most, maybe some officer was drunk inside. Cross didn't want something like that to jeopardize his promotion."

Glenner was just about to write "Bar quiet and unremarkable" in his notebook to end the day's inspection and use the opportunity to gain some benefits for himself at the dinner party,

when the bar door was suddenly opened.



Chapter 17 Testing


A girl with long, golden hair, estimated to be around 160cm tall, was leading another girl with similarly long, golden hair, but only about 140cm tall, out of the bar. They were talking as they walked.

"Lorina, you need to be more careful when you talk to the Lord of the Mist next time. Don't be so rude."

"But the Lord of the Mist isn't angry, and I think he likes to joke around."

"You should call him Lord of the Mist."

The girl gently tapped the other girl on the head and sighed, clearly annoyed by the girl's earlier words.

As soon as they stepped out, they noticed two men standing across the street watching them. They simultaneously stopped talking.

Alice's eyes were wary, while Lorina feigned shyness and shrank behind Alice.

Their reactions were typical of women encountering strange men on the street.

Glenner and Cross, being far away, didn't hear what they were saying.

Neither of them seemed to care.

Now that they've put the door open in front of me, I can't pretend I didn't see it.

Damn it, how did these two brats get here, and at this exact moment!

Kross was furious. He'd been happy all afternoon that he hadn't seen any gangsters in town, which proved Remo understood the risks and benefits. But now, these two had suddenly appeared out of nowhere, and he couldn't help but suspect Remo had done it on purpose.

Glenner straightened his collar and said to Kross beside him, "Since it's open, let's go in and take a look."

"This, okay, okay..."

Kross was seething with anger, but he couldn't do anything about it. Glenner had already said so; if he made an excuse now, wouldn't he seem guilty?

No problem. Glenner hadn't noticed anything wrong with the residents, so Remo shouldn't be either.

Thinking about how the residents he'd subtly hinted to through his officers hadn't been exposed, someone as shrewd as Remo shouldn't be either.

Kross reassured himself as he and Glenna approached the bar.

"Two ladies, still here so late? This isn't a place for girls."

Glenna approached and politely greeted them, inquiring about their presence.

It was an occupational hazard; seeing anything unusual or someone nearby made him want to investigate.

Alice's eyes scanned his clothes, finally settling on the number "32" on his left collar.

Alice vaguely recognized it; the uniform seemed to be that of a city police officer.

However, she wasn't sure. She'd never been to the city, only seen it in the newspapers before. The police in her town didn't have a uniform, but she was probably right.

"I work here. This is my sister."

Alice answered Glenna's question honestly. She had already heard from the Lord of the Mist that city police officers would be coming.

The Lord of the Mist told me to answer whatever I asked, not to worry about it at all, and that if I was dissatisfied with Director Kross, I should just say bad things about him, and he would protect me if anything happened.

However, to avoid causing trouble for the Lord of the Mist, Alice decided to keep a low profile. At least, she couldn't discuss the Lord of the Mist. As for the director, if he didn't ask for her opinion, she'd let it slide this time; but if he held a grudge, it would be troublesome.

Alice thought to herself.

"Is that so? It seems this bar's opening hours are quite unconventional."

Glenner glanced at the bar and continued, "What's the name of this bar?"

"It doesn't have a name."

Glenner and Alice exchanged questions, while Cross, standing nearby, was on tenterhooks.

He was terrified that Alice would speak ill of him because of his past.

After a brief chat, Glenner ended the conversation and decided to go inside the bar himself.

He hadn't gotten anything useful, but he could tell that this girl named Alice was hiding something.

This only fueled his curiosity about the bar.

He pushed open the door and went straight in.

There weren't the groups of people drinking and boasting that he'd imagined.

The bar was deserted, so deserted that only a bartender remained behind the counter.

But considering it was a small-town bar, that was quite normal.

However, there was something unusual: he smelled blood.

It was faint, but definitely there.

Cross followed Glenner in, and upon entering and seeing Remo, immediately called out, "Remo, two bottles of whiskey, this guy's a big shot in town!"

He shouted this for two reasons: to curry favor with Glenner and to warn Remo not to have a seizure.

Glenner detected a hint of nervousness and ingratiation in Cross's voice.

Interesting.

Glenner sat down at the bar, and Remo pushed two glasses of whiskey in front of him.

The moment Glenner saw Remo's face, he noticed something was amiss.

Remo's features closely resembled the profile of the perpetrator in a bloody murder case in Dexter City a few months ago.

Although their police station only had a suspect profile and absolutely no concrete clues,

the combination of the bloody smell he'd caught upon entering confirmed Glenner's suspicion: this guy was the killer!

So he'd been hiding in this remote town... This was exactly the big case I needed!

Glenner's excitement surged. Curiosity was justified; if he hadn't acted on his unusual curiosity through the "light," he might have missed this opportunity to catch a murderer!

Calm down. Pretend not to notice for a while. As a murderer, he'd be wary of the police; he couldn't alert him.

But Cross was actually protecting a murderer. No wonder he was so nervous. Forget about promotion this time. Once I catch Remo and hand him over, you might even lose your job.

Glenner glanced at Cross sitting beside him, took a sip of his drink, and then casually asked, "You work at this bar?"

"This bar is mine."

Robbed.

Su Mu added in his mind.

"Really? I'm curious, can you make money running a bar in such a small town?"

"I don't know."

Glenner frowned.

It wasn't because of Remo's answer; he'd seen plenty of uncooperative people like that.

But what made his intuition race was Remo's attitude.

Emotionless words, mechanical movements, and those lifeless eyes.

He'd only ever seen that kind of look in corpses.

This Remo was strange.

Glenner tensed up.

He'd never met such a bizarre person before.

"Really? By the way, can you manage such a big bar all by yourself?"

Glenner's hand had unconsciously moved to the revolver at his waist; he'd fire at the slightest movement from Remo.

Su Mu felt something was off.

Didn't this guy see Alice before he even came in? Why was he asking this?

"There are employees."



Chapter 18 Mutation


“By the way, Mr. Remo, how long have you been running this bar? I’ve never heard of it in town.”

Here it comes!

Kross’s heart leaped again. He picked up his glass, took a sip, and then gave Remo a meaningful look.

Remo didn’t even look at him.

“I don’t know.”

Glenner felt a vein throbbing in his temple.

Even the fear he had felt at the beginning because of Remo’s inhuman behavior vanished.

Remo truly knew nothing; he didn’t know anything about it.

Glenner wasn’t going to waste any more words.

He simply stood up, pulled out his revolver, and pointed it at Remo’s head.

"Mr. Remo, come with me."

Kross was startled by Glenner's sudden action and immediately stammered, "This... Mr. Glenner, Mr. Remo doesn't usually go out, so that's why... Please don't be angry."

Glenner glanced at him and said leisurely, "A few months ago, a murder occurred in Dexterity. The victim was a 25-year-old young lady, the wife of a nobleman. The murderer was Remo. Kross, are you going to protect him?"

Kross was dumbfounded.

What's going on? Remo isn't just a gang leader, is he? Although gang killings are common, how dare you kill a nobleman!

Kross immediately reacted and retorted righteously, "What! Remo, you actually killed someone! I really misjudged you!"

First, he needed to distance himself; he had to immediately prove his ignorance.

Even so, a promotion was unlikely this time, but not doing this meant not only no promotion but also potentially losing his job. Keeping his job was paramount; promotion could come later.

“Sheriff Glenner, keep an eye on him. I’m going to the station to get reinforcements right away!”

With that, Cross ran out.

He did this not only to show his stance but also because…

This Remo was a mob boss. Although he hadn’t seen any of his men all day, they might be hiding. What if he got surrounded if he didn’t run now?

Remo was a ruthless man who dared to kill even noble lords. Let alone a lowly chief like himself, he probably dared to kill even Glenner!

He needed to go back and get everyone from the station.

If Glenner died, he could use his numerical advantage to take down the mob. Although his station would likely suffer heavy casualties, if he could take down Remo, his future would be limitless!

Remo killed the noble patriarch, and if he killed him, those noble patriarchs would definitely promote him!

If Glenner miraculously survived the encirclement, he could use the favor of saving his life and helping him solve a murder case to build a relationship with him.

Even if he failed the inspection this time, he could easily pass the next one.

Anyway, he wouldn't lose out.

————

Kross escaped, but Glenner didn't care.

He already had Remo under control; surely an ordinary person wouldn't be afraid of a gun?

Just when Glenner felt victory was assured, he met Remo's gaze.

This eye contact revealed something unusual about Remo.

His eyes weren't the usual blue or gray here; they were red.

It was crimson, like freshly spilled blood, and that crimson hue seemed to flow in his eyes.

And deep within his eyes, there appeared to be a wisp of black mist.

Glenner froze, then blinked, but saw nothing.

However, the hairs on his body stood on end as if in response; the [Light] in his mind was frantically alarming, warning him that this person was dangerous!

Glenner instinctively took a step back, his brain racing.

What was that? A hallucination? Some kind of peculiar illness?

Or… was he also a superhuman?!

The moment this thought crossed his mind, Glenner felt a chill run down his spine.

He had never seen another superhuman; his knowledge of supernatural abilities came entirely from his ancestors' notes.

He only knew that advancement required rituals and potions, and that there were eight paths to superhuman ability.

But apart from the [Light], he knew nothing about the characteristics of the other paths, or how to deal with them if he encountered one.

Glenner's hand gripping the gun trembled slightly, but he quickly gritted his teeth to stop the shaking and said with a steely resolve, "Mr. Remo, put your hands behind your head now, or I'll shoot!"

He was taking a gamble. Glenner could only pray that Remo's supernatural abilities wouldn't allow him to withstand the bullets.

After calming down a bit, Glenner began to analyze the possible limits of Remo's supernatural abilities.

To kill a nobleman's wife while he was being escorted by guards showed his exceptional assassination skills.

Judging from the guards' testimonies, they all lost consciousness instantly. Considering the guards weren't dead, it proved he hadn't used firearms, but rather relied solely on physical strength—his physical prowess was immense.

Furthermore, his speed allowed him to knock out multiple people before the guards could react.

Most importantly, since Remo chose to flee here after the killings, he must be wary of the police's firepower.

In other words, firearms could still harm him, and Glenner still had a chance of victory.

The advantage was on his side!

With the mental enhancement provided by the [Lamp], Glenner's thought process took less than a second.

Glenner took a few steps back, creating a safe distance to ensure he could react quickly enough while maintaining his pistol's accuracy.

"Mr. Remo, I know you're a superhuman, but you'd better think carefully. If you resist now, I'll shoot immediately."

Remo remained expressionless, simply asking calmly, "How did you figure that out?"

Su Mu was somewhat surprised. From what he understood, superhumans should appear no different from humans. How did this person know?

Some kind of supernatural ability?

Glenner hesitated for a moment, but quickly made a decision.

"Your disguise is clumsy, or rather, you've already begun to mutate. Your eyes have started to show inhuman characteristics, and I have the ability to resolve this mutation."

Glenner's honesty was primarily to buy time.

He was certain Remo couldn't ignore the damage from firearms, but he wasn't sure of the limits.

The best course of action now is to stall for time by discussing topics that interest Remo. Once Cross arrives with fully armed police officers, Remo will have no chance to escape.

As for how to determine Remo's interest, it wasn't just about Remo asking. Another factor was the notes left by his ancestors, which stated that if one couldn't resist the corrosive influence of the whispers during the digestion of the potion, their body would mutate. Initially, only small-scale anomalies would appear, but later, entire limbs would transform into inhuman structures.

No superhuman could accept becoming a monster, and would undoubtedly try to find a solution. He could easily use a false possibility to keep Remo in suspense.

After all, given the involvement of secret supernatural beings, no one could be certain of the truth of such a matter.



Chapter 19 Suppression


Su Mu was somewhat surprised. He understood the concept of "mutation"—the backlash of a potion, something Remo remembered.

But what did it mean that he, or rather Remo, had already undergone a mutation?

He hadn't seen it in the memories.

"What kind of mutation?"

Su Mu asked, controlling Remo, though he wasn't particularly concerned. Remo was useless to him; while the synergy from mixing drinks was good, requiring human blood was too extreme, and he'd never actually mixed one himself.

"Your eyes are starting to turn crimson, and there's a black mist surrounding them. If I'm not mistaken, you often hear muttering, right? That's the result of the potion backlash."

Muttering? Black mist?

What?! It's all so real.

Su Mu immediately realized, but this guy saw the black mist he was controlling in Remo's brain...

?

Holy crap!

Su Mu was genuinely shocked this time. His possession had been seen, even though he didn't know it was his own possession and thought it was a backlash from the potion. But his black mist had been seen.

He had observed the possessed individuals from other humans' perspectives before, but the conclusion was that ordinary people, or even those with the [Cup] level of superhuman ability, couldn't see themselves while possessed.

Should he kill him to silence him...?

No, killing someone who hadn't done anything would make him seem like an evil god. He had to have some moral principles.

Besides, this guy had only seen the black mist of possession.

Another reason Su Mu chose not to kill him was that Glenner had seen Alice but hadn't noticed him inside her, proving that Glenner didn't have that strong an ability to open boxes. Letting him live wouldn't threaten Alice or himself.

For a moment, Glenner suddenly felt the [Light] within him start alarming wildly. He almost instinctively fired or begged for mercy, but the [Light] alarm quickly subsided, leaving him somewhat bewildered.

What's going on? Has his intuition gone haywire?

Glenner gripped his gun tightly and continued, "I have a way to solve this for you, provided you cooperate and return to Dexterity first."

Does he think I'm stupid?

Su Mu wanted to roll her eyes at him; his nervousness was practically overflowing when he lied.

It would take at least an hour to get here quickly from the police station; Su Mu still had time to play around with him.

Bang!

The door was violently pushed open.

Glenner was initially overjoyed upon hearing the sound, then somewhat puzzled.

Great, backup has arrived!

But did Cross move so quickly? He arrived with his men in less than 10 minutes?

Glenner turned around, Glenner was stunned, Glenner couldn't believe it.

"Wolves...weren'e?!"

Two werewolves, each 3 meters tall and covered in fur, stood at the door.

After pushing open the door, the two werewolves walked straight towards Glenner.

"Don't come any closer!"

Glenner immediately turned his gun on the werewolf.

This was the first time in his life he had ever seen a werewolf, a creature he'd only ever seen in storybooks.

Squeak.

The small door to the side of the bar opened, and Remo stepped out.

Glenner saw Remo and was about to turn his gun to stop him, but the werewolf steadily approaching made him hesitant to act rashly.

The spirit of [the lamp] was frantically alarming in his head, urging him to run away immediately.

Bang! Bang!

Driven by his spiritual alarm, Glenner couldn't withstand the pressure and fired at the two werewolves' heads.

But after firing, Glenner felt even more desperate.

The 11.43mm bullets only left small dents in their fur, without even a drop of blood.

Glenner gritted his teeth and fired at the werewolves' hearts and shins, but still couldn't cause any damage. Only after emptying the magazine did Glenner come to his senses and uncontrollably stumble back a few steps.

Glenner's composure crumbled, and fear began to show on his face.

"Don't come any closer!"

Glenner hurled his empty pistol at the werewolf, but it couldn't budge its advance.

The mere sight of werewolves, fantastical creatures existing only in stories, before his eyes was already unbelievable, but now these creatures were displaying physical resilience unaffected by firearms.

How could a police squad leader, just making a brief appearance in a remote town, encounter something like this?

Glenner regretted it. If he'd known it was this dangerous, he would never have come.

He was only a ninth-tier [Lamp], inherently weak in direct combat, and had only been a superhuman for less than a year.

Glenner had given up on any chance of advancement and planned to escape through the gate before the two werewolves could run.

Although he didn't think he could outrun the two werewolves, what if they targeted the closer Remo? Wouldn't he have a chance to survive?

The fantasy was appealing, but as soon as he started running, one of the werewolves blocked his path.

The werewolf grabbed him by the neck and lifted him up.

The immense strength rendered Glenner unable to struggle; his legs were off the ground, and he could only writhe haphazardly.

"Remo, help me! If you don't help me, no one can save you from your mutation!"

Glenner could only hope that Remo would lend a hand.

But when he laboriously turned his gaze to Remo, he found his demeanor and movements strikingly similar to these werewolves—emotionless and driven solely by efficiency.

Remo walked straight towards him, and the other werewolf made no attempt to stop him.

They were in cahoots?!

"Wait, wait, let me go! I can give you a way to resolve this!"

Glenner grabbed the werewolf's fingers, trying to pry them open, but the werewolf wouldn't budge.

Remo didn't respond, but slowly placed his hand on Glenner's head.

The instant the contact occurred, Glenner's spiritual intuition exploded, a sharp alarm instantly filling his mind.

It was like a death knell, or perhaps a hallucination; Glenner seemed to see a cloud of black mist enter his brain along Remo's arm.

Then he fell silent.

The werewolf put Glenner down.

Like the werewolf and Remo, Glenner calmed down and found a seat.

Su Mu, on the other hand, was reviewing his major life events.

Born into a middle-class family with a little money, excelled academically in school, graduated from police academy, and rose from rookie to squad leader in just one year.

He had passed the background check, and aside from frequently complaining about his superiors being instigators, he had surprisingly not done anything wrong.

This made Su Mu somewhat emotional. It seemed there were still some good people in this world. Almost everyone he'd possessed was a CS (Cybernetic) user, so this guy who hadn't even killed anyone was a bit unusual.

Alice didn't count; he hadn't possessed her.

And what was most remarkable was that this guy was also a superhuman.

A superhuman without any abnormal hobbies was truly abnormal. Although he'd only met two superhumans so far, judging from their memories, the entire superhuman community was anything but normal.



End of Chapter 20


However, this may also be related to Glenner's extraordinary heritage.

It was left by my ancestors, and even the potions were prepared. I just need to drink it and digest it slowly. Unlike other superhumans, I don't need to do any quick-money "jobs" to buy a ritual or potion ingredients.

In summary, the second generation.

However, he felt that he had discovered the traditional skills of the extraordinary.

Tell a lie before you die...

Ed, Remo, and this guy named Glenner—none of them told the truth.

But it doesn't matter, since you can basically know everything by possessing it anyway.

Su Mu now roughly understands why Glenner can see his true form.

It's probably because of the unique nature of the [Lamp]'s spirit that this guy hasn't seen other superhumans before. Otherwise, he would have a clearer understanding of his role. This is basically a human superhuman radar. The red color he saw should be the spirit of the [Cup].

However, since I haven't committed any major evils, it's not good to find a reason to kill... so I've been possessed.

Moreover, he was quite interested to see if accepting a superhuman follower would expand the scope of his descent.

Under Su Mu's control, Glenner shook hands with Remo, and Su Mu's black mist returned to Remo through the contact, though not all of it was withdrawn.

Glenner's thoughts returned, but he had no memory of being controlled, and he looked around blankly.

But they didn't see the two werewolves that Su Mu had already controlled and left.

"What's going on? Am I hallucinating?"

You weren't hallucinating; you really did see the werewolf.

A emotionless voice appeared in his mind, and Glenner stood up abruptly, instinctively reaching for his waist only to find nothing.

What is that? Is there a voice in my head?

Oh no, have I already started to mutate?!

"Stop arguing. I don't want to waste my breath on you. In short, you're under my control now, so you have to listen to me, understand?"

The words came from Glenner's mouth, but he hadn't even thought about saying them, or even about speaking!

This is not delirium; delirium cannot control the body of a superhuman.

"Stop thinking nonsense and answer me, either die here or listen to me."

"I'll obey your orders! I'm willing to follow them!"

Without further hesitation, Glennard said this.

Although he hadn't figured out the situation yet, he was certain that he only had one option: to obey.

His own body was even controlled by this unknown entity, meaning that the entity could kill him at any time, sparing his life only because it felt he was still useful.

The only thing I need to do now is find a way to prove my worth; only in this way can I survive.

Glenner had no choice but to agree to whatever this mysterious being said next, but he was now more curious about the identity of this mysterious being.

I remember the last thing I saw before I lost consciousness was...

Remo placed his hand on his head.

Combining this with the mysterious presence currently within oneself...

Could it be Remo's extraordinary ability?!

Glenner began searching for Remo.

Still behind that bar...

"Lord Remo, what do you need me to do?"

He used honorifics in his address.

But Remo didn't answer him as he expected. Instead, he looked up at him, and this time Glenner saw an emotion in his eyes—a kind of...speechlessness?

"This guy is dead."

Remo said this.

?

What's the meaning?

Isn't he still standing in front of me?

"Don't worry about those trivial things. All you need to know is that Remo died because he resisted. Just do as I say and don't ask any more questions."

Remo was also controlled by that mysterious being?!

Glenner felt that his entire worldview had been shaken.

How could that be? Remo is still a living person in every way.

Just as he was in a state of confusion, his spiritual intuition brought him a new perspective.

If the physiological characteristics are still present, and there is still breathing, then what died... was it the soul?

Glenner believes in the existence of the soul, after all, he himself has superpowers.

This explains Remona's mechanical movements: it was due to the loss of his soul.

Glenner felt a chill run down his spine.

That unknown being didn't actually need to say so much to him; he could have easily become like Remo, but he didn't.

Then why keep yourself?

Afraid of being exposed to the police?

It's possible, but wouldn't it be more convenient to directly control oneself? Others wouldn't be able to detect that they are being controlled.

Did they want to profit from his police officer status?

As I said before, just take direct control; there's absolutely no need to go through all that trouble.

It can't be because he's kind-hearted, can it?

So what is the point of keeping yourself here?

"You work at the Dexterity Department, write me a letter of recommendation for my school."

......

...

?

Remo's words were devoid of emotion and did not sound like a joke.

But... what does it mean to write a letter of recommendation for your school?

What do you mean? Are you trying to tell me you're still a minor and want to go to school?!

Even if you are a minor, with your abilities, what's the point of going to school!

Glenner felt he had suffered a severe psychological shock.

"What nonsense are you thinking? Answer me."

"able!"

Although he couldn't understand it, Glenner complied.

————

Su Mu was in a good mood. The new use of the black mist he had just figured out was quite practical. Simply put, it allowed the black mist to stay inside his body like a parasite. As long as it didn't touch his head, it wouldn't cause any harm. If he wanted, he could also quickly control his head and kill him directly.

As for why it wasn't hidden directly behind the head like Alice did, it's mainly because the intensity wasn't high enough.

This wisp of black mist descended through Alice, and its value was not as high as that of the one that descended through the rift. It dissipated as soon as it left the body, so this was the only option.

I also managed to get a letter of recommendation from the school.

Of course, that's secondary; the point is that since he has this status, we should make the best use of him.

Most importantly, he has already possessed her, and if we take it slowly, it is entirely possible to turn this Glenner into her follower as well.

He wasn't worried at all that Glenn wouldn't encounter danger. This was a world with superhuman abilities, and Glenn was a police officer. From what he knew, these superhumans wouldn't dare call themselves superhumans if they didn't have at least a dozen lives on their hands.

Glenner's future is definitely bright.

First, pretend to be an expert, making him think I'm unfathomable. Then, when he encounters danger later, I'll appear as a divine intervention to save him from dire straits. This will definitely boost his faith in me.

Su Mu's plan certainly wouldn't be so casual. He could also use Glenner's identity as a police station squad leader to obtain some official intelligence, and perhaps even find some information about extraordinary pathways.

Most importantly, Su Mu didn't believe that in a world where all extraordinary paths had their own inheritance, there wouldn't be an official organization for extraordinary individuals. If Glenner infiltrated the official organization for extraordinary individuals, he could use him as a mole.

This also helps to prevent Edward's superhuman identity from being exposed in the event of a conflict.

Ed didn't care at all about dying, but after arriving in Dexterity, he still wanted to use his identity as Ed to give Alice the status of an adopted daughter so that she could go to school.



Chapter 21 Arrest


It would be too ridiculous if Alice and Remo's house were raided right after they finished school.

As a great god—whose identity was still unknown—he couldn't allow himself to make such a mistake.

"By the way, do you know what to say when you see Kross?"

Su Mu controlled Remo to speak.

"I know! I was mistaken, Remo isn't a murderer!"

Glenner didn't hesitate at all.

Su Mu, controlling Remo, nodded in satisfaction and added, "We can't completely let Kross go. Report his collusion with the gang."

————

Forty minutes later, Kross arrived at the bar entrance with a group of police officers carrying kerosene lamps, surrounding the bar.

Kross raised a megaphone to his mouth.

"Remo, you're surrounded! Surrender now, or our guns won't have eyes!"

After shouting, Kross waited a few more minutes, and seeing no movement from inside, he sensed something was wrong.

"Damn it, did they kill Glenner and run away? If he gets away, Officer Glenner will be dead here with me..."

Cross was terrified. If he really died before the killer was caught, his career would be buried along with Glenner's.

He grabbed the collar of a nearby officer and shoved him forward.

"You, go in and see what's going on."

The officer stumbled from Cross's rough action, then pointed incredulously at himself.

"Chief... me?"

He was just a rookie, and you expect him to go in and confront those gangsters?

Cross ignored the man's nervousness and fear, threatening directly, "Stop talking nonsense, or I'll fire you right now!"

Under Cross's threat, the rookie officer had no choice but to walk towards the bar.

Just as he was about to push open the bar door, it was pulled open from the inside.

The rookie officer was startled and immediately took several steps back, fumbling several times before finally drawing his pistol from his waist, his hands trembling as he gripped it.

The other officers surrounding the bar's entrance also nervously pointed their guns at the opened door.

Inside, Glenner dragged the unconscious Remo out, his face grim.

Cross was initially taken aback, then immediately rushed forward, loudly proclaiming in a concerned tone, "Oh, Officer Glenner, are you hurt? Quickly take Mr. Glenner to the hospital! You guys take this murderer back to the station and lock him up!"

Cross wasn't genuinely concerned about Glenner's injury; he just wanted to make a good impression.

After all, he was almost certain that his affair with Remo had been exposed, and now he could only appease Glenner while stubbornly denying everything.

Glenner shook his head.

"I mistook him for someone else. This Remo isn't the murderer."

"What?"

Cross didn't understand.

"So you just misread it?"

Glenner didn't give Kross a chance to press further, instead pulling a list from his pocket and waving it in front of him.

"Admit it yourself, or should I read it for you?"

Kross's worried expression froze instantly.

What did that mean? Did he have evidence of his collusion with Remo?

That shouldn't be possible. Remo, a gangster, couldn't possibly keep records of every payment, could he?

Was he bluffing me?

Klose immediately feigned ignorance, asking, "Mr. Glenner, what are you talking about? Did that Remo say something to sow discord between us? You can't believe him; he's a gangster!"

Glenner looked at him coldly.

"A gangster? I never said Remo was a gangster, how did you know?"

Damn it, he let it slip!

Klose felt a cold sweat break out on his forehead.

"Ah, this, this is my guess. He dared to target a nobleman; he must be someone powerful, with connections. Mr. Glenner, this matter must be thoroughly investigated!"

Glenner didn't want to waste any more words. He slowly walked up to Klose and patted the list on his shoulder.

"This records the amount of his monthly bribes. Do you want me to personally check the accounts at your police station? I think you know the consequences of falsifying accounts."

It's over.

That was the only thought in Klose's mind.

With all the witnesses and evidence in his hands, he could no longer pretend ignorance.

Kross knew he couldn't refute it now, but he couldn't beg for mercy in front of his subordinates. He could only feign innocence, saying, "Mr. Glenner, investigate as you see fit. My conscience is clear."

Then he whispered in Glenner's ear, "Mr. Glenner, I really didn't want to do this. You see, if a gang starts causing trouble, my police force is simply powerless against them. Please, sir, show leniency. I'll apologize to you tomorrow."

Kross intended to try bribing the prosecutor again.

But Glenner clearly didn't want to discuss this with him and directly handcuffed him.

"We'll talk when we get back to Dexter City."

"Sir, this is all a misunderstanding, a misunderstanding!"

Kross panicked. He hadn't expected Glenner to give him no room to maneuver, handcuffing him in front of everyone.

He wanted to struggle, but dared not; if he were charged with assaulting a police officer, he'd be finished.

He still had one chance: waiting for Chief Glenner to bail him out; he'd bribed him handsomely over the years.

If an investigation really came, neither of them could escape.

The other officers, clearly not expecting this turn of events, stood frozen, exchanging bewildered glances.

How could they have come to raid a gang only to have their superior killed?

Should they continue guarding him?

After handcuffing him, Glenner glanced at the remaining officers, guns still raised and bewildered, and said coldly, "What are you all standing there for? Put your guns away. Or are you all his accomplices?"

The officers immediately lowered their guns and shook their heads repeatedly.

"Sir, we really don't know anything. We just get our fixed salaries every month."

They were joking; who would stand with the chief? He always got the money, and they were always the ones taking the blame.

Every time a resident reported that gangs had robbed someone or something, the police could only bury their heads in the sand. Over time, the gangs held a grudge, and the officers ended up spending twice as much on groceries as everyone else.

In a sense, those gangs not only exploited the residents, but they also exploited the police officers.

————

6:56 PM, Hall Town Police Station.

Glenner finished reporting the matter to his superior via telegram from his office. His superior, being astute, immediately changed his story upon hearing that Cross had colluded with the mob, distanced himself from Cross, and sent reinforcements to Hall.

However, he still subtly hinted that Glenner shouldn't continue.

Glenner had no intention of going against his superior.

But this wasn't because he was cowardly; rather, it was the mysterious entity controlling him that compelled him to do so.

That mysterious entity seemed to want to eliminate them all at once, and didn't want him to alert the enemy.



Chapter 22 The Church of Holy Communion


After finishing the follow-up work, Glenner drove away that night, with Kross strapped to the back seat of the police car.

As for Remo, he was immediately locked up in the local detention center.

Remo was arranged by Su Mu; after all, he was a relatively useful fighting force, and it was better to keep him here to guard the base. Although he didn't think his stronghold in the forest would be discovered, it was better to be safe than sorry.

---

The next morning.

Alice and Lorina had just finished breakfast when Alice heard Su Mu's voice, telling them they could go out.

As soon as the two stepped out with their luggage, they saw a car parked at the door.

The car window rolled down, and Edward, in the driver's seat, looked at them and said, "Put your luggage in the trunk yourselves."

Alice's first reaction upon seeing that face was fear.

It was the first time she had felt the fear of death, but Alice quickly relaxed.

There was nothing to be afraid of anymore; the Lord of the Mist would protect her.

The owner of that face was already dead.

"So this is what cars actually look like," said Lorina, looking at Edward.

"Is this the cultist? His eyes look terrifying. Do all the people controlled by the Lord of the Mist have eyes like this?"

Alice playfully tapped Lorina on the head, but had no intention of lecturing her.

Because the Lord of the Mist seemed completely unconcerned, and was even quite talkative.

"Don't expect too much from the range mode. If you want high-performance mode, I can make his eyes sparkle too."

Su Mu's tone was relaxed, showing no anger at Lorina's curiosity.

What kind of adult gets angry with a child?

Having someone to lighten the mood is quite nice; Alice is still too serious.

—

Afternoon, Dex City.

Ed drove back to his residence.

The two girls, upon getting out of the car, immediately leaned against the wall, covering their mouths as if they were about to vomit.

Su Mu was quite understanding; cars of this era were indeed quite sturdy, their only use perhaps being to display wealth.

However, Su Mu didn't feel anything, because he completely blocked the sensory sharing with Ed, as easily as playing a driving game.

His house was quite luxurious, a two-story villa, but there was practically no furniture left. The previously mountain of garbage had been cleaned up under Su Mu's control; the only problem now was that it was empty.

But now that he had money, it would be enough to buy a few things.

As for what to buy, Su Mu left it all to the two young girls; he was rather lazy and uninterested in such things.

The two girls were very excited when they heard they were going to buy furniture and go shopping, not only for the feeling of having money to buy things, but also for the novelty of shopping in a big city.
