Vivian had just explained the structure of the Security Bureau to him. Generally, there's only one Security Bureau per city, and the captain's position is similar to the chief of his police station. Furthermore, a captain's strength must be at least at the seventh rank to hold this position.

Is this a seventh-rank powerhouse? Will the mysterious entity within me be discovered...?

Although Su Mu told him he could rest assured, his life was at stake, and he couldn't help but feel nervous.

The man noticed Glenn's nervousness and smiled kindly. "Don't be nervous, young man. Just treat this as a casual chat. There's no entrance exam here."

"Okay..."

Glenn calmed himself and looked at the man, awaiting his question.

He couldn't just call me over for a quick look, could he?

"First of all, we apologize for secretly investigating you, but it was also to ensure the safety of the residents."

The man frankly admitted to secretly investigating Glenn.

"That's to be expected, for the safety of the residents. But have you investigated every superhuman?"

"Ahem..."

The man coughed lightly, his eyes slightly evasive.

"Well, you're the only superhuman we've discovered..."

?

Glenn felt somewhat confused.

The only one discovered...

So, an organization with such a powerful background only discovered me as a superhuman?

The intelligence that mysterious entity sent me indicated there were at least three eighth-tier cultists in the entire city of Dex.

Glenner was somewhat puzzled. Was the Security Bureau too... incompetent in investigation, or had he been too careless?

After much thought, he realized it was probably his own fault. After all, since becoming a superhuman, he hadn't really tried to hide anything. His resume should be unbelievable to anyone.

A 100% case-solving rate with not a single wrongful conviction…

It seemed he really was a bit outrageous.

“Ahem, anyway, our Security Bureau needs your abilities.”

The man steered the conversation back on track, continuing, “Your principle should be [The Lamp], and your abilities are related to investigation, right?”

Glenner nodded.

“Your investigation is quite detailed.”

“…”

The man felt he couldn't continue the conversation; that one sentence had ruined it.

“We need your abilities to help us investigate an organization.”

The man chose not to respond and directly changed the subject.

"What organization?"

"The Church of Communion."

"The Church of Communion?!"

The man glanced at the somewhat agitated Glenner and asked, "Judging from your tone, you've heard of this organization?"

Then he went on to explain for Glenner.

"Indeed, the Church of Communion is quite well-known in the underworld."

The man said solemnly, “About a year ago, our Security Bureau investigated and discovered that the Church of the Holy Communion might be present in Dex City. However, we only officially confirmed it yesterday; they have already established themselves in Dex City.”

Your investigative capabilities are terrible! A year to confirm the news, and only yesterday?

Glenner couldn't help but complain inwardly, feeling annoyed that he had been found by such poor investigative techniques.

Seeing that Glenner didn't intend to speak, the man continued, “You should have heard at least a little about the atrocities committed by the Church of the Holy Communion.”

He pushed a stack of documents in front of Glenner.

“Take a look at the atrocities committed by the Church of the Holy Communion within our Kingdom of Plit.”

Glenner picked up the documents, and the first thing he saw was the large red text on the cover.

In 1894, in Agra City, the Church of the Holy Communion held a ritual to summon the Goddess, killing 200,000 civilians and three fifth-tier transcendents.

The date was ten years ago.

Agra City...

Glenner remembered this incident. News reports at the time stated that a series of explosions in multiple factories within the city, caused by aging equipment and delayed detection, resulted in 130,000 deaths and 20,000 missing.

Now it seemed the news had concealed the truth.

Glenner was horrified.

The Holy Communion Church was a complete terrorist organization, actually forcing an entire city into a sacrifice.

Glenner glanced at the report and continued scrolling down.

In June 1900, the Holy Communion Church attacked the Asif City Security Bureau, resulting in the disappearance of three sealed artifacts and the deaths of seven eighth-tier superhumans.

He had just learned from Vivian that sealed artifacts referred to superhuman items possessing extraordinary power, but whose improper use could be life-threatening. Sealed artifacts also had different levels, nine like superhumans, but generally, a superhuman of the same level was inferior in ability to a sealed artifact of the same level.

These were major incidents resulting in heavy casualties. Besides the events that nearly paralyzed the entire city, there were numerous serial murders and mass blood sacrifices.

Just a cursory glance suggested that the Church of the Holy Communion had already taken over 200,000 lives.

Glenner felt a tightness in his chest.

He simply couldn't imagine the scene behind these numbers.

Seeing that Glenner had looked enough, the man spoke: "The entire Church of the Holy Communion consists of transcendent beings who adhere to the [Cup] principle, and the [Cup] is the most anti-human of all principles. Desire, indulgence, depravity, bloodlust, and so on are their synonyms. They've harmed countless Plits over the years, but our Security Bureau isn't just sitting idly by."

The man picked out a document and handed it to Glenner.

Glenner

took it and glanced at it.

Special Operations Plan against the Church of the Holy Communion Headquarters.

"Two years ago, our An Yin Bureau obtained the location information of the Holy Communion Church's headquarters and then organized a raid on the Church. The An Yin Bureau, along with the [Winter] Principle Silent Sons Church, launched a surprise attack on the Church's headquarters."

Thirteen bishops, two archbishops, and 72 believers of the Holy Communion Church were killed. Thirteen spiritual items and four sealed artifacts were seized.

"The operation can be considered a great success. The Church of the Holy Communion has been silent for two years since the raid, but their influence in the underworld is too deep. Even so, they haven't been completely wiped out. Our Security Bureau has been opening branches in various locations for the past two years to find the remnants of the Church of the Holy Communion."

The man took a sip of tea and continued, "I'm not familiar with other regions, but here, even after receiving information, it took us a year to finally find a clue about the Church of the Holy Communion."

"Therefore, we need your help. The [Lamp] principle has a natural advantage in investigations, and it will also help you digest the potion, won't it?"

The man revealed his true purpose.

The Security Bureau lacked capable individuals who could gather intelligence, and Glenner could fill that gap perfectly.

"I can guarantee your safety. Although our branch has only been established for less than two years, we do have sealed artifacts distributed by headquarters. One of them can protect the holder from one fatal injury. If you are willing to join the Security Bureau and take on the responsibility of investigating and tracking the Church of the Holy Communion, I can give you that sealed artifact."



Chapter 34 Sealed Artifacts


"What are the containment conditions for that sealed artifact?"

Glenner was intrigued, but still asked first.

If it was the kind of artifact where you'd lose half your life to save one, he'd have to think carefully.

"The containment conditions are simple: withstand its increasingly intensified babbling over 24 hours. Once 24 hours have passed, you've successfully contained it."

"Just that simple? What does the babbling look like?"

"Not particularly simple. You need to stay in the room with it for 24 hours. But don't worry, we'll be watching you outside for 24 hours. If anything goes wrong, we'll rescue you."

"As for the form of the babbling, it's similar to when you drink a potion, but in the last hour, the quantity and intensity of the babbling feel like a seventh-tier potion."

Seventh-tier?!

I'm only ninth-tier! Is this going to kill me?

Seeing Glenn's surprise and wariness, the man added, "Don't worry, I've been there. I've handled cases before. It sounds like it's easy to go insane, but it's actually not that bad. It won't turn you into a monster like with a potion. At most, you'll just be mentally exhausted for a month or two."

*Just accept it, I'll protect your head.*

Su Mu's voice echoed in Glenn's mind again, but after the previous rehearsals, Glenn was almost used to it.

"I accept."

The man's brow relaxed.

"Thank you for your kindness and courage, Mr. Glenn. It's getting late. Let's discuss the specifics of the work tomorrow. We'll arrange it with your director."

The man stood up and extended his right hand to Glenn.

Glenn also stood up and shook his hand.

"Oh, right, I forgot to mention, my name is Black."

"My name is Glenn, but you already know that."

"...Haha..."

Black chuckled awkwardly twice, then prepared to see Glenn to the door.

Glenner suddenly spoke up, "Wait a minute, Captain Blake, I have some intelligence here."

Glenner hadn't forgotten the induction gift Su Mu had given him; now it was time for that gift to shine.

---

8:37 AM

Blake sat at his desk in his office, his right hand pausing its writing motion. His left hand trembled slightly as he picked up his teacup and took a sip of black tea, easing the dryness in his mouth.

He picked up the intelligence report he had personally copied, his hands even trembling slightly with excitement.

It contained intelligence on the Holy Communion Church's branch in Dex City, even including information on the three extraordinary individuals who frequently appeared at this branch!

While its authenticity wasn't yet certain, it at least gave their Security Bureau a clear target.

"I'll send someone there to check it out tomorrow!"

Blake then looked at Glenner with admiration.

Only a ninth-rank, and already providing such significant assistance to their Security Bureau so soon after joining.

This kid is terrifyingly powerful; he must be carefully nurtured!

"Glenner, did you find all of this out by yourself?"

"...Yes."

Glenner felt a little guilty, but could only agree.

He couldn't very well say that someone else had helped him get it, could he?

Blake smiled even more broadly.

"Okay, okay."

Blake chatted with Glenner for a while longer before letting him go home.

Glenner sat in the carriage heading home, a mix of emotions washing over him.

A monthly salary of 30,000...

He was finally feeling better.

*It's over. Has the school been arranged yet?*

*It's all arranged. Alice and the others can start school in a week.*

*Hesitating*

—

Back home, Alice was still somewhat dazed.

Partly because the nearly one-hour round trip made her feel nauseous, and partly because she was no longer an ordinary person.

As soon as she opened the door, Lorena poked her head out of the kitchen.

Lorena looked into Alice's eyes and said, "Sister, come help me! I don't know how to cook the other dishes."

"Ah, okay."

Alice went into the kitchen and took over the cooking task, while Lorena watched her cook, learning how to prepare.

Twenty minutes later, the two were sitting at the table eating when Ed, still soaking wet despite having dried a bit, opened the door.

It wasn't raining outside. Just to be on the safe side, Su Mu drove to the river to wash the blood off Edward before returning.

Lorina, while eating, asked, "Lord of the Mist, would you like to eat with me?"

Alice, hearing this, nervously glanced at Su Mu, somewhat hoping he would try her cooking.

"No need. This body can survive without food."

Su Mu shook his head. These bodies, possessed by his black mist, could survive entirely on the energy provided by the mist; eating was a rather inefficient way to obtain energy.

"I see..."

Alice was a little disappointed, but understandable. After all, Su Mu was a god; why would he need to eat?

Lorina glanced at Alice's disappointed expression and continued, "Want to try some? My cooking is delicious!"

"Besides, Lord of the Mist, aren't you curious what the food we ordinary people eat tastes like?"

Don't act like I've never eaten before.

Su Mu thought to himself.

In his past life, he had eaten... well, for how many years?

Tragedy enough, Su Mu had absolutely no memory of how long he had lived before transmigrating.

"Alright, I'll try some too."

Su Mu decided to try it, not just because he was persuaded by Lolina, but also because he wanted to confirm his human identity through eating.

"I, I'll get you some food!"

Upon hearing Su Mu's reply, Alice excitedly got up and went to the kitchen, bringing out a plate with two loaves of toast and a plate of roasted meat drizzled with sauce. She placed them on the empty seat next to her, then looked at Su Mu expectantly.

After sitting down, Su Mu felt awkward using the knife and fork beside him. He cut a slice of toast with butter and ate it.

Mmm... delicious.

Su Mu chose to fully immerse himself in the meal, experiencing the texture and flavor of the bread in its entirety.

Although it seemed somewhat different from what he ate in his past life, this was the first meal he had eaten since transmigrating.

He felt like he had returned to the city from a deserted island.

Is this what food tastes like?

Since transmigrating, the only smell I've encountered has been blood. Is this how delicious food is?

Su Mu used her knife and fork to cut off a piece of meat and ate it.

Delicious.

Lorena leaned closer to Alice and whispered, "The Lord of the Mist seems to eat just like us."

Alice, seeing the satisfied expression on Su Mu's face as he ate, felt a sense of relief and even joined Lorena in a quiet discussion.

"Yeah, this is the first time I've seen such a wide range of expressions on the face of someone controlled by the Lord of the Mist."

"Is this what they call high-performance mode?"



Chapter 35 My Own Body


"It's a pity I'm using someone else's body. If only I still had my own..."

Just as this thought crossed Su Mu's mind, the black mist began to move on its own.

Alice was still chatting quietly with Lorina when black mist appeared from the ring behind her head.

"Sister, there's black mist coming out from the back of your head!"

Lorina looked at the black mist that had appeared behind Alice's head with curiosity. It wasn't coming from inside her head, but rather appearing out of thin air about 10cm behind it.

Lorina could roughly guess what it was, but this was the first time she had seen it with her own eyes.

"Eh?"

Alice also saw it; the black mist gathered from behind her onto Edward, who was standing beside her.

"Lord of the Mist?"

Alice wasn't sure why Su Mu had summoned the black mist at this moment, but she usually only saw it during battles.

So she instinctively pulled Lorina into her arms, nervously looking around.

After about a minute of silence, the black mist dissipated.

And Ed, who had been sitting, vanished.

In his place stood a handsome, refined young man, still wearing Ed's wet coat, which looked excessively large and ill-fitting on him.

His long black hair even trailed on the floor, and his eyes were black with a hint of blood red.

His build wasn't particularly muscular, but he was quite tall, and his demeanor was somewhat gloomy.

In short, he was handsome, unlike anything Alice had ever seen before.

Wow, so handsome!

Wait, who is this?

The young man was somewhat dazed.

A cloud of black mist hovered around the young man, scrutinizing him from all sides, even coalescing into a blood-red eye to observe him more clearly.

Holy crap, possession!

Su Mu had previously thought that being able to control others was already a powerful form of possession, but after Alice's upgrade, his possession ability had become even stronger.

It's not the kind of control over stronger superhumans, but rather the ability to directly decompose the flesh and blood of the possessed, using black mist to create a body of the same kind, mimicking its particle structure.

It's not so much a identical body as a similar one, but rather a body of the same kind.

Ed's original body had completely vanished under the black mist's decomposition and analysis. This body was created by the black mist by imitating human body structure and utilizing its own properties.

In every aspect, it has absolutely nothing to do with Ed's body; it's merely a body created by combining human body data with the [Cup] spirit.

Su Mu's current organs have no relation to Ed, and are even largely unrelated to humans; they are entirely created by the black mist.

However, with the human data provided by Ed's body, the appearance created by the black mist is far more realistic than the humanoid black mist it previously created haphazardly.

But in reality, this body contains no traceable genetic samples, because it is composed of the black mist and the flesh and blood created by the black mist controlling the [Cup] spirit.

If someone were to try to determine the genes through the skin or hair, they would find nothing.

His body was a bug—a bug where DNA didn't exist—completely defying common sense.

Or perhaps the black mist was actually his DNA?

Su Mu wasn't quite sure, after all, he wasn't purely human anymore.

However, this possessed self didn't seem to be rejected by the world like his black mist form.

But there were drawbacks.

Su Mu picked up a knife and cut his finger. Instead of blood, a thick black mist appeared.

He had no blood in his body; the black mist served as blood.

Furthermore, since this body couldn't be used as a vessel for descent like Alice's, the black mist couldn't leave the body for long.

Therefore, his current state was more like wearing a human skin to avoid the world's rejection.

Sounds like a wicked god.

And although the black mist had recorded human body data, creating a similar body that could evade the world's rejection required consuming spiritual energy.

Creating this body had almost completely depleted Edward's [Cup] spiritual energy, so this body possessed no spiritual energy whatsoever.

Through his other eye, Su Mu looked at the entire body and felt a sense of familiarity.

Was this what I looked like in my past life?

"So handsome! But why is his hair so long? Did he include all the growth I've accumulated over these 30 million years?"

As this thought crossed her mind, the long hair began to dissipate from the ends, turning back into black mist, eventually settling at a suitable length.

"That looks much better."

"Um, Lord of the Mist?"

Alice asked tentatively.

Su Mu turned to look at her, then nodded.

"Yes, it's me."

The Lord of the Mist actually looks like this?

Alice was shocked. In her understanding, shouldn't such evil...non-righteous gods have a bunch of eyes and tentacles, making them incredibly terrifying?

How come the Lord of the Mist looks so...so handsome, almost cute.

No, no, that's too rude of me!

By the way, the Lord of the Mist actually has black hair? This is the first time I've ever seen this hair color.

"The Lord of the Mist's hair is black!"

Lorina, unlike Alice, didn't think much and immediately moved closer to Su Mu, curiously poking his hair with her finger.

"It doesn't feel any different to the touch."

"Hey! Lorena, what are you doing?!"

Alice quickly slapped Lorena's hand away.

How dare you touch a god's head like that!

Su Mu didn't mind, instead generously saying, "Go ahead and touch it. What's wrong with a god satisfying a believer's curiosity?"

Su Mu was overly excited. After thirty million years, he had finally become human again.

Although, thanks to his good education, he hadn't become a murderous psychopath after thirty million years, he was still afraid that he might go insane again one day.

And now, Su Mu had a much stronger sense of being "human."

Alice is simply my lucky star!

"Sister, even the Lord of the Mist said so!"

Lorena looked at Alice with a pitiful expression, then moved closer to Su Mu, poking his arm and touching his hair.

The main reason was that Su Mu didn't look like an ancient person from a story who had lived for an unknown amount of time; on the contrary, he looked remarkably young. Lorena even felt that Su Mu looked the same age as her.

"His skin feels so good to the touch."

To Alice, Lorena's words sounded like an invitation to commit a crime.

*Gulp... Anyway, even the Lord of the Mist has agreed...*

Alice also tried poking Su Mu's face.

It feels so good...

This is a relaxed, everyday scene, while on the other side, a different picture emerges.

At Ens's home, Ens and Miria had just finished a heated exchange in bed.

Ens, naked, picked up a cigarette from the bedside table and started smoking.

Miria lay contentedly in bed.

Both were used to this situation.

"The Cup" is like this: all actions are based solely on one's own desires.

Ens disliked Miria's arrogant personality, but he quite liked her body.

"By the way, hasn't Zobert returned yet? I haven't had a proper chance to get to know him properly."

Miria asked.

Ens glanced at her.

Entering a state of sagehood, Ens felt disdain for Miria's decadence.

“He needs to check on the situation after he gets back.”

Miriam didn't care; that was just her personality, and these indulgent thoughts had only increased since she drank the potion.

But none of that mattered. In fact, the more decadent she became, the faster she advanced; the more she indulged, the faster the potion was absorbed; and even her mentor was captivated by her body.

She enjoyed this feeling.



Chapter 36 False Information


Zobert returned to Ens's house, where Ens and Miria were already dressed and sitting in the living room.

"You're back. How did it go? Is he backed by any organization?"

Ens asked first upon seeing Zobert return.

"Yes, it's another [Cup] organization, located outside the city. They seem to have some supernatural items. The old man's control was broken. I saw him talk to someone, and after that, his mind returned to its pre-transaction state. I estimate that person is at least a peak eighth-tier supernatural being."

This was fabricated by Su Mu himself.

Since the Holy Communion Church's plan was to double-cross them, he would play along, giving them some false information, deliberately exaggerating his strength to see if he could trick the mentor into showing up, and then use Zobert as a expendable tool to test the mentor's strength.

If he couldn't fool them, it didn't matter; it was just an experiment, and he had a backup plan.

It was strange, though. Although the three were nominally the mentor's disciples, Zobert was clearly not well-received. Aside from his initial entry into the church and his advancement to eighth tier, Zobert had absolutely no right to meet the mentor.

Normally, the only person with contact information for the mentor is Ens, but judging from Zobert's memories, Miria seems to have a way to see the mentor as well, leaving Zobert completely oblivious.

Therefore, even possessing Zobert's body, he has no way of knowing the mentor's strength.

He can handle eighth-tier opponents now, but judging from Zobert's memories, once a seventh-tier [Cup] is reached, one could easily defeat ten eighth-tier [Cups].

If that mentor has the ability to inflict fatal damage on Alice before he can activate his domain, he's finished.

Besides, the Security Bureau will probably be sending "warmth" (a term of endearment) tomorrow. If he eliminates the two now, wouldn't his gift to the Security Bureau be meaningless?

Su Mu also plans to infiltrate his mole into the higher ranks.

As for why he said the control was lifted... Edward is dead, not even a corpse. If he said the control wasn't lifted, wouldn't not seeing him tomorrow give him away?

Eighth-tier peak?

Ens and Miria were both bewildered when they heard this. Weren't there only nine levels of transcendence?

What's this "peak" thing?

Su Mu noticed their confusion and suddenly realized he might have given himself away.

Damn it, he'd read too many novels in his past life. This world's transcendence system didn't seem to have an early, middle, late, and peak division...

"It means you're about to advance to the seventh rank. We're at the early eighth rank..."

Su Mu roughly explained the four strength divisions within the same rank.

The two suddenly understood.

So that's what it meant?

Indeed, this detailed breakdown seemed more reasonable. After all, the strength of an eighth-rank person who had just drunk the potion was definitely different from that of an eighth-rank person who had fully digested it.

Just the level of proficiency with their transcendent abilities was enough to create a significant difference. Furthermore, before the potion was fully digested, the occasional delirium would interfere with their combat power.

Moreover, they had completed all nine stages, and they could feel that when they first became superhumans, their superhuman abilities were not as powerful as they were when the potion was almost fully digested because they hadn't fully digested it.

Both were astonished by Zobert's ranking system.

So this quiet guy was actually quite clever?

Their initial impression of Zobert was that he was aloof and unsociable, not at all like a [Cup] superhuman. They had no idea why their mentor had taken him in.

Miriah praised him approvingly, "No wonder he's a top student from a prestigious university; his brain works really well."

"Next time the mentor calls me alone, I'll tell him about this ranking system. Maybe I can even get some superhuman items..."

Miriah was already planning how to use Zobert's ranking system to her advantage.

"Wait, how did you know he's at the peak of the eighth rank?"

Ens's question caused the atmosphere among the three to freeze again.

Right, superhumans don't have a connection; aside from certain specialized sensory pathways, it's basically impossible to determine whether someone is an ordinary person or a superhuman.

Su Mu felt a bit stupid.

This online novel ruined me!

Although a preconceived notion led to a slight mistake, Su Mu quickly found a reason.

"I deduce that someone capable of detecting and dispelling hypnosis must be at least an eighth-tier cultivator, and certainly stronger than those of us who have just entered the eighth tier."

This reasoning was sound; someone below the eighth tier couldn't possibly dispel an eighth-tier's extraordinary abilities.

Ens was convinced and glanced at Zobert, asking suspiciously, "Why did you change your clothes?"

Millia also noticed this and said, "Still in a suit? Want to come with me tonight?"

"My original clothes got muddy while I was tracking them."

Su Mu had already prepared an explanation, and Ens didn't pay much attention. Milia was used to Zobert ignoring her and was thinking about which noble's house she should stay at tonight.

Ens frowned and said, "The control was dispelled, but you still alerted them."

Then he looked at Milia, his eyes filled with reproach.

Millia was also somewhat embarrassed.

She had just said everything was fine, and now trouble had struck.

“Well, so what? The three of us eighth-tier can just fight them off.”

Millia continued stubbornly, unwilling to admit her mistake.

“Are you deaf? There’s at least one peak eighth-tier. What if there are other eighth-tier or even seventh-tier players? How are you going to fight them?”

“I…”

“Enough with the ‘I’. I’ll tell our mentor the truth about this tomorrow.”

The three parted on bad terms.

Millia and Zobert returned to their respective homes, while Ens frowned again after Zobert left.

He sensed something was wrong with Zobert.

Although Zobert had always been expressionless, this time the deathly aura was particularly strong, and the idea of ​​peak eighth-tier was something someone like him wouldn’t think of.

Forget it, maybe I’m just overthinking it?

Ens shook his head.

Although the three of them were students under the same mentor, they weren’t actually very familiar with each other.

In fact, there was some friction between them.

The reason was simple: the resources their mentor gave them were limited. If one person received more rewards, the other two would receive less.

All three of them wished for the other two to die, a relationship that destined them to know nothing about each other.

—

March 23rd, 9 AM, overcast, South District of Dex City.

Ens returned to his mentor's mansion and told him about his gains from the previous night.

He also preemptively informed his mentor about Zobert's detailed classification of the extraordinary levels, embellishing Miria's actions from the previous night.

The mentor's expression darkened noticeably upon hearing about Miria's behavior; he clearly disapproved of her presumptuous actions.

"Your new perspective on the extraordinary levels is excellent, and it can be considered a contribution to the Church. This is for you."

The mentor took a small box from a nearby cabinet and pushed it towards Ens.

Inside was the bone ring he had brought to the ferry yesterday.

Ens exclaimed excitedly, "Thank you for your kindness, Mentor."

*Heh, Zobert, thanks to you I was able to obtain such a valuable item.*

Inwardly mocking Zobert's foolishness, Ens accepted the bone ring.

“Continue to observe. Once that organization lowers their guard, I'll take care of them.”

“Yes, Master.”

—

Meanwhile, Blake sat by the window of a second-floor coffee shop a street away from Enss's residence.

He held a camera made from a modified telescope, pretending to photograph the scenery while observing Enss's residence.

A portable lamp sat on the coffee table in front of him.

Across from him sat a man in a suit, wearing glasses and white gloves. Beside the man was a long object wrapped in black cloth.

“Captain, is the information reliable?”

The man's name was Edric, 27 years old, an eighth-tier [Battle-hardened] of the [Blade] Principle [Killer] pathway, primarily responsible for direct combat with superhumans.

He covered himself completely in a suit because his body was covered in dense scars, even his hands were filled with scars of varying sizes, almost no intact skin was visible. He had to dress like this to avoid attracting attention.



Chapter 37 The Security Bureau's Operations


Blake took a sip of coffee and replied, "Very credible. That's intelligence from the [Lamp] Code."

Edric clearly didn't trust information from a newcomer to the Security Bureau.

"Excuse my bluntness, Captain, after investigating for so long, we can say we know that guy's background inside and out. He's never been in contact with any supernatural forces. The source of his potions is uncertain, but it's probably inherited from his ancestors."

"His blatant display of his supernatural abilities makes me certain he knows nothing about the Hidden World. In other words, he became a superhuman simply because of luck. He probably doesn't even know how many Codes there are. For someone like that to produce intelligence we haven't been able to uncover in over a year right after joining the bureau, do you think that's reasonable?"

Edric paused, glancing around.

Because they had the entire second floor booked, there was no one else there.

After confirming no one was coming up, he whispered, "I strongly suspect that guy might be an undercover agent..."

Blake glanced at him and retorted, "Edric, you just said yourself that he hasn't had any contact with other supernatural forces. Don't you find it strange that you've come to the conclusion that he's an undercover agent?"

"That was until the day before yesterday when he was the captain. The police station he used to work for sent him on an outbound mission, ostensibly for inspection. Sending a police squad leader to inspect the countryside? That alone is very strange."

Edric gave his reasoning, and Blake didn't refute it, but instead offered his own.

“Your reasoning is sound, but we can’t let this opportunity slip away. Edric, you know our An Yin Bureau’s lamps have been blessed by the Church of the Holy Light. They didn’t sound an alarm after he entered the An Yin Bureau, which at least proves he’s not a [Cup] or [Moth] superhuman, nor does he possess any superhuman items or sealed artifacts related to those two principles.”

“What if the Church of the Holy Communion has placed a control or curse on him…”

“Edric, whether it’s a curse or control by superhuman abilities, our lamps can detect it. Since there was no alarm, it proves his mind isn’t compromised…”

Blake interrupted Edric’s questioning, firmly stating his position.

“Now, your task is to wait. Once the owner of that house returns, the two of us will probe him.”

Blake placed his right hand on the lamp.

“As long as this light turns red, it proves the intelligence is correct.”

After a pause, Blake continued, “If an enemy appears that isn’t part of the intelligence, it means Glenner is indeed an undercover agent. I’ll kill him then. He’s still in the Security Bureau containing sealed artifacts; he can’t escape.”

Edric didn’t say anything. Since the captain had made the arrangements, he only needed to focus on completing his mission.

Just then, a carriage stopped in front of Enss’s house.

Blake, who was constantly monitoring the situation, noticed this and picked up his binoculars to observe.

“It seems like the person is back.”

By the light of the streetlamp, Blake could make out the person’s appearance.

It matched the features described in Glenner’s intelligence.

Putting away his binoculars, Blake said to Edric, “Let’s go, the target has appeared.”

Edric didn't waste any words, standing up to pick up the long, tightly wrapped object in black cloth beside him.

---

Back in his bedroom, Enns looked at the bone ring on his right index finger, his eyes filled with excitement.

Although the strength of supernatural items wasn't as great as sealed artifacts, the boost to a supernatural being's combat power was still enormous. This bone ring, for example, gave him the ability to anticipate an enemy's supernatural pathways and thus formulate countermeasures.

Although its limit was only the eighth tier, once it exceeded that, the information provided by the bone ring would become chaotic.

But it was enough for him. As long as the color and brightness of the bone ring started to change continuously, he knew it was a situation where he had to run.

He was quite confident in his escape abilities.

The [Cup]'s physique was no joke; he was completely confident he could escape from any supernatural being of the seventh tier other than [Cup].

Just as he was satisfied and was about to have a servant summon Zobert to ask if there was any new information, there was a knock on his door.

“Master, there are two unfamiliar people outside looking for you. They say they just moved in and want to meet their neighbors.”

The butler’s voice came from outside, making Enss somewhat puzzled.

Neighbors?

Didn’t I buy all the houses around here?

Enss walked out of his bedroom and instructed the butler to take them to the reception room.

He didn’t go to the reception room immediately, but went to his study, took out a revolver, and put it in his pocket.

Probably police officers. Could it be that my murder last month has been discovered?

Although somewhat surprised, Enss wasn’t concerned.

If they really were police, he could just subdue them first and then have Miria hypnotize them.

Thinking this, Enss pushed open the reception room door.

The two men inside the reception room were dressed in a sharp suit, like a successful businessman.

The other, an older man, was dressed casually.

As soon as Enss entered, their eyes locked onto him.

Edric was somewhat surprised.

Ens's appearance perfectly matched the description in Glenn's intelligence report.

High nose bridge, brown curly hair, heavy dark circles under his eyes, 183cm tall.

Moreover, only Ens, the owner of the house as described in the intelligence report, was present; no one else was there.

Could it be that Glenn's intelligence was accurate, and that he wasn't sent by the Holy Communion Church?

But how could that be?

How could someone completely ignorant of the underworld, without knowing anything, have gathered more information on their own than the entire Security Bureau?

Is our Security Bureau really that incompetent, or is the investigative ability of the "Lamp" principle just too strong?

The thought only lasted a moment.

As soon as Ens entered the reception room, the lantern carried by Blake emitted a red light.

Blake and Edric exchanged a glance.

Attack!

Without a word, the two instantly stood up and rushed towards Ens.

And Ens wasn't entirely unresponsive.

The moment he entered and looked at Edric, a faint stinging sensation shot through his right index finger. He instinctively looked down.

Eighth-tier [Blade]

The instant he saw the white light on the bone ring, he understood the situation.

Almost the instant the two stood up, Enns pulled a pistol from his pocket and fired at them.

What's going on? Why is it a superhuman?!

Could it be that my identity as a member of the Holy Communion Church has been exposed?

When?

A bullet flew out, but was easily blocked by Edric with a long, black object in his hand.

As the black cloth covering it was torn, the object's true form was revealed.

It was a long sword.

When he turned his gaze to the older man, the tactile sensation from the bone ring became strange.

It alternated between cold and hot, and the faint light it emitted became multicolored.

Level 8 or higher!

---

While the battle raged, inside the Rose Security Company, Glenner sat in a room similar to a police interrogation room. On the table in front of him was a pure white sculpture of an angel with two pairs of wings.

Although it was a sculpture, it was only about 10cm tall.

Why hasn't that mysterious person made a move yet? He seems to be losing steam…

Su Mu's attention wasn't on Glenner at all; his entire focus was on observing how the Security Bureau usually operated.



Chapter 38 The Nun


"The Security Bureau's competence is lacking."

Su Mu, having silently observed the entire process from a distance, offered a disappointed assessment.

"Looks like I'll have to handle the aftermath."

————

Security Bureau.

Outside the room, Vivian was watching Glenna's situation through a one-way glass window.

The captain's task for her was very simple: just keep an eye on him, but unless he was truly under the influence of the delirium, she couldn't let him out.

Vivian understood the captain's meaning; clearly, he still didn't fully trust Glenna.

But to be honest, it was understandable. She had just arrived at the company today and heard that Glenna had provided the specific addresses and approximate abilities of three eighth-level subordinates of the Holy Communion Church.

The sheer weight of this news made Vivian immediately think it was utter nonsense.

What a joke! He'd accomplished something the Security Bureau hadn't done in over a year since joining!

Wouldn't this make the Security Bureau look incompetent?

Especially incompetent to her!

Before recruiting Glenner, she was in charge of the entire An Yin Bureau's intelligence system, and she personally conducted most of the intelligence gathering on superhumans.

She was a [Door Seeker] at the ninth level of the [Enlightenment] principle.

While not a specialization in investigation like [Lamp], she still possessed the ability to sense spiritual fluctuations within a 500-meter radius.

However, her perception was somewhat unstable; unless the spiritual fluctuations were exceptionally strong, she couldn't actually determine whether there were superhumans among a group of people.

But at least she had this ability!

And today, this veteran of over two years in the An Yin Bureau was completely outmatched by a newcomer in her area of ​​expertise—intelligence!

She felt a shattering of her resolve.

But Captain Black's assignment of this mission—ostensibly for protection but actually for surveillance—was a subtle hint that Glenner's identity might be questionable.

Although somewhat shameful, Vivian's pride did recover slightly.

If Glenner was a spy, then the source of this intelligence would make sense.

Rather than a newcomer with absolutely no knowledge of the clandestine world surpassing the Security Bureau in understanding the Church of Holy Communion, it's more plausible that he's a spy.

Vivian's sharp gaze fell upon Glenna, who was staring intently at the angel sculpture in the room.

—

Glenna sat in his seat, enduring the effects of the incantations.

Only four hours had passed, but the incantations had already left him drenched in sweat.

The illogical, meaningless, and shrill noises in his head were causing Glenna immense suffering.

If it's this difficult now, how intense will the last hour be?

Glenna felt this sealed artifact would be more effective for interrogating prisoners.

He now felt the potions within him were also becoming restless, stirred up by the incantations.

Wasn't it supposed to protect me? Why hasn't that mysterious being made a move yet?

Or does it think that intervention isn't necessary at this stage?

That seemed reasonable enough, after all, that mysterious being was at least two levels stronger than him... But I'm not that strong!

Glenner felt that if that mysterious being didn't intervene, he might not last more than five hours.

This sealed artifact was simply beyond his ability to contain at his ninth-rank level.

He even began to suspect that the Security Bureau was setting him up.

The only things keeping him going were Vivian outside the room as a safety net, and the mysterious presence surrounding him.

However, he dared not contact Su Mu, fearing that if he bothered her, she would simply abandon him.

About half an hour later, Blake and Edric returned to the Security Bureau.

Vivian, who had been sitting somewhat bored in front of the glass, stood up and bowed upon seeing the two.

"Captain, everything seems normal."

Then she noticed that both of them were wrapped in cloth.

"Captain, what's this?"

Blake removed the cloth; his clothes were covered in blood, as were Edric's.

"You're injured? I'll go call the medical staff!"

Vivian was somewhat surprised and instinctively assumed they were injured, intending to call for medics.

Blake waved his hand.

“No need. It's mostly enemy blood. A mere [Cup] that just entered the eighth tier isn't enough to injure either of us.”

Vivian breathed a sigh of relief, then hesitated before nervously asking, “So…”

Blake nodded.

“That’s right, Glenner’s information is accurate.”

Did our Security Bureau really recruit such a powerful superhuman?!

Vivian was first pleasantly surprised, then conflicted.

Am I really a useless idiot?

Vivian didn't want to admit it, but the facts were undeniable.

She felt somewhat ashamed.

Previously, she thought Glenner’s information source was questionable, and she had mistakenly considered him a potential threat.

But now it seemed it really was just her own incompetence…

Although Glenner had previously worked in the police department's investigation department, it was reasonable for him to be strong in investigative skills.

But this strength was absurd!

Investigating ordinary people and investigating superhumans are not on the same level!

Vivian felt a sense of frustration, as if her job was being completely outclassed by someone else's hobby.

Edric, too, looked at Glenner in the interrogation room with a complex expression.

This guy really is alright; his intelligence-gathering ability is better than the entire station's...

Edric felt ashamed as well. He had been so confident that Glenner was definitely suspicious, only to be proven wrong.

"Then, what about Ens?"

Vivian rallied. Although her intelligence-gathering ability had been completely outmatched, she still had some advantages in the field of interrogation... she should have.

As soon as she finished speaking, Edric lowered his head in shame, and Blake was equally embarrassed.

"He got away. Although he's only just entered the eighth tier of [Cup], we lack the means to control him and couldn't catch him quickly. Even though we kept chasing him, he still managed to escape into the shopping district."

Vivian understood.

The captain's criterion is "Winter," meaning his abilities primarily relate to the dead, and his direct combat capabilities aren't particularly strong. While Mr. Edric adheres to the "Blade" criterion, he still struggles against enemies of the same rank, such as the "Cup," who possess high health and defense.

Most importantly, their Security Bureau cannot openly use their supernatural abilities in front of others. If the enemy escapes into a crowd, they become very vulnerable.

A large part of what makes secrecy secret is its inability to be exposed to mainstream society.

Knowledge related to the supernatural is cursed; the more one sees and learns, the easier it is to descend into madness, and most ordinary people cannot withstand this curse.

"That Ens is clever. He concealed his bone spikes when entering the crowd, clearly a threat to us."

"If you continue chasing me, I'll have to use my ability here."

Ens's threat was successful. Blake couldn't accept the consequences of the entire street going insane.

If such a horrific supernatural event occurred within his jurisdiction, he might lose his head ten times over.

"However, we can send someone to his house to see if we can find any information."

---

Meanwhile, Ens, who had barely escaped, was also in poor condition. He was currently hiding in an alleyway far from the city center.

Even though he managed to escape using hostage tactics after being pursued by two supernatural beings, he was nearly dead. He was almost killed by just two stabs from [Blade]. If it weren't for the short-term recovery ability provided by burning his bone marrow, he might not have even escaped his own home.

“Luckily, that [Blade] only looked to be at the early stage of the eighth rank…”

Ens assessed his own strength and estimated it to be around the mid-stage of the eighth rank. His mastery of his abilities was significantly better than Zobert's, and he also received more care from his mentor. All these factors combined allowed him to escape successfully today.

Where exactly did things go wrong? When was my identity as a superhuman exposed?

Ens searched through his memories three times but still couldn't figure out how the Security Bureau had come knocking on his door.

“Sir, are you injured? Do you need treatment?”

A girl with long brown hair and wearing a nun's habit approached Ens.

Ens was startled by the sudden voice, almost thinking he had been caught by the Security Bureau. Turning around, he saw a girl who looked to be only 15 or 16 years old, and immediately breathed a sigh of relief.

Then he noticed the girl's attire.

The nun's habit of the Church of the Holy Light of Salvation.

"Sir, can you still speak?"

The girl's expression was anxious, her hands flailing about, unsure of what to do.

Let's use her blood to restore her condition...



Chapter 39 Vanya


Vanya was an orphan adopted by the Church of Holy Light, where many others like her were abandoned at birth.

She was fortunate to receive not only care but also the status of a nun.

Most orphans adopted by the church are asked to leave at age 16. The church is only responsible for raising them until they turn 16, after which they must rely on themselves.

Vanya didn't see anything wrong with this. These orphans were abandoned by their parents, and the church's willingness to care for them until they were 16 was already a great act of kindness.

Children her age were gradually leaving the orphanage to find their own way, but she was too weak to find work to support herself.

She didn't have any skills; she couldn't do jobs like sewing or baking.

Because of her poor health, even factories wouldn't hire her.

Her only strength was listening to the church's prayers every day, something she remembered vividly.

She had tried working as a temporary chimney cleaner for wealthy women.

But she was overcome by the fumes as soon as she climbed into the chimney and was taken to the hospital by a carriage the family had called.

As a result, she ended up owing them money.

But she didn't blame the rich family; she knew she had done wrong.

Finally, with nowhere else to go, she stumbled back to the church entrance one night and was seen by the nun who had previously cared for her.

On the nun's recommendation and with her familiarity with church prayer, she successfully became a nun in the Church of the Holy Light.

Her daily work involved listening to others' complaints and suffering and offering them guidance.

But as time went on, she became somewhat melancholic.

The scriptures said that the Virgin Mary loves humanity and takes away all suffering, leaving only happiness on earth.

Holy light illuminates every corner, leaving no place for evil.

The Virgin Mary brings courage and strength to humanity, enabling them to overcome adversity time and again.

But reality is different.

The Virgin Mary did not take away suffering; some still succumb to illness, others succumb to despair after the loss of loved ones.

The scriptures are deceitful; God does not exist.

Vanya didn't know what she should do; she simply continued to listen to others' stories, as if by magic.

At least, they felt a little better after each story.

If only God truly existed…

She hoped for divine guidance, and the realization that God did not exist left her feeling lost.

Her sixteen years of life, though short, were almost entirely spent with the Church. She genuinely believed in the story of the Holy Trinity, believing that with sufficient devotion, happiness could be attained.

Now she discovered that reality was different; the methods she used to comfort and deceive herself were no longer effective.

She felt a sense of meaninglessness in her life. Before, when she was sad, she would comfort herself by thinking that as long as she persevered, San Sheng would eventually see her and approve of her, gradually improving her life. Now, that comforting thought no longer worked.

She even wished she could go back to the days when she worked temporary jobs for rich people; at least then she could deceive herself.

And today was her day off.

The elder sister seemed to notice her recent low spirits and gave her a day off.

She didn't know where to go.

She didn't get along with the other children in the orphanage and was always isolated; they often mocked her frail body.

With few friends, she could only wander the streets alone.

Just now, she saw Ens in the alley.

Her kind nature led her to approach, wanting to see if she could offer any help.

Then she saw Ens extend a finger towards her.

Before she could understand the meaning of Ens's gesture, a white bone spur, still tinged with blood, grew from Ens's finger, piercing straight towards her heart.

She instinctively took a step back, causing the spur to deflect, only piercing her shoulder.

But the immense strength of the superhuman used to sling her entire body to the wall with that single bone spur.

"It hurts so much!"

Vanya had never suffered such a severe injury. The pain of the bone spur piercing her shoulder contorted her face, and tears welled in her eyes.

She instinctively tried to run, but the bone spur pinned her to the wall, barely able to touch the ground on tiptoe, unable to exert any leverage.

She struggled to open one eye, staring in terror at Ens's face.

She had so many questions. What was this bone spur? Why was it attacking her? What had she done wrong?

Am I... going to die?

As this thought surfaced, a sense of relief washed over the girl.

At least, she no longer had to grapple with the meaning of life.

Just then, another set of footsteps came from the alleyway.

Ens, whose health had slightly improved thanks to the girl's blood, immediately became alert. He retracted the bone spur, stood up, and prepared to flee again.

As the bone spur retracted, Vanya fell from the wall.

But the large amount of blood drawn earlier had left her head foggy, unable to think clearly. She slumped to the ground, glancing instinctively at Ens.

Was he afraid?

Then, she strained to look at the alleyway entrance.

The footsteps were soft, but clearly audible to Ens.

They sounded light; the person approaching was small, like a child's footsteps?

Then the footsteps appeared at the alleyway entrance.

Wearing a dark gray overcoat, with a hood covering only the lower half of his face.

The overcast sky made it impossible for him to make out his features, but judging from his height and clothing, he was just a young boy.

Ens breathed a sigh of relief upon seeing the person.

Not one of those two from the Security Bureau.

He then slowly walked towards the man.

Since he'd delivered himself to his doorstep, he wouldn't stand on ceremony.

And the boy also slowly walked towards him.

Vania, her head spinning, opened her mouth, trying to urge the boy to leave.

But her weakness prevented her from uttering a word.

Watching the two draw closer, Vania felt increasingly anxious.

"Don't...come any closer..."

Her weak, hoarse voice echoed in the alleyway, where only footsteps could be heard, but no one stopped because of her words.

Ens, however, found it strange.

This child just walked over like that?

"Kid, what are you doing here?"

Ens gradually slowed his pace.

His calmness was unusually pronounced.

Could it be other superhumans from the Security Bureau?!

Ens subconsciously glanced at the bone ring.

It emitted no light, nor did it emit any tactile sensation.

Phew, was I overthinking it?

Just as the two were about 5 meters apart,

Ens suddenly realized something was wrong, and he stopped in his tracks.

Don't all human bodies possess a certain degree of [cup] spirituality?



Chapter 40 Lord God?


Something's wrong!

Without hesitation, Ens instantly sprouted bone spurs from his hand and leg bones, aiming straight for the boy.

Vanya, who had been watching closely, felt a sudden tightness in her chest.

No! Did I do something wrong? I don't want anyone else to die in front of me.

The boy, instead of retreating, charged towards the retreating Ens the moment the bone spurs appeared.

The boy easily dodged the bone spur aimed at his head with a slight tilt of his head; the remaining spurs grazed the hem of his clothes.

Such speed!

Cold sweat dripped down Ens's forehead. Undeterred, he swung his hand horizontally.

He swung the bone spur that the boy had dodged towards the boy's head, and new bone spurs sprouted from the already exposed ones.

This time, the boy didn't dodge.

He simply raised his hand and caught the bone spur in his hand.

Ens was stunned by the boy's action.

At such close range, dodging was already difficult enough, and yet he caught it?

This was an opportunity.

Although somewhat surprised, Ens's keen senses in combat remained unaffected.

The bone spurs in the boy's hand began to grow rapidly, piercing his palm directly, countless spurs emerging from the skin.

Taking advantage of this moment to drain some blood...

Ens was just about to use the boy's carelessness to weaken him when he discovered he couldn't use the bone spurs to draw his blood.

Then he saw that what flowed from the boy's palm wasn't bright red blood, but wisps of thick black mist.

Is this guy even human?

The black mist looked extremely eerie, and Ens's intuition told him to be careful.

With a decisive action, he used his other hand to break off the growing bone spurs.

Since the bone spurs grew from his own bones, forcibly breaking them also caused considerable damage to his physical condition.

He dared not stay any longer and turned to flee deeper into the alley.

At the same time, another bone spur extended from his body, aimed directly at Vanya's heart, intending to drain it completely.

"You're cursed anyway, so just give me your life."

Facing the bone spur hurtling towards her, Vanya closed her eyes, recalling her life.

It seemed there was nothing worth remembering. Ever since she discovered her faith couldn't provide clear direction, she had become lost and confused.

She needed a faith to live, but she couldn't convince herself to continue believing in a non-existent god.

As Ens turned his head, a black mist appeared in his peripheral vision.

Then he was completely enveloped by the black mist.

The expected pain didn't come. Vanya slightly opened her eyes.

She saw Ens enveloped in something black. The bone spur, having just reached her feet, snapped and fell to the ground, while the boy remained as leisurely as ever.

Two seconds later, the black mist surrounding Ens completely dissipated, leaving Ens lying on the ground covered in more wounds and bloodstains.

Vanya's mouth gaped slightly as she witnessed this.

The fact that bones could grow outside the body was already incomprehensible to her, but at the time, her attention was entirely focused on the relief of finally dying, so she hadn't thought about it at all.

Now that she was out of danger, Vanya finally had time to ponder the series of bizarre events that had just occurred.

Why can human bones grow out of the body?

Why is that boy's "blood" floating in the air?

Vanya snapped out of her daze and looked at the boy.

Because her vision was blurry, she couldn't see clearly, but in her eyes, the boy was looking at his own bleeding palm. The "blood" flowing out was strange.

It was black, and instead of dripping onto the ground, it floated into the air and disappeared in a short while.

A sudden thought appeared in Vanya's mind.

Are you my god?

The boy didn't look for long, but ran towards Vanya.

Before the wound healed, he had to save her first.

The boy was Su Mu.

His reason for being here was simple: he wanted to see how the Security Bureau made arrests.

So he had been secretly observing from a distance for a long time. Thanks to his non-human body, Su Mu's vision was ridiculously good, almost like a human-sized eight-times magnification scope.

But he seemed to have overestimated An Yin Bureau.

A mid-stage seventh-tier fighter and an early-stage eighth-tier fighter, fighting two against one, and they still managed to let him escape.

The situation inside the house wasn't clear, but once outside, they fought almost the entire way, eventually managing to get Ens to hide in the crowd.

Although a person covered in blood would be quite conspicuous in a crowd, the two from An Yin Bureau didn't choose to continue the pursuit.

Su Mu could roughly guess the reason; based on what he overheard from Glenna last night, they were probably afraid Ens would fight them to the death.

However, Su Mu wouldn't let such an opportunity pass.

Even with his completely spiritless body, he should be able to easily handle a weakened mid-stage eighth-tier fighter; after all, lacking spirit didn't mean lacking ability.

So he secretly followed. Because Alice's level up had restored his senses, he easily pinpointed Ens's location.

However, there were still some disadvantages in a fight.

First, the black mist cannot detach from the body for more than 5 seconds. Second, its maximum extension distance is only 10 meters. Furthermore, this black mist cannot be released casually when acting alone; it can only emerge from wounds.

Ultimately, it's not as convenient or easy to use as a follower.

Moreover, Ens's proficiency with the ability is indeed much higher than Zobert's. This makes Su Mu even more certain that Zobert is disliked. They advanced together in his memories, but Zobert's advancement was so fast, how come he's lagging behind?

However, his own stats are still superior.

Vanya watched as the boy walked to her side and placed his injured hand on her shoulder pierced by bone spurs. Soon, she felt less cold than before, and her vision cleared considerably, although she felt a momentary daze when the boy's hand first touched her.

She saw the boy's face under the hood.

How should she describe it?

She felt that the boy's appearance was not human.

Su Mu withdrew his hand and stood up; the wound on his hand was gradually healing.

It wasn't the platelets that stopped the bleeding; it was because he had absorbed some spiritual energy from Ens while using the black mist to create his domain. His body had absolutely no regenerative abilities; to recover, he either had to absorb energy from enemies or rely on his followers.

The black mist surrounding this body weakens her physical attributes with each point it dissipates.

As for why it can heal, well, that's how the stats are set up. Originally, it was only for piecing together severed limbs, but after understanding human anatomy, it seems it can also be used for things like blood production.

The healing isn't free. Like controlling Glenna, Su Mu hid some black mist within her body and examined her memories.

However, this time was different. Aside from one or two flashbacks, he saw almost nothing, as if Vanya had no important memories of anything.

The most unusual thing in those one or two flashbacks was the image of a nun hanging herself in an orphanage.

What Su Mu found incomprehensible was that his own face was more important in her memories than that of the nun who inexplicably committed suicide.

What does this mean? I know I'm handsome, but this…

Su Mu looked at Vanya, still kneeling on the ground, her eyes filled with piety.

Her nun's habit clearly identified her as a member of the Holy Light Church, but he saw no useful information.

Su Mu was a very cautious person.

So he decided to threaten her again.

Su Mu looked down at Vanya.

"You should know the consequences of telling anyone, right? I can kill him, and I can kill you too."

Vanya didn't show any fear, but obediently nodded.

"Yes, I understand."

Su Mu was somewhat curious about the girl's lack of fear, even her devotion and trust.

She witnessed a murder scene and had no reaction at all?

"What is your purpose in being here?"

He'd ask for information first. Although he couldn't directly access her memories, the information he passively obtained through emotional perception was completely reliable.



Chapter 41 Deceiving New Believers


Vania told Su Mu everything about herself without reservation, even recounting her entire life story, but she skipped over the matter of the nun who committed suicide.

Although he hadn't asked about her past, this information still reassured Su Mu somewhat. Vania's church either didn't have any superhumans, or Vania couldn't contact any superhumans within the church. Either way, it didn't matter much; even if she went back and told him about it, it would just be considered childish nonsense. If, by some unfortunate superhuman, someone overheard it, they would simply think it was a battle between wild superhumans, and he would still be fine.

Furthermore, there was the insurance policy within her body. Considering that even the seventh-level An Yin Bureau couldn't detect it, and that a seventh-level being could protect an entire city, this insurance policy had no chance of being exposed.

"That's how it is, Your Majesty."

Damn it!

Su Mu had been listening attentively until Vania's last sentence completely snapped him out of his daze.

When was it exposed? Does their church really have an evil god like me whose true form is black mist?

But something's not right. If his identity as an evil god was exposed, why is this nun still so devout?

"A god?"

Suppressing his surprise, Su Mu asked calmly, "Why do you think I'm a god?"

"Because you saved me, and you even defeated the demon."

"A demon?"

That Ens?

"Yes, the scriptures say that demons attack humans, and the divine being, out of compassion for all beings, saves those attacked by demons..."

Vanya then briefly explained the contents of their Church of the Holy Light's scriptures to Su Mu.

In short, Vanya believed that because she thought God didn't exist, she lost her purpose in life, and then she used her superpowers to defeat Ens, making her believe that she was a true god descended.

You know what, although the reasoning was wrong, the result was actually correct.

"Um, Your Majesty, may I ask your name? And... I hope to receive your instructions on what I should do next?"

Vanya didn't know what was right, but the God was certainly right, so she just needed to listen to the God.

Which of the Three Saints are you?

The Holy Light?

Possibly. The scriptures don't mention the gender of the Holy Light.

The Holy Mother...

Skip that.

The Holy Son?

Also possible. This one is recorded as male.

Either one is fine.

Please tell me what I should do.

Looking at Vanya's earnest eyes, Su Mu felt a little troubled, but then he thought again.

Wasn't this a good opportunity to deceive a believer?

Su Mu looked into her eyes seriously and said, "Are you sure you want to know my name? I'm not one of the Three Saints you worship."

Vanya tilted her head.

Not one of the Three Saints I worship?

Are you actually an evil god?

Vanya opened her mouth slightly, covering it with her small hand, looking somewhat incredulous.

But she quickly continued, "It's alright, please tell me."

Even if it's an evil god, it's fine, after all, you saved my life.

The scriptures say that God loves the world, so you who saved my life must be a true god.

"Then, what if I ask you to offer your soul?"

Without hesitation, Vania clasped her hands together at her chest, closed her eyes, and devoutly said, "I am willing to offer my soul for you."

The scriptures say that evil gods tempt mortals to offer their souls. Would offering her soul mean she had done something wrong?

That doesn't matter. The Three Saints have never answered my prayers, the scriptures are false, and the one before me is the true God.

I just need to obey God's will.

As the girl finished speaking, her soul appeared in her subspace, and a black ring appeared in the air behind the back of her head.

This was too easy!

New stronghold acquired.

Should I try to seduce more young girls?

No, no, that's not the right thought.

Should I say that those who haven't experienced society are naive, or should I say that religious faith makes one foolish?

Vania continued to stare at Su Mu with fervent eyes, looking every bit the fanatical believer.

Su Mu felt a chill run down his spine under that gaze.

"Alright, stop staring at me. Continue your shopping spree. I'm leaving."

Su Mu turned to leave, but Ens's body was pulled to its feet by a cloud of black mist emerging from behind Vania's head.

"Eh, wait, wait, Your Majesty, you haven't told me your name yet, and you haven't given me any instructions..."

"Instructions are to enjoy your shopping and not tell anyone what happened here."

"Then your name..."

"...Su Mu."

Su Mu initially wanted to continue using the alias "Lord of the Mist," but after thinking about it, he changed it to his real name.

After all, this name held great significance for him; it was his only connection to Earth.

He used the alias "Lord of the Mist" partly because he was too lazy to come up with a fake one, and partly to satisfy his vanity.

Now, he'd stick to his real name; he was afraid he'd forget it if he didn't use it for too long.

Moreover, once it's confirmed that the believer has offered their soul, their very existence truly belongs to him, eliminating any possibility of betrayal.

A glint flashed in Vania's eyes, a smile playing on her lips, her voice tinged with a relieved sob.

"Thank you for your instructions, Lord Su Mu. I will complete the mission well."

With that, Vania wiped away her tears, smiling as she left the alley.

Su Mu watched Vania's retreating figure, lost in thought.

"This child's mental state seems a bit unhealthy..."

Before meeting him, her emotions were like stagnant water, devoid of life.

But after regarding him as a god, her emotions shifted from fear and anxiety when she didn't receive his instructions, only returning to normal afterward.

She seems like the type who lacks independent thought and only knows how to obey orders.

From her description, she hasn't been abused?

Or is this how all religious people become after losing their faith?

"Cults are truly terrifying..."

Su Mu, completely oblivious to the fact that he was the leader of a cult, said this.

*Um...Master, are you still there? I'm starting to feel the strain...*

Glenner's voice reached Su Mu's ears through the black mist within his body.

Su Mu was startled.

"You actually contacted me? Has the sun risen in the west?"

Then he suddenly remembered what he was supposed to do today.

He had originally planned to use helping Glenna as an excuse to check on the sealed artifact…

But he woke up this morning to become a bloodthirsty spectator, completely forgetting that Glenna needed his help…

*Don't worry, I just took care of something.*

—

Inside Rose Security Company.

Glenna, whose head was almost completely filled with piercing incantations, couldn't take it anymore and tried to contact Su Mu.

He finally breathed a sigh of relief after hearing Su Mu's response.



Chapter 42 Internal Conflicts


"Speaking of which, should we continue with Mr. Glenner?"

Vivian, sitting outside the glass, couldn't help but ask, watching Glenner's face shift between pain and vacant stares.

Aside from arranging the search of the Enns family home, she had practically witnessed Glenner's transformation from a vibrant young man to a lifeless figure.

Especially after confirming Glenner wasn't an undercover agent, his plight was even more unbearable.

To suffer such a mental shock on his very first day on the job, even though he should be given a week of paid leave afterward...

Blake, who had already changed his clothes, sat at the long table, seemingly unconcerned.

"It's only been four and a half hours, let's wait a little longer."

Blake's intention in giving Glenner this containment opportunity was simply to test his mental strength and prevent him from escaping.

As a veteran, he couldn't afford to be unprepared.

However, he didn't lie to Glenna. If Glenna could truly contain it, he would give it to him; if containment failed, he would give him a few spiritual items first. While their effects wouldn't be as good as the sealed artifact, they would still be quite valuable. Normally, new recruits wouldn't be given such high-quality items.

The sealed artifact, the [Guardian Angel], did indeed prevent one fatal injury. Although exceeding its tolerance limit would still result in death, exceeding that limit required at least a full-force attack from a seventh-tier offensive method.

As it stood, it could be considered a life-saving opportunity.

He hadn't lied about the containment conditions either, but he didn't tell Glenna that he had only successfully contained it at the seventh tier. He had tried at the eighth tier, but lost consciousness in less than 10 hours and only recovered after a week…

Although it seemed like he had tricked a newcomer, Glenna didn't appear as pathetic as he had been.

So he wanted to see Glenna's limits.

However, he now had to admit that Glenna's mental strength was much stronger than he had imagined.

If it were me at level nine, I probably would have collapsed in two hours…

Should I say it's truly worthy of the [Lamp] title, or worthy of the ancestral method?

Edric, who had also changed his clothes, sat down a little distance away from the two, his longsword once again wrapped and held close to his chest.

Edric's opinion of Glenner had changed considerably.

He'd been dragged along to stake out Ens as soon as he entered the room today, only receiving a brief explanation of the situation from Blake on the way.

Therefore, he had initially disbelieved the information Glenner provided.

But it turned out he was wrong, and he now acknowledged Glenner's abilities.

Especially seeing Glenner endure this long with only level nine strength made him even more impressed.

While he was decent in direct combat, he had almost no resistance to this kind of mental damage.

However, seeing Glenner looking like he was about to die, it was clearly too much of a stretch.

Just as Edric was considering whether to also ask the captain to end the meeting, the door was violently pushed open.

"Captain! You sent a complete rookie to contain a sealed artifact?"

The voice was loud, revealing surprise, displeasure, and a hint of jealousy.

Blake turned his head in displeasure.

"Dylant, you really need to work on your temper. The others need to work; don't make a scene."

The newcomer, named Dylant, was 34 years old. He had dark circles under his eyes, looking like he hadn't slept for a week, but his demeanor didn't suggest he was simply unwell.

"It's always good to change one's personality, but Captain, a newbie getting a sealing artifact? I've worked at the An Yin Bureau for 10 years and I've never heard of such a good thing!"

Dylant was jealous.

"A newbie who just joined, only a ninth-rank rookie, gets this treatment? Is he your illegitimate son?"

Vivian and Edric could tell that Dylant was practically insanely jealous; he even dared to say such things.

But they couldn't say anything yet. Dylant, like Black, had been assigned to Dex from the An Yin Bureau headquarters in Igwent. Black was responsible for various management tasks and also shared combat duties, while Dylant mainly handled logistics and equipment supply.

And the two of them were new recruits hired locally by the An Yin Bureau a few years ago; they had absolutely no say in front of this veteran from headquarters.

Blake frowned, not wanting to waste words with Dylant, and said sternly, "The sealed artifact was entrusted to me by the organization for safekeeping. You have no right to question it before anything happens. I can give it to whomever I want."

Although Glenner's current abilities made it virtually impossible for him to contain the sealed artifact, as the captain of the Dex branch of the Security Bureau, he wouldn't be happy to be pointed at and insulted like this by his subordinate.

"This is outrageous! Who calls the shots in the Security Bureau now?"

Dylant was also enraged by Blake's merciless words.

Flames suddenly erupted around him.

The lantern Blake had placed beside his seat also lit up with a warm orange light.

*Bang!*

Blake slammed his hand on the table and stood up, a chilling aura emanating from him clashing with the heat emanating from Dylant.

Edric, sitting a little further away, felt the biting cold and couldn't help but shiver.

Vivian, sitting between Dylant and Blake, was even more miserable.

Her left side was unbearably hot, while her right side was icy cold.

The enhancement of her physique by the [Enlightenment] principle was already subtle, and being surrounded by an eighth-tier and a seventh-tier spiritual being made her feel suffocated.

Other spiritual beings weren't a problem, as they were barely noticeable except for those specializing in reconnaissance. However, Dylant's [Forging] and Blake's [Winter] were inherently elemental, and their external manifestations had a very noticeable physical effect.

"Dylant, are you planning to become an enemy of the Security Bureau?"

"..."

Dylant gritted his teeth and finally said fiercely, "Blake, you'll regret this."

"I'm the captain!"

Just as Dylant was about to slam the door and leave, something unexpected happened.

The interrogation room, which was only lit by a single lamp, suddenly emitted a bright white light, the blinding light piercing through the one-way glass and filling the room with all four people.

The first to notice was Edric, who had been staring intently at the interrogation room. He only saw the angel sculpture on the interrogation table seem to tremble a few times, and then he felt himself go blind...

He didn't even have time to pay attention to the knife that fell from his chest; he shielded his eyes with his hands, blinded by the white light, and curled up into a ball.

The captain didn't mention using this tactic during the containment of that sealed artifact!

Vivian was much more relaxed. Having endured the combined spiritual energy of the two, she hadn't looked up at all, instinctively burying her head under her arms, thus avoiding the direct beam of white light.

Even so, her vision still caught sight of the floor suddenly brighter.

What's going on?

Are the An Yin Bureau's lights this effective?

Blake reacted quickly, turning to look at the interrogation room.

His seventh-tier strength allowed him to see inside despite the blinding white light, but he still couldn't see anything.

Damn it, something's happened?!

This shouldn't be happening. Why would the [Guardian Angel] react so strongly during containment?

Neither the headquarters' records nor my own previous containment attempts have shown this!



Chapter 43 Perfect Containment


Dylant was also blinded by the sudden white light, first stunned and bewildered, then overjoyed and mocking.

"Heh, got what you deserve for carelessly handing over the sealed artifact. Let's see how you explain this to headquarters."

Blake, following his memory, immediately tried to push open the door to the interrogation room.

But then, the white light filling the room abruptly vanished.

Vivian, seeing that the two had stopped projecting their spiritual energy, finally looked up to see what had happened.

Edric was rubbing his eyes, Captain Blake was gripping the doorknob, seemingly about to push open the interrogation room door, while Dylant leaned against the door, seemingly waiting for the show to begin.

Vivian didn't know what had happened.

Seeing the white light disappear, Blake's hand also stopped on the doorknob. He leaned to the right, trying to peek through the glass at the inside.

Edric finally recovered from his dazed state and stared wide-eyed into the interrogation room, fearing that something might have happened to Glenn.

Inside the interrogation room, Glenner remained seated.

However, his mental state was completely different from before.

His previous near-madness seemed utterly nonexistent.

Glenner was now remarkably calm, even having the leisure to pick up the angel sculpture and examine it closely.

Under the watchful eyes of the three, Glenner actually tossed the angel sculpture into the air.

Vivian gaped, completely unprepared for Glenner's stunt, and instinctively stood up, intending to stop him.

If the sealed artifact were broken, that would be a death sentence!

In contrast, Edric...

Seeing that Glenner even had the energy to play around actually put him at ease; at least it proved he was alright.

He turned around, glanced at the chair he'd knocked over, and reached out to right it before sitting down again.

As for why he wasn't worried about the sealed artifact breaking down?

Because he'd almost completely forgotten the rules he was taught when he joined the company.

At his level, he had no access to the internal affairs of the Security Bureau.

His job was simply to go out and fight.
