Glenner had initially intended to leave to notify the others in the department, seeing the situation was dire and knowing he wasn't skilled in direct combat, but before he even reached the ground, he was restrained by the chirping of insects.

Of course, his mind was a complete mess, incapable of thinking clearly. Then, he blinked and saw this scene.

He quickly guessed that Su Mu had intervened, and to avoid revealing anything, he agreed.

"It's nothing, it's what I should do, Captain."

Blake smiled, "No need to be modest. Your performance is far better than mine back then. Cough, cough, let's not talk about that. Go get the logistics department to deal with that spiritual creature's corpse."

Blake coughed up a little blood and looked back into the cell.

"We might be able to extract some extraordinary items or potion ingredients from this spiritual creature's corpse."

Blake then turned to Glenner, saying, "Speaking of which, the fact that the [Guardian Angel] can directly harm spiritual creatures after perfect containment is a major discovery!"

Glenner felt a little flustered under Blake's inquisitive gaze.

"How was I supposed to know this sealed object had this effect? ​​I feel like containing it is the same as not containing it at all."

Glenner mentally scoffed, but outwardly forced a smile and explained, "I only just found out, Captain. To be honest, I don't really understand the other abilities of the [Guardian Angel] after perfect containment."

Blake felt a little disappointed, but he understood.

Unlike potions, which automatically grant abilities upon successful ingestion, sealed artifacts' powers are not automatically generated in the mind.

The abilities of sealed artifacts are derived through experimentation.

---

6 PM, still overcast, looking like it was about to rain.

Blake sat in his office, having just finished writing a message for headquarters.

The message read: A small outpost of the Church of the Holy Communion had been destroyed; Dylant was suspected of being corrupted by a potion and needed additional manpower; Glenner's resume for the "Lamp" principle; the "Guardian Angel" had the potential for perfect containment; the Dex City branch of the Church of the Holy Communion suspected of possessing a spatial portal connecting to the woodlands.

After sending the telegram, Blake picked up his coffee and took a sip.

Was today a rather eventful day?

Blake thought back carefully; since arriving in Dex City, he hadn't had to deal with so many things as today.

The only good news, besides Glenner's perfect containment, seemed to be the spiritual materials extracted from the corpse of the spiritual creature.

Although he initially lacked confidence in the logistics of his small An Yin Bureau branch, after handing it over to them, he actually managed to concoct a supernatural item using some previously distributed books on crafting such items.

【Chaotic Smoke】

It was quite simple and crude. They modified the structure of a smoke bomb, mixing powder from the bodies of spiritual creatures into the smoke.

This powder could cause mental confusion in those who came into contact with it.

The effect was mediocre; an experiment on Edric only caused him to be momentarily disoriented.

However, even a moment was enough to be fatal in combat.

Then, they extracted some materials for the 【Moth】Principle potion from the insect's corpse.

Blake leaned wearily against the back of his chair.

Next came the bad news.

Vivian delivered intelligence about the search of the Ens family that afternoon.

Nothing useful was found. No information about the Holy Communion Church was left behind. The butler couldn't be interrogated either; he seemed to have amnesia, completely forgetting Ens's usual activities.

He still carries remnants of the [Cup's] spiritual essence, most likely the ability of the person named Miria mentioned in Glenn's intelligence.

This also indirectly corroborates the veracity of Glenn's other intelligence.

So, although their Security Bureau destroyed a base, they failed to uncover the true mastermind, and the trail went cold.

The only thing they knew was that the mastermind was referred to as the Mentor.

Another suspicious point was that the old man Glenner and Edric arrested at noon appeared to be a nobleman's butler, but when they sent people to check the Dex City personnel list, they couldn't find any record of that old man.



Chapter 55 Relaxation Time


In most cases, the lack of records is actually normal. They don't even keep track of how many people live in the slums; government departments seem indifferent to the lives of the poor, and even the police turn a blind eye to reports from there.

But the old man's clothes clearly indicated he wasn't from the slums; he was obviously from a noble family. Logically, such a person shouldn't be unrecorded in government files.

Blake rubbed his temples.

Should he call this money-grubbing capitalist incompetent, or should he say he's good at hiding his true nature?

Although Blake wanted to curse the capitalist government as a bunch of incompetent fools, considering the involvement of the supernatural, and specifically [Moths]...

It's probably not fair to blame the government for being incompetent.

But the trail went cold, and the Security Bureau was back in a state of confusion.

"Sigh, we can only hope that with Glenner joining and the support of the Security Bureau, we can find more clues..."

---

Meanwhile, at West Manor, in the backyard.

Henry sat in the pavilion, sipping tea, but the table before him wasn't carved from marble; it was a person.

The person knelt before Henry, using their back as a tabletop. On their back rested porcelain plates laden with ornately presented pastries and desserts, a testament to their high status.

A gust of wind seemed to stir, causing the person's body to tremble, shifting the previously neatly arranged plates on their back.

Henry frowned and poured the half-finished tea from his hand directly over the person's head.

It wasn't because the plates were arranged to his liking; he simply wanted to do it.

Henry actually felt somewhat uncomfortable with the neatly arranged plates; the way the wind blew made them look more pleasing to the eye.

But he wanted to punish this servant.

Henry wasn't in a good mood. He'd ordered the old butler to fetch someone at noon, but the person hadn't returned yet.

This only deepened his dislike for the old man.

A lackey who only became a superhuman thanks to the West family's influence dared to criticize his actions. If it weren't for his father needing this "black glove," he would have dealt with this "black glove" first.

It's been so long since that old man got any news; could he have died out there?

Henry didn't think the old butler would be in trouble, but cursing him in his mind made him feel better.

Just then, a woman who had been kneeling beside him approached.

"Young Master, are you angry?"

The woman's eyes held worry and heartache for Henry, as if he had suffered some great injustice.

The woman didn't look at the kneeling man once, as if she hadn't seen him at all.

Henry looked at the woman's somewhat unfocused blue eyes and a smile crept onto his lips.

He was getting tired of this woman; it was time to recycle her.

As Henry thought this, Vania's image reappeared in his mind.

When will that old man finish dealing with me and bring that nun back to me...?

—

"Achoo!"

Vania sneezed and rubbed her nose.

Could it be that Lord Su Mu is thinking of me?

A sense of satisfaction welled up within Vanya at the thought, and she cupped her slightly flushed cheeks in her hands, chuckling foolishly.

"Vanya, what are you doing here? Let's go to the dining hall for lunch."

A nun in her forties greeted Vanya with a kind expression and invited her to eat with her.

Vanya snapped out of her reverie and replied with a smile, "I was just praying to God, I'm coming."

This nun was the one who had recommended Vanya to become a nun. Vanya had a very good impression of her; she had helped her when Vanya was almost starving and sometimes even treated her to foods she couldn't normally afford.

The nun kindly patted Vanya's head as she ran over.

"Vanya is truly a devout believer, but you mustn't forget to eat well. Only by becoming healthy can you please God..."

The nun's words were gentle, like those of a caring elder, but her eyes held a deeper meaning as she looked at Vanya.

—

Blake was a man of his word. After get off work, he generously took everyone from the Security Bureau out for dinner.

However, the departments responsible for various security management within the bureau didn't receive this treatment. After all, if they went out together and came back to find their homes had been burglarized, it would be disastrous. Protecting their homes was essential. The Security Bureau had been burglarized before because of this, and afterwards, the departments responsible for internal security were required to work year-round without days off.

Fortunately, it was a shift system, so there was no need to worry about them working themselves to death.

"Hey, Glenner, I heard you're a ninth-tier being, right? When did you become a superhuman? Did you really contain that [Guardian Angel]?"

The Security Bureau members were eating together in a restaurant that had been booked out when a colleague approached Glenner and asked curiously.

Glenner glanced at him, thinking for a while before remembering that it was Blake who had introduced him—the one responsible for weapons management within the Security Bureau.

"I was lucky, it's an inheritance from my ancestors. As for containment, I didn't feel any different from usual."

His colleague's eyes sharpened.

Damn it, it's Versailles.

"I really envy you, having a family inheritance. I don't know how long I'll have to grind before I can exchange for a potion."

Glenner looked at him with some confusion.

"You have to exchange for potions yourself?"

"Didn't the captain tell you? Well, with so much going on today, there might be some things I didn't have time to tell you."

His colleague briefly explained the An Yin Bureau's management of recruitment and potions to Glenner.

First, when recruiting, they choose those with stronger mental resilience than ordinary people, or directly recruit people like Glenner who are already superhumans. This ensures they won't be corrupted by contact with superhumans. Second, they consider contributions.

Whether it's killing reckless superhumans outside or managing internal affairs like him, the An Yin Bureau will record contribution points. As long as you have enough contributions, you can exchange them for potions to become a superhuman.

Of course, there are also ways to become a superhuman without making any contributions.

One is to spend more than 3 hours with a [Guardian Angel] while existing as an ordinary person; the other is to survive an attack by a superhuman without becoming contaminated.

Both points are easy to understand. If an ordinary person has such strong mental resilience, then what's there to say? Just take the path you want and have a senior member guide you through the ritual. The Security Bureau is currently short-staffed.

The second reason is similar, and it also ensures loyalty more. The first point requires a detailed background check, while the second only needs a simple investigation to ensure there are no criminal records.

After all, an organization that has a grudge against wild superhumans shares the same interests as an organization that targets wild superhumans.

After hearing this, Glenn felt even more embarrassed. He had just joined, and someone else's recruitment tool had been taken away...



Chapter 56 Another gift?!


Around 8 PM, it started raining.

Captain Black kindly gave Glenna an umbrella and helped him call a carriage home.

After finishing his meal, Glenna returned home, placed the umbrella by the door, and took off his coat and shoes.

Reflecting on the day, it had been quite fulfilling...

However, thinking about how his head was spinning from the pain of containing the sealed artifact this morning, how he was controlled by the [Moth] when he went out at noon, and how the person he captured then self-destructed and controlled him again...

Glenna felt that his initial thought was correct.

Joining the Security Bureau meant the possibility of sudden death at any time. The difference was that initially he thought it would be because of the mysterious person inside him, but the reality was that the work of the Security Bureau could kill him at any moment.

Glenna's lips twitched at this thought.

He thought having the support of the royal family would be a great job, but now it seemed like he was risking his life.

If it weren't for the mysterious person's help today, he might have died on his first day on the job...

But the most dangerous moment today also seemed to be caused by that mysterious person...

Shaking his head, Glenner decided he couldn't think like that. Logically speaking, he was bound to encounter those spiritual beings sooner or later.

Glenner felt fortunate for his pragmatism and went to the living room to rest on the sofa.

Then he saw a figure sitting on the sofa watching him.

Emotionless eyes, a face as expressionless as a corpse.

Glenner was startled, taking several steps back and drawing a pistol from his waist, pointing it at the figure.

"Who are you? What are you doing in my house?!"

Oh no, his home had been raided by other superhumans on his first day?!

*Don't panic, this is your first day's gift.*

Su Mu's voice rang in Glenner's mind.

The figure waved in unison, then pointed to the sofa, indicating for him to sit down.

?

"A gift?"

Glenner felt numb.

He holstered his pistol and sat down next to the figure.

"Sir, who is this person?"

Glenner asked Su Mu.

Taking a closer look, Glenner felt the person looked familiar, then suddenly realized—wasn't this the same deadpan face as Remo?!

And the appearance was familiar; the facial features seemed familiar.

There was also the spirit of the [Cup] on him.

*It's Ens, the one I told you about last night. Your chief couldn't catch him, so I caught him for you.*

......

Glenner surprisingly accepted this explanation.

He felt that even if Su Mu said he had captured the leader of the Holy Communion Church, he wouldn't find it so unacceptable.

"So, what do you plan to do with him, sir?"

*Take him to the Security Bureau tomorrow and say you bumped into him on your way to work.*

......

"Sir, isn't that reason a bit..."

Isn't that treating him like a fool?

Putting aside the absurdity of accidentally bumping into an escaped superhuman on the way to work, the idea that a mere "lamp" could capture someone with a "cup" sounds like pure fantasy!

"Don't worry, in his current state, even if you were only 10 years old, you could easily handle him. Besides, didn't Blake give you specially made handcuffs?"

Ens, under Su Mu's control, answered Glenn's question.

Upon hearing Su Mu's words, Glenn subconsciously touched the metal handcuffs at his waist.

These were the handcuffs the captain had instructed them to issue to personnel needed for fieldwork. He originally had a portable lamp, but since Dylant was currently in solitary confinement, and no superhuman was available to manufacture them, such high-precision supernatural tools weren't easy to make.

"Besides, you don't need to worry about being exposed. This guy has lost consciousness and won't remember what we're talking about. In his current state, he'll be dead the moment I release him."

Glenn accepted.

He had no choice but to accept. Although the mysterious man seemed easy to talk to, that was just his good temper.

Glenn knew his own limitations; daring to negotiate might mean he wouldn't see the sun rise tomorrow.

"Sir, how can I repay you for your help?"

Glenner couldn't help but ask. He was genuinely curious what benefits this mysterious expert, who could so easily cite information from the Holy Communion Church and capture a cultist who hadn't even left a seventh-tier transcendental alive, was hoping to gain from feeding him experience.

He couldn't just be doing a good deed out of pure kindness, could he?

"I initially thought that with you as a spy, my whereabouts in Dex City wouldn't be easily exposed, but after witnessing the level of the Security Bureau, I realize I was overthinking it."

Indeed.

Glenner agreed, then a new thought popped into his head.

Was this implying I was useless?

Glenner panicked a little, then heard Ens speak again: "But I've discovered that while the Security Bureau's intelligence capabilities are lacking, they seem to have a lot of potions. Save up some contribution points and exchange them for a few bottles of potions or sealing artifacts for me."

Glenner breathed a sigh of relief.

At least he still had some use and wouldn't be disposed of.

"Okay, leave it to me, I guarantee I'll complete the mission!"

"By the way, there's some new information about the Church of the Holy Communion, but not much this time."

More?!

Even if Glenner had to do it again, he still found it unbelievable.

Have you infiltrated the Church of the Holy Communion like a sieve? One new piece of information every day?

This time, the information concerns the mentor's exact address and the possibility that he possesses more than one extraordinary item.

The address was what he saw in his memories.

22 Keger Street.

The extraordinary item was speculation. Considering that the bone ring could be given as a reward, and that it was just a random explanation of a minor realm classification method from a web novel...

The extraordinary item that could be obtained through something like that probably wasn't very important. If the mentor didn't have other extraordinary items, Su Mu believed he wouldn't have given the bone ring to Ens.

Based on the mentor's personality as seen through his memories, he didn't seem like a generous person.

Su Mu had originally planned to pack up all the alcohol Remo had gotten his hands on and give it to the Security Bureau in exchange for some contribution points, but considering its rather illegal origin, he ultimately gave up on the idea.

He'd just have to stockpile it for now.

Glenner listened while taking notes on paper, internally grumbling.

Is this really not much? This is the address of what's suspected to be the leader's residence, especially for a cult like this.

Although he hadn't personally witnessed cult members, based on the historical files of the Security Bureau, they were almost all lunatics. Extracting information about high-ranking members from lower-level followers was virtually impossible.

Not to mention, some cults had specific defensive measures; anyone who dared to speak would be poisoned to death.

Furthermore, after hearing this information, Glenna felt his potion digestion process had accelerated significantly.

At this rate of one piece of information per day, he felt he'd digest it all in just a few days…

---

After finishing his business, Su Mu also planned to sleep.

Lorina initially intended to sleep with Su Mu, but after being dragged into the room and lectured for three hours by Alice, she gave up. Su Mu even noticed what looked like a bump on Lorina's head when she came out of the room…

Su Mu didn't care. He was a veteran with 30 million years of history, practically on par with Ultraman in terms of lifespan!

He treated Lorina and Alice like children.

After taking a quick shower, Su Mu lay down on the bed. Although her body wouldn't get dirty without a shower—after all, she wasn't human—a shower was still very comfortable. Su Mu quite liked to sing while showering.



Chapter 57 Omnipotence (Confirmation)


It was 8:30 PM, which, according to his previous life, would be the time for evening entertainment.

Although Edward's house didn't have electric lights—a relatively recent invention in this world—they still relied on kerosene lamps for lighting at night.

But Su Mu could see clearly without any light. The biggest problem was... this world didn't have cell phones.

Besides sleeping, Su Mu really didn't know what else to do at night.

The eyeball that had been burned seemed even more excited, constantly rubbing against Su Mu's face, making him a little itchy.

During dinner, Su Mu used the black mist behind Alice's head to scan it from his original perspective.

He discovered that the eyeball's spirituality seemed to have become stronger, meaning that this eyeball could be upgraded by killing spiritual creatures.

However, the spirituality of this eyeball was very peculiar.

It was colorless and transparent in his vision.

He vaguely remembered it.

Unlike supernatural beings who adhere to specific rules, spiritual beings exist in the An Yin Bureau's records as being without rules. These beings possess the purest form of spirituality.

The spirituality of these beings can be transformed into a specific spirituality through a particular ritual. In other words, these beings are, to some extent, a panacea.

However, Su Mu isn't in a rush right now; he'll nurture them for now.

---

March 24, 1904, 6:00 AM, Rain, 221B Baker Street

The patter of rain enveloped Dexterity. Su Mu got out of bed and first pried the eyeballs stuck to his face and placed them on his head.

Thankfully, these eyeballs didn't have mouths, otherwise Su Mu suspected he would have woken up with a face full of drool.

Su Mu walked to the window and glanced at the pedestrians with umbrellas on the street below.

Glenner had already brought Ens to the An Yin Bureau.

—

Inside the An Yin Bureau.

Glenner pushed open the door. The man sitting at the front desk smiled and waved at him.

"Good morning, Glenner. What's this...?"

After greeting him, the man looked curiously at Ens, who was following behind Glenner.

He noticed that Ens's hands were handcuffed, and he looked very weak.

Did he find an informant about the Church of Communion?

Glenner first placed his umbrella on the umbrella stand by the door and greeted him in return.

"Morning, Bronke."

After greeting him, Glenner switched back to his performance mode, his expression serious. "I found him on my way here. He's a follower of the Church of Communion."

The man blinked, his brown eyes filled with a clear bewilderment.

It took him a while to react, his eyes widening in disbelief as he stared at the weak Ens.

“Holy...Holy Communion…!”

Before the man could finish speaking, Glenner immediately raised his hand and put his index finger to his lips, signaling him to be quiet. The man hurriedly covered his mouth, not uttering the word “Holy Communion.”

Although no one from their pretentious security company would be coming at this hour, this level of vigilance was necessary.

“I’ll take him to find the captain first.”

“Ah, okay, okay!”

Brunk watched Ens obediently follow Glenner, his heart filled with surprise.

Although he was just a front desk clerk for a security company, he still knew a bit about the Church of Jesus. How could a member of the Church of Jesus be caught like that?

Wasn't he introduced as "The Lamp" when he joined the company?

—

Blake sat in his office looking at a telegram from headquarters when there was a knock on the door.

"Come in."

The door opened, and Glenner walked in, looking somewhat nervous. Blake smiled and was about to greet him when he saw Ens behind him.

Squeak.

Blake suddenly stood up, the chair sliding backward with a creak.

A chill instantly shot out, spreading from the floor and quickly freezing Ens's shoes in place.

Glenner shivered from the sudden cold and quickly explained, lest Blake accidentally freeze him too.

"Captain, calm down, let me explain!"

............

......

Blake sat back in his office chair and listened to Glenner's explanation, feeling like he might still be half asleep.

"You mean you bumped into him on your way to work?"

"Yes."

"And you arrested him all by yourself?"

"Actually, he wasn't capable of resisting much. When I saw him, he was practically dead."

Glenner tried to explain, but Blake didn't believe him. What a joke! A single [Cup] couldn't heal his injuries in a whole day? He and Edric hadn't even dealt that much damage.

But Blake couldn't think of a reasonable explanation.

It couldn't be that Ens deliberately let Glenner capture him just to send him to the Security Bureau, could it?

What for?

To sell himself to Glenner for contribution points?

Blake, pulling himself back from his reverie, said, "The situation... I understand. Take him to the underground cell; someone will handle the interrogation."

Blake dispelled the ice created by his ability, and Enns regained his mobility, but Glenner didn't get up.

Meeting Blake's questioning gaze, Glenner said, "Actually, I've already gotten some information..."

...

?

Wait, you can even interrogate people?

Then what's the point of my Security Bureau's interrogation room?

—

22 Keger Street, inside the mentor's residence.

The man sat on the living room sofa, reading a newspaper and drinking red tea, appearing quite relaxed.

But his furrowed brow and frequent glances at the door revealed his agitation.

Enns hadn't arrived yet. Normally, he would be here reporting by this time, but he was nowhere to be seen.

Could he have run away with the bone ring he'd given him yesterday?

It's unlikely. None of the three of them have any chance of returning to a normal life now. The desire for the [Cup] will only drive them towards increasing greed and extremism.

Once they leave the Church of Holy Communion, they won't have such good options.

He doesn't believe the [Cup] can suppress their desires; that would be tantamount to suicide.

Just then, there was a knock at the door.

The butler opened the door, but it wasn't Ens who entered, but another pale and thin boy.

The man's brow furrowed even deeper.

The boy was a member of his intelligence network, which typically conducted intelligence gatherings only every two weeks. Why was he coming to see him now?

The boy noticed the man sitting on the living room sofa and immediately ran to him and knelt down.

"Sir, something terrible has happened! Mr. Enns has disappeared!"

!

"Disappeared? He ran away?"

He really ran away? Has he lost his mind? Without the protection of the Holy Communion Church, does he not want to stay in Dex City?

"N-no, it seems Mr. Ens's house was attacked by the Security Bureau yesterday."

The boy recounted the information he had received from his other informants.

The mentor was even more puzzled.

When did the Security Bureau become so ruthless, immediately identifying Ens as a member of the Holy Communion Church?



Chapter 58 The Plans Against the Security Bureau


The mentor tapped his fingers on the table, his brow furrowed.

"Is there any news of Ensemble?"

"No...no, our people don't know where he went either..."

This was strange. According to their intelligence, Ensemble should have escaped successfully, but he hadn't contacted them at all yesterday?

Could he have been ambushed?

The mentor thought this was highly likely; otherwise, Ensemble wouldn't have remained silent for a whole day after being attacked by the Security Bureau.

Then who could have killed him?

The Church of the End of All Things? Or the ferryman's administrator? Or that "Academy"?

The ferryman's administrator always maintained neutrality; he wouldn't intervene—doing so would damage his own reputation.

Could it be that "Academy"?

He thought that was unlikely too. The Academy was also a strange organization, almost never interacting with other superhumans.

It could only be the Church of the End of All Things, a church that had clashed with their Holy Communion Church.

The mentor felt his thoughts instantly clear.

It must be the Church of the End of All Things in the West District. They've also noticed the location of the Security Bureau and secretly sent them some intelligence!

But how did they know so much?

The three main secret societies in Dex City have always been aware of each other's existence but not their specific locations.

This is precisely why they've maintained their current balance.

Thinking calmly, his home hadn't been attacked, so they shouldn't know his address.

Therefore, the West family, with whom he had a cooperative relationship, should be safe.

Ens and Miriam always carefully changed carriages each time they came. Given the Security Bureau's capabilities, they shouldn't be able to find his residence using this method, so he still held the initiative.

But remaining hidden was clearly not a good idea.

The Holy Communion Church had to try taking the initiative.

Although they were missing Ens, their overall strength might be somewhat inferior to the Security Bureau.

But they didn't need to worry about mortals. They could have Milia release the extra insect egg in the residential area. The appearance of a spiritual creature in the residential area was such a significant event that the An Yin Bureau would definitely mobilize its entire force.

Then, he could lead Milia and Zobert to wait at a distance, waiting for the An Yin Bureau to weaken after dealing with the spiritual creature before surrounding and annihilating them.

If they could also bring the West family along, that would be even better.

Although this might lead to the An Yin Bureau sending more people from headquarters later, dying together was better than dying alone.

Since the Church of the End of All Things wanted to harm my Holy Communion Church, I had to drag them down with me.

At worst, I could abandon this stronghold and develop elsewhere. After all, it was just a branch church; as long as I, the bishop, was safe, there was still a chance to make a comeback.

But the Church of the End of All Things was different. Their church had always been rooted here; running away wouldn't be so easy.

The instructor remained silent for a moment, then took an insect egg from his pocket and said to the kneeling boy, "Open your mouth."

The boy looked up and hesitated when he saw the insect egg held in the instructor's fingers, but under the instructor's cold gaze, he opened his mouth.

The man tossed the egg into the boy's mouth, and a nauseating stench instantly filled his mouth.

The egg had a sticky texture; he could even feel a live worm wriggling inside, its skin pressing against his tongue.

"Swallow it."

The cold words extinguished the boy's urge to vomit.

With a few gasps, the boy swallowed the egg whole.

"Cough—cough cough! Ugh!"

The boy knelt on the ground, clutching his neck, his mouth agape, trying to expel the nauseating sensation.

Then, a foot was stomped hard on his head, his nose slamming against the carpet.

"Don't ruin my carpet."

"Yes, sir!"

The boy trembled, his nose tilting slightly to the right, but he dared not cry out.

The boot ground against his dry, yellow hair before moving away.

"I won't treat you badly. Tomorrow at noon, I'll give you 100,000 yen."

The boy's trembling body froze with fear.

"M-Sir, really?"

He couldn't believe it. 100,000 yen!

If he continued living his old lifestyle, he could live a lifetime without working!

Even living the life of those adults with respectable jobs would be enough for a year of luxury!

Besides, he didn't really have any other options.

He had once killed someone for a wealthy man.

The wealthy man was displeased because he overheard someone speaking ill of him while doing charity work.

But he didn't want to get his hands dirty, so he asked him to kill someone.

He did it. He killed an ordinary textile worker. During the killing, he made a mistake and didn't notice the little girl hiding in the closet, so he was pursued by the police.

If this man hadn't paid his way out, he would be in jail now.

So, in reality, he had no other way out.

"Why would I lie to you?"

The mentor's voice remained calm, but to the boy it sounded like heavenly music.

The boy raised his hand and slapped his left cheek hard.

"I'm sorry, sir, I didn't doubt you. You're the most generous person I've ever met!"

Of course, generous. I'm most generous to the dead.

"You'll deliver a letter to Miria later. You don't need to take those little rats to gather intelligence today; you'll stay with Miria."

"Yes, sir!"

—

Inside the main mansion of West Manor.

An elderly man with gray hair and a wrinkled face sat in the main seat.

An expensive overcoat exuded aristocratic air.

To his left sat a middle-aged, overweight man.

Beside the man sat Henry, seemingly indifferent.

The man looked troubled.

"Father, the old butler hasn't returned home yet..."

Before the man could finish speaking, Henry interrupted, "Dad, I think he's definitely betrayed the family!"

The man, interrupted, glared angrily at his son.

"Shut up! The old butler is utterly loyal to the West family! How could he betray us! Back then, if it weren't for his help, how could Grandpa have escaped the An Yin Bureau's pursuit!"

"That was then. The way he acts now, he's practically behaving like he's the next head of the West family!"

The man stood up angrily, his eyes gleaming as he glared at Henry.

Henry, not to be outdone, rose and activated his spiritual power to confront his father, even showing signs of gaining the upper hand.

The old man in the main seat deepened the wrinkles on his face, his withered fingers tapping twice on the armrest of his chair.

A powerful wave of dizziness instantly enveloped the father and son.

"Shut up, both of you! All you do is argue at home. Does arguing solve anything?"

His voice was hoarse and deep, but it resonated like a booming bell in their heads.

The dizziness quickly subsided. The man felt like insects were crawling in front of his eyes; he shook his head until his vision returned. Henry fared slightly better, only feeling a slight headache.

Both were equally fearful and bowed deeply to the old man in apology.

"I'm sorry, Father..."

"Grandpa, I'm sorry..."

Seeing that the two had calmed down, the old man spoke: "Henry, go and contact the Church of Holy Communion. See if you can get them to help search."



Chapter 59 Special Extraordinary Items


“Me?”

“Yes.”

“But, Grandpa, you’ve been handling all the negotiations with the Church of Communion. I don’t know that bishop at all.”

The old man’s cloudy eyes glanced at him.

“That woman named Miria you saw last week is the bishop’s mistress.”

Henry straightened up instantly, staring wide-eyed at the old man.

“Huh?”

The man beside him also looked at the old man in astonishment, then gave his son a look that said, “You little brat, how dare you?”

No, you should be the father…

“Don’t even think about it. The Church of Communion won’t care about this kind of thing. Go find Miria and ask her to help us ask.”

“Ah, okay…”

Henry wanted to refuse, but faced with the old man’s unwavering gaze, he could only agree.

—

Miri’s home.

Miri sat back on the sofa, stepping on the boy’s head with her foot. The boy remained kneeling, his humiliation lessened.

"If I finish today's work, I'll have 100,000. For that, no hardship is too much."

Outside the window, the sky was overcast and raining, making the room somewhat gloomy. Miria lit a candle and began reading the letters by its light.

Besides the unimportant matter of Ens's suspected murder, there was another task from her mentor.

"Tonight at 8 PM, at 221B Baker Street, have that boy find someone who says my name. Make sure you're at the designated location in the east before he does. The Security Bureau will definitely send people. Wait 30 minutes before approaching. By then, the Security Bureau personnel will be exhausted. Use your abilities to distract them. You don't need to fight; Zobert and I will handle the direct combat.

There's an item in the envelope that can disrupt spiritual detection. Take it.

Also, you're getting quite close to that young master from the West family. Try to get him to join this operation."

After reading the letter, Miria placed it on the burning candle.

She then took out the extraordinary artifact her mentor had mentioned from the envelope.

It was a piece of human skin. A horizontal line was etched on the white side, and directly below the line was a black cross.

Miriah stared at the design for a while, then suddenly felt confused.

What was I supposed to do? Why am I staring at my hand?

She suddenly snapped out of it and shook her head.

This extraordinary artifact actually made her subconsciously ignore its existence!

Miriah found it incredibly strange, having never encountered such an artifact before.

She turned the skin over to look at the back.

The back was bright red flesh, seemingly still fresh, with blood flowing.

But when Miria tried to rub some blood on her hand, she found that she couldn't pick up any blood with her fingers.

The texture was still that of viscous blood and elastic muscle.

She could even feel the muscles contracting when she touched them, as if they were still alive.

Miria didn't feel disgusted; instead, she found it quite novel, constantly rubbing the back of her hand with her fingers, as if she were directly playing with someone else's flesh.

This realization excited Miria.

While Miria was engrossed in playing with her supernatural gadgets, the boy peeked up to see what she was doing.

Then he saw the human skin in her hand.

He felt as if he heard the screams of countless souls, and something seemed to rise in his throat.

*Slap!*

Miria suddenly slapped him across the face, bringing him back to his senses.

The boy was about to apologize when he noticed something strange about his tongue.

He saw his tongue dancing in front of his eyes.

A long, thin strip…

“Ah, ah!!!”

Miria felt a headache coming on.

“Idiot, who told you to look up!”

She was also getting impatient seeing the boy's mutated tongue after being corrupted.

She reached out and grabbed the still-dancing tongue, twisting it off with all her might!

“Ahhh!!”

The intense pain made the boy scream.

“Shut up.”

After saying that, Miria used her fingernail to cut a gash in her palm and dripped her blood onto the severed tongue.

The tongue, which had been hissing and bleeding, healed in no time.

However, it was still clear that the tongue had been roughly damaged.

The other half of the long, thin tongue was still writhing on the ground.

*Plop.*

Miria stomped on the severed tongue.

Only then did she breathe a sigh of relief.

She had been so engrossed in playing that she had forgotten about this mortal, almost corrupting him into a spiritual being. Luckily, she reacted quickly and interrupted his gaze.

Putting the human skin back into the envelope, Miria stared coldly at the boy.

The boy trembled with pain, but immediately knelt down to apologize.

“I…I’m sorry! Lady Miria! I…I really didn’t mean to.”

His pronunciation wasn’t as clear as before; it sounded muffled, clearly indicating he wasn’t used to his new tongue.

Miria desperately wanted to kill him. Even if he retained some cognitive abilities after being corrupted, he wouldn't live long. Even without contact with the supernatural, the memories alone over the next month might worsen the corruption.

But she couldn't kill him now; her mentor needed him in her plans.

She placed her foot back on the boy's head, this time applying more force, and wiped the blood from her feet onto his dry, yellow hair.

But her mentor actually told this kid his name?

Looking at the kneeling boy, Miria became curious again. She'd been with her mentor for so long, and he'd never mentioned his name.

However, since her mentor hadn't said it, she wouldn't pry. Miria was only slightly surprised that her mentor knew about her affair with the young master of the West family.

Although she hadn't expected her mentor to have people monitoring them, it made sense on second thought; they were a cult.

Thinking of this, Miria suddenly remembered a book her mentor had shown her when she first joined the cult.

Speaking of which, she wondered when she'd get to see that secret manual her mentor had shown her again.

Thinking of the secret technique, an unnatural, infatuated look appeared in Miria's eyes.

Knock, knock, knock

A knock sounded on the wooden door, pulling Miria from her reverie.

Who would be looking for her at this hour?

Removing her foot from the boy's head, Miria walked towards the door, but stopped halfway.

Wait, could it be someone from the Security Bureau?

Miria thought it was very likely. Her mentor's letter stated that Ens had been ambushed by the Security Bureau at home that morning, and it was morning now, which was also the case for her.

She immediately became alert, turned around, kicked the boy's leg, and whispered, "Go wait at that window over there. If anything happens, just jump out."

After all, this was a crucial part of her mentor's plan. If he died at her hands, she'd probably be crippled for a month.



Chapter 60 Actions against the Church of Holy Communion


The boy scrambled to his feet and looked in the direction Milia was pointing.

He's just a rat from the slums after all; the mere mention of death makes him run faster than anyone else.

Could they have run into that bishop from the Holy Communion Church?

Although his grandfather said the bishop wouldn't care about such things, he was still a little panicked if they were discovered.

"Ahem, of course I'm here to catch up with the beautiful Miss Miria."

Miria's words immediately froze Henry on the spot.

His target was exposed! How did she know?!

Chapter 61 A Two-Way Game and a Peaceful Life

Actually, Su Mu guessed.

As for his father? He could just refine him into a magic potion. His strength was entirely bestowed by the great Lady Sinessa; that kind of extraordinary ability gained through magic potions was incomparable to that of a born extraordinary being like himself!

Henry was a genius born with extraordinary abilities, yet he had to be overshadowed by a mortal who gained extraordinary abilities later in life. For Henry, this was a disgrace!

Just half an hour later, he would reap a huge harvest.

7:36 AM, 12 Walter Street.

A group of people dressed in black surrounded the target house.
