Would someone like the Lord of the Mist be kept as a mistress...? It seems the Lord of the Mist is more interested in superhumans. So, if I become stronger, can I use superhumans to keep the Lord of the Mist as a mistress...? No, I can't think about such disrespectful things anymore!

Although she warned herself not to think about such nonsense, Alice couldn't help but drift into daydreams.

Su Mu glanced at Alice.

What was this child grinning about now?

Su Mu could see that Alice's soul was in a state of mixed excitement and guilt.

But he couldn't understand why Alice always suddenly fell into this state.

Direct soul searching… communicating with the soul could yield answers, but Su Mu still needed to respect the privacy of his followers. As long as no problems arose, it was fine.

Su Mu withdrew his gaze and looked at another person whose emotions were also complex.

Vivian's hand, which had been completely useless, had been restored, but her fingers were still somewhat unaccustomed to it, twitching occasionally.

She kept glancing at Su Mu while reporting.

Su Mu could sense the complex emotions mixed with gratitude in her eyes, but he didn't think much of it. After all, he was the one who had helped her reattach her hand; such emotions were normal.

At the time, he felt he should give the An Yin Bureau a return gift for all the things he had taken from them.

Seeing Vivian's severed hand and the half-dead Berlaut, he treated them both, and also gave Edric, who was lying in the study on the second floor, a little healing.

Vivian's hand required a bit of precision to reattach limbs; it was her first time using her spiritual power, and she was still a little clumsy. However, the finished product was almost indistinguishable from the original; it just took some getting used to.

Berlaut was simpler; she just needed to analyze his blood type and then give him a transfusion.

Edric was the most seriously injured. If Su Mu hadn't used the black mist to possess and heal him later, he might have truly died.

After possessing him, he glanced at the battle scene involving the four.

It can only be said that the extraordinary item played a crucial role. Without the spiritual power replenishment from the cane, Bruce would probably have been worn down by the three from the An Yin Bureau.

Su Mu was not only the highest damage dealer but also the highest healer in the entire battle.

He was too well-rounded.

He had crowd control, healing, and damage output.

And the healing was almost costless.

The small amount of spiritual power he used to save the others was replenished in just a few minutes.

Using his renewable resources to gain a favor with the An Yin Bureau seemed like a sure win.

Although it's terrible in every way, the Security Bureau is still an official organization of this empire, so it wouldn't hurt to cultivate goodwill.

On the sofa to Su Mu's right, Blake sat upright, looking extremely reserved.

After witnessing Su Mu's displayed strength several times, he didn't dare to breathe too loudly.

Not only was he a high-level [Blade] principle transcendent, but he also seemed to possess a healing transcendent item and a sealing artifact that could directly convert a person into a spirit.

This strength, this resource.

Is Dex really just a small city?

It feels like this place is full of hidden talents!

And this young man has a secret society behind him, which is even more terrifying. A secret society with the strength to easily kill a seventh-tier transcendent... If a fight breaks out, unless the headquarters intervenes, this secret society in Dex City could probably roam freely...

Su Mu was also somewhat puzzled after hearing this.

How did these three fingers end up back in my hand again?

Su Mu was a little disgusted.

He had no use for the fingers; their current level of spirituality was negligible. Making potions with them seemed less worthwhile than robbing other secret societies or the Security Bureau.

Keeping them as a collection… he didn't have that kind of hobby.

Hey!

Su Mu suddenly thought of a use.

"I'll just take them to that gathering next week and sell them again!"

"Pack them all up for me."

"Okay, no problem."

Vivian replied and went back upstairs.

Su Mu stared at the ceiling, deep in thought.

Could he give Vania the [Forging] potion, or perhaps the [Cup] potion? Her body was so weak; becoming a [Cup] superhuman should improve her physique considerably.

Thinking this, Su Mu recalled several [Cup] fighting styles.

Forget it, [Cup] fighters always injure themselves first; letting Vania fight would probably kill her with pain.

Besides, their abilities are really unattractive; they're nowhere near as handsome as Blake's superhuman abilities.

Su Mu turned his gaze to Blake, who instantly sat bolt upright, cold sweat beading on his forehead.



Chapter 80 Post-War Cleanup


Two hours later, Su Mu left carrying two large bags, followed by Alice carrying three sheathed longswords.

One bag contained heavy banknotes, and the other bag held potions and secret manuals.

Blake watched Su Mu and Alice leave from Bruce's house.

Su Mu didn't go directly to the Security Bureau to choose; he knew what they had anyway, so he had Blake send someone to bring him what he wanted.

Blake had no objections whatsoever; in fact, it suited him perfectly.

Although the boy was very agreeable, if his base were exposed, he would definitely have to change locations, which would be troublesome and might even anger the boy.

Blake was overjoyed that the boy had proposed this win-win solution.

Blake watched the boy and girl disappear at the end of the street, his tense nerves finally relaxing.

"Phew, finally over."

He wiped the cold sweat from his forehead and went back inside.

“Captain, we’ve collected all the intelligence except for the supplies. There are quite a few clues about other cities’ Holy Communion churches.”

An An Yin Bureau member approached carrying a stack of letters and several books. His colleague picked up a book, opened it, and pointed to the words circled in various sizes.

“It seems the bishops of the Holy Communion church use this method to communicate by spelling words.”

Blake waved his hand weakly, saying, “Take it all back to the station. We’ll organize it and send it to headquarters later.”

Upstairs, Edric came down, rubbing his head.

His face still bore the burn mark from the grazing blood, and his clothes were long gone; he was now wearing Glenner’s coat.

Under the coat, bullet-hole-like burns were visible.

Su Mu had originally intended to remove them all, but Blake stopped him.

It was better to leave these marks. Although he didn’t know the principle behind the boy’s treatment, it would be troublesome if the enhancements Edric had accumulated for so long were also removed.

"Awake? How are you feeling? Any discomfort?"

Blake asked with concern upon seeing Edric.

"No, Captain, I actually feel lighter. To be honest, when I opened my eyes and saw I was still here, I thought I had gloriously died in the line of duty."

"We almost all died. Luckily, someone from a secret society intervened."

Edric knew what Blake was talking about. As soon as he opened his eyes on the second floor, someone gave him a preliminary check-up and told him what had happened while he was unconscious.

Frankly, when he first heard it, Edric felt like he was listening to a story.

What did it mean that a [Blade] superhuman suddenly appeared and instantly killed Bruce of the Holy Communion Church in two moves?

And he even carried a healing-type sealed artifact that pulled him back from the underworld?

After taking Bruce's Boiling Blood head-on, he didn't think he could survive. He might not even be able to withstand a single attack of that intensity.

But as his teammates vividly described how the boy had severed Bruce's arm in one fell swoop and survived a Boiling Blood attack at point-blank range unscathed, he felt a sense of unease.

Are high-level [Blades] really that powerful?

Moreover, according to the other team members, the boy didn't have any scars, so that was only the minimum level of strength they could achieve!

Edric felt the injustice of the world.

Meanwhile, Berlaut, who had been nearly drained by Bruce, sat on a small sofa, his head propped up by his hands, deep in thought. His broken sword lay on the table in front of him.

Damn it, all the weapons I brought were taken. I came fully armed and left empty-handed…

Ammunition and such are consumables; once they're gone, they're gone. But I didn't even save my sword.

In fact, his sword wasn't even taken by that secret society; it simply melted down after the battle couldn't withstand the intensity…

If it had been taken, there might have been a chance to get it back later, but the fact is, his sword was melted and broken.

That was his sword, which he'd had for three years!

He'd used it since he joined the company, and now it was just broken…

But he couldn't blame anyone else. He couldn't blame Vivian for going up there and offering herself up, could he?

He wasn't that heartless. Although, according to others' accounts, Vivian hadn't actually played a significant role, this chivalrous spirit was highly valued by the Prit Empire. If he dared to use it to make things difficult, he'd be finished.

Besides, ultimately, it was because he was too weak that the others had to rush in. If he were strong enough, like that mysterious boy who followed the "Blade" principle, this wouldn't have happened.

Black patted Berlaut on the shoulder.

"I'll talk to your captain about the weapon. He won't punish you."

Berlaut shook his head but didn't say anything. He was still immersed in the grief of the broken sword and the regret of his own weakness.

Vivian, responsible for gathering information, was in the best condition among them.

However, she was always absent-minded when collecting clues.

Right now, her mind was filled with the image of that boy in black.

So strong, a boy who looked younger than her was so strong?

———

Meanwhile, Su Mu and Alice returned home.

As soon as Su Mu entered, he tossed the bag of money aside and grabbed the bag containing potions and secret techniques, heading towards his room.

The eyeball in the living room, still engrossed in studying its multiplication table by candlelight, sped up and buried itself in Su Mu's hair upon seeing him return.

Making a sentient being memorize math was simply too torturous.

It was a sentient being, after all; was it reasonable to make it learn math?

Lorena, on the other hand, fared much better. Upon seeing the two return, she ran to the door.

"Lord of the Mist, I've memorized it all!"

Su Mu, however, was completely focused on the potions and secret techniques. Seeing Lorena's expectant look, he simply patted her head.

"Then I'll recite it to your sister later."

He then returned to his room. As for the eyeball on his head, he decided to leave it there, since he was in a good mood.

Lorina, seeing Su Mu merely pat her head before returning to the house, especially with that eyeball still pressed against her, puffed up her cheeks in anger.

It's not fair that he was allowed to follow the Lord of the Mist into the house when he hadn't even memorized the eyeball!

Alice, seeing her sister's unhappy expression, could do nothing but pat her head.

"Alright, don't be angry anymore. Come on, recite it for me."

Inside the room, Su Mu took out three secret manuals from his bag. Even in the pitch-black room, he could clearly see the covers.

*The Deconstruction of the Sacred Body*, *The Skull Song*, *Orchid Metamorphosis, Volume One: The Feast*

Su Mu first opened *Orchid Metamorphosis*, but after only two pages, he slammed the book shut.

Then he opened it again, glanced at it, and then slammed it shut again.

Dude, why aren't the little figures in your illustrations wearing clothes?



Chapter 81 Preparing to confiscate property


Is this...right?

Why is this secret manual written like a pamphlet?

Su Mu suppressed his strange emotions and tried to quickly flip through the entire book with a calm mind.

Good news, the later parts aren't a pamphlet.

Bad news, the later parts are a collection of bizarre stories...and he even thoughtfully used a very realistic art style to illustrate the specific scenes described in the text, just in case people couldn't understand.

Holy crap, what does it mean when a group of naked people are in a circle, eating the person in front while being eaten by the person behind?!

Su Mu felt like he had been mentally polluted.

His brain felt like it had been forcibly conjured up by knowledge.

Damn it, what kind of crap are these cults reading?!

Although he felt like his brain had been attacked, it wasn't entirely without benefits.

After reading the contents of the book, Su Mu actually gained another ball of [Cup] spiritual energy!

Although after merging, it was still only at the late stage of the seventh rank, the quantity had increased.

This gave Su Mu a new inspiration: knowledge also possesses spirituality. Could he accumulate spirituality by continuously reading the secret manual?

Su Mu flipped through the manual again.

Unfortunately, repeated reading wouldn't gain more spirituality, but it was still enough.

The manual contained annotations by Bruce, summarizing its contents on the last page, revealing the ingredients, ritual, and extraordinary abilities for the eighth-tier [Cup] principle [Heartbreaker].

Based on the description, Miriam should be this one.

The ability is to plant a seed in the heart of the listener by infusing spirituality into one's words.

The more the other person cares about you, the more subtly your subconscious will be altered.

Furthermore, the speed of spirituality recovery can be increased through the attention of others.

It seemed like a slow-start type, but unfortunately, he kicked it to death in one go, failing to activate it successfully.

Su Mu flipped through the other two books.

It also records the corresponding rituals for the other two at the ninth and eighth ranks.

*The Sacred Body Deconstruction Technique* is a tutorial on dissecting the human body, with incredibly realistic illustrations showing all the organs. It records the advancement ritual for the [Blood Cloner].

*The Skull Song* is a song praising an avenger who was killed and resurrected as a skeleton.

It records the ritual for the eighth-rank [Bone Forger], but it's not the only ritual; there's another one that can resurrect the dead.

First, you need a dead human corpse and a seed imbued with the spirit of a [Moth].

Then, the seed is placed inside the skull, and the corpse is dissected. The internal organs must be precisely removed before the skin is stitched back up.

Then the corpse can "come alive" again as a walking corpse, obeying the commands of the person performing the ritual.

However, this isn't important; Su Mu doesn't need it.

What's important is that it gave Su Mu a small amount of the spirit of the [Cup], the [Moth], and a little bit of the spirit of [Winter].

However, because Su Mu currently possesses a massive amount of spiritual energy, neither the [Cup] nor the [Moth] can achieve a breakthrough at the minor realms he has set for himself.

They are currently at the late seventh-tier [Cup] and mid-eighth-tier [Moth] levels.

The [Winter] content is very low, only about half that of the ninth-tier.

But it can still be considered an improvement.

Su Mu felt somewhat unsatisfied after finishing the book.

Not because the content was particularly exciting, but because the knowledge it provided offered a sense of enlightenment.

Furthermore, the three potions from the Holy Communion Church were all ninth-tier [Blood Coagulator] potions, and Su Mu still couldn't figure out how to use them.

Reading this kind of erotic literature late at night was quite invigorating, and Su Mu was now having trouble sleeping.

Then Su Mu thought of someone.

Henry West.

That noble young master.

He had originally planned to hand over a message to the Security Bureau tomorrow, but given the Security Bureau's current state, he felt that going to fight again might kill him.

Besides, since he couldn't sleep now, why not go to his house for a visit?

Maybe he could even snag another secret manual.

—

Inside the West family's manor, located away from the city center.

In the main house, an elderly man with a wrinkled face sat in the main seat, a kerosene lamp lit by a servant beside him.

The man beside him, Henry's father, sat in his seat, the kerosene lamp illuminating his agitated profile.

"Father, that boy hasn't returned for so long, could it be..."

The old man glared at him.

"He's Lady Seneza's child, he won't be so easily harmed."

"I know, but he's only at the eighth rank now, what if the Holy Communion Church...?"

"We still have a partnership with the Holy Communion Church, they wouldn't dare to attack us."

The man was still anxious; they had been waiting all day and Henry hadn't returned.

Although he felt no affection for this "son," and even harbored some resentment...

He was Lady Seneza's chosen son, and if something happened to him, Lady Seneza might unleash divine punishment, a burden the West family couldn't bear.

The old man, the current head of the family, also had these concerns.

He pondered for a moment and finally spoke, "Go see if you can find him at Miria's residence."

After receiving the old man's permission, the man immediately got up and went outside.

The old man in the main house also felt a sense of anxiety.

The West family was merely a minor noble family, incomparable to true nobles or orthodox supernatural organizations.

At over 80 years old, he was only barely at the seventh rank, and his potion digestion was extremely slow; after all these years, he had only digested about one-tenth of it.

He was troubled daily by the potion's incantations.

When he first met the bishop of the Holy Communion Church, he thought he was going to die.

After all, he hadn't even had time to react before that person had gotten close.

[Moth] wasn't good at direct combat, and as an old man, he was even less adept.

Fortunately, Lady Seneza had bestowed a blessing, leaving a gate to the woods in the manor, successfully deterring Bruce and preventing him from easily attacking him.

The two sides remained in a stalemate.

Because of this incident, he dared not leave the manor even for a moment.

Within the manor, with the protection of Seneza, he could still contend with the top fighters of several secret societies in the city. Without that protection, he might not even know how he died.

This was also why he sent his nominal grandson to negotiate.

As long as he didn't leave, he would still hold a certain deterrent effect on the Church of the Holy Communion.

After all, he controlled a gateway to the woodlands.

If they're determined to oppose him, then he must be prepared for a relentless onslaught of spiritual beings.

Through several exchanges of letters, he also learned the bishop's whereabouts.

If a real fight breaks out, no one will have an easy time.

This is why he dared to send his grandson.

Moreover, Henry is essentially still a spiritual being.

He grew from an egg laid by Seneza that devoured a human.



Chapter 82 Blocking the Door


The man left and went straight to the stables, instructing the coachman to prepare the carriage for departure.

But he encountered trouble as soon as he left the manor.

The carriage stopped, and the man frowned, looking through the window at the road ahead.

The surroundings were overgrown with weeds and trees, and a young boy blocked the way. In the dim light, only the kerosene lamp hanging from the carriage made it impossible to see his face clearly; only his black hair was vaguely discernible.

The man, preoccupied with finding Henry, didn't notice him at all, and impatiently said, "Where did this lowly commoner come from? Get him out of here."

"Yes, sir."

The coachman respectfully alighted, holding the kerosene lamp in one hand and a wooden cane in the other, pointing it at the boy.

"You, what are you standing there for? Get out of here! Do you know who's in the carriage? That's the West family..."

The coachman approached, waving his cane, intending to shoo the boy away.

But when he got closer and saw the boy's face in the lamplight, he paused, stunned.

It sounded strange, but he even felt his heart stop.

Then he saw the boy bite his thumb, then walk up to him.

Before he could react, the boy placed his hand on his head, and he lost consciousness.

After quickly reviewing the boy's memories and finding he was just a laborer, Su Mu lost interest.

After erasing the boy's memories of himself, Su Mu released the driver, letting him lie down on the ground.

The man in the carriage also noticed this unusual scene. His previously scattered attention sharpened, and he watched Su Mu warily.

Su Mu made no attempt to hide from him, walking directly towards him, his left hand in his coat pocket, a few drops of black mist emanating from his right thumb.

He had come alone this time, and as he approached West Manor, he encountered a carriage emerging from within.

Alice wasn't brought along because it was almost 10 PM, and making her work overtime would be inhumane. Besides, judging from Henry's memories, this West Manor truly was protected by an evil god.

Su Mu wasn't entirely comfortable having Alice come; it was safer to come himself.

Although this body was relatively weak, its advantage was its fearlessness.

Now, with the regenerative [Cup] spirit, he could do whatever he wanted with this body; he wouldn't care if he died.

However, Su Mu wasn't entirely alone; he had brought his eyeball with him before leaving.

The eyeball was still rubbing against his hand in his coat pocket.

He brought the eyeball because West Manor likely had many spiritual beings; having one with a restraining effect would make things easier. After all, the black mist in this body couldn't replenish itself, and the eyeball could teleport, so there was no fear of trouble.

The kerosene lamp on the carriage illuminated Su Mu's profile.

The man only now noticed the strangeness about the boy.

Why is it black hair?

Are there black-haired Plits?

Then, upon seeing the boy's illuminated profile, his breath caught in his throat.

A greedy thought, mixed with a destructive urge to possess him, surged into his mind, and the magic potion within him stirred restlessly.

Just as the man's thought crossed his mind, Su Mu stopped.

Su Mu stopped about two meters away from the side of the carriage.

He felt that none of these cult members were normal.

The moment he approached, he sensed the man's repulsive emotions.

No, what does it mean?

Just getting close made Su Mu uncomfortable; that sticky feeling, even without a physical form, made him want to vomit.

Unlike the pure intimacy and dependence he felt with Alice, the man's emotions were mixed with things that couldn't be tolerated.

"Damn, you exude a lewd aura from head to toe."

Su Mu felt that Alice was right to buy him those hooded clothes; he shouldn't have chosen not to wear them for comfort.

He silently took two steps back.

Sometimes being passive isn't all good.

The man, at this moment, also came to his senses, his eyes greedily scanning Su Mu.

He completely ignored the fact that the coachman had suddenly collapsed.

A mortal, simply taken down by another mortal.

That's what he thought. He'd been preoccupied with Henry's situation and hadn't noticed how the boy had been knocked down by the coachman. Now, his attention was focused on the boy's appearance, so he hadn't thought about it further.

But his gaze suddenly changed.

His eyes fell on Su Mu's fingers; what was flowing wasn't blood…

This boy was a superhuman!

The man quickly opened the car door, pulling back about 20 meters before stopping.

Su Mu breathed a sigh of relief after the distance was created; at least Su Mu's emotions were mostly normal vigilance now.

He no longer had to suppress that lecherous urge to fight him.

He raised his hand, channeling the spirituality of the [Cup] into the black mist flowing from it and converting it into blood. Then, he pointed his thumb and forefinger at the man.

Su Mu didn't choose a more advantageous, dark place to gather his strength; instead, he openly gathered it under the light of the kerosene lamp.

And the man, in the dark, could see it clearly.

The ability to manipulate blood?

Is it the [Cup]? Could it be someone sent by the Holy Communion Church?

Why?

Is the Holy Communion Church planning to completely oppose our West family?

How dare they?

Looking at the blood bead poised to strike in Su Mu's hand, the man shouted, "I'm on the left!"

An ordinary person shouting like that would be considered a fool, but the man was a transcendent being, an eighth-tier [Distorted One].

Unlike the old butler, whose eighth-tier advancement was largely due to the blessings bestowed by the family head and Lady Sinessa, he advanced through his own abilities.

That last sentence was also one of his transcendent abilities.

His words could create cognitive distortion.

Unless one possessed a transcendent item that could resist mental attacks or their transcendent ability itself had mental resistance, it was impossible to defend against it.

Although he was only eighth-tier, this move gave him some fighting power even against a seventh-tier opponent.

Furthermore, he had some understanding of the Church of the Holy Communion.

The Church of the Holy Communion was basically composed entirely of [Cups] who only knew how to enhance their power. Against a [Moth] like him who fought with his brains, if they couldn't defeat him at the outset, the [Cups] wouldn't have another chance to harm him after his ability activated.

Although he wondered why this boy from the Church of the Holy Communion hadn't attacked earlier but had stopped and waited for him to run away before attacking,

he wouldn't let this opportunity slip by.

After saying that, the man was about to take advantage of the boy's confusion to say a few more words and expand his advantage.

Then he noticed that the boy's hand hadn't moved at all.

He met Su Mu's gaze in surprise.

He found that Su Mu's eyes showed no panic after being attacked, but rather an unchanging calm.

This guy really has a supernatural item that can resist mental attacks?!

Considering this possibility, the man tried to lunge to the side to avoid the blood droplet poised to be released from the boy's finger.



Chapter 83 A Cult Nest Full of Insects


"Wait! Our family hasn't shown any disrespect to your Holy Communion Church, has it? My father even gave your bishop a gift yesterday!"

The man asked this as he lunged to the side.

Then, while still in mid-air, a drop of blood pierced his neck, and he collapsed to the ground.

The man clutched his neck, looking at the boy in horror, his hands stained with blood.

After confirming the hit, Su Mu slowly walked over.

"What bishop stuff? I'm not from the Holy Communion Church. I even waited a while to see what your ability was, but you're really good at holding back, not using it even now."

"Also, do you think I'm blind? Here's your left side again."

Su Mu was utterly confused by the man's cryptic words.

It was dark, but he wasn't blind. Besides, the other man had directly revealed his location; even without X-ray vision, it was impossible to miss.

And the man's cognitive distortion ability was so weak that Su Mu hadn't even sensed it.

He thought the man was rambling nonsense.

"Cough, cough, you..."

The boy approached the man with a mocking expression, seemingly about to say something, when more blood spurted from his neck.

What? He didn't use his abilities? He used them!

And they were completely useless... Wait, could this guy not have brought any supernatural items at all, just relying on his mental strength to tough it out?

The man realized what was happening, and looking at Su Mu's disappointed expression, the more he thought about it, the more likely it seemed.

Although it was hard to accept, the boy genuinely seemed unaware of his abilities and even intended to test his skills against him.

Moreover, supernatural items related to mental strength were extremely rare, and this boy claimed he wasn't from the Holy Communion Church, so it was even less likely he would possess such a thing.

But this result was even harder for him to accept.

His strongest control, an ability that could affect even a seventh-tier opponent, and this boy had taken it all without a scratch? He hadn't even noticed.

This was utterly humiliating!

Su Mu stood before the man, looking down at him.

"How boring. I think I'll forget about you by tomorrow."

Su Mu, watching the man lying motionless on the ground after his blood bead pierced his neck, began his victory speech.

As for why he suddenly said that, he simply found it amusing.

Now he completely understood why villains in movies and TV shows always loved to mock others.

It was indeed fun, especially seeing his opponent looking like he wanted to fight but couldn't.

And every time this happened, a bunch of famous quotes would pop into his head, and he felt it would be a waste not to say them.

He crouched down and placed his hand on the man's head.

The man's eyes, which had been staring at Su Mu, gradually lost focus.

......

After reading the memories, Su Mu felt his worldview had been refreshed once again.

Originally, he thought of Henry as a superhuman who had been turned into a spiritual being by the backlash of the potion.

But after reading his father's memories, he realized that the guy wasn't human at all!

Henry's birth was entirely due to the West family's birth-related actions.

They actually threw their infant children into the woods to be fed to spirit creatures, and fearing there wouldn't be enough sacrifices, they even threw the mothers in with them!

Henry emerged from the woods a few days after the West family's last child sacrifice, when the West family reopened the gate to the woods.

Besides these, the most useful thing is that the West family possesses secret techniques and potions; a successful raid would definitely yield profits.

And the evil god who resembles the "Moth" principle is named Seneza, and appears to be a giant spider.

————

Bang!

The gate to West Manor was kicked open by Su Mu.

Behind him followed a man with a hole in his neck.

Since directly converting to a spirit would consume too much black mist, Su Mu controlled it first, planning to convert after returning home.

The manor was quiet at this moment, except for a few chirps of insects.

Su Mu looked around.

There were no servants; he didn't know if they had gone home or if they didn't employ many people.

The eyeball popped out of Su Mu's pocket. Being confined to the pocket the whole way had been quite torturous for it, and it certainly wouldn't have been happy if Su Mu hadn't stuck his hand inside.

Freed from the pocket, the eyeball immediately returned to its rightful place:

on Su Mu's head.

Two pairs of wings fluttered on Su Mu's head, making it look from a distance like Su Mu had grown extra ear feathers.

Used to this, Su Mu didn't say anything and continued walking inside.

The location was in a relatively remote suburb; the manor was quite large, with the front yard alone covering an area the size of several houses in the city.

The villa in the middle was also lavishly decorated, with a blue roof and white walls, and several rows of windows.

As they headed towards the villa, the eyeball on Su Mu's head stopped rubbing against his head.

The eyeball descended from Su Mu's head and floated in mid-air, warily surveying its surroundings.

*There are others of the same kind.*

*Malice.*

Having received the telepathic message, Su Mu switched his vision from human mode to black mist mode, and then discovered that his eyes were filled with countless light gray specks radiating the spirit of [moths].

They were everywhere: underground, in the small courtyard, inside the villa, on the roof of the stables.

Wow, there must be over a hundred of them...

Su Mu was shocked by the number.

So, are the people from the Security Bureau really just sitting around doing nothing?

He felt that the Security Bureau's standing in his heart had dropped again, although it hadn't been that high to begin with.

There were many, but most were barely at the early stage of the ninth rank, and overall not particularly strong, probably only slightly stronger than the one that exploded in the An Yin Bureau.

Those seemed to sense Su Mu's presence and began to tremble restlessly.

One of them burst from the ground beneath his feet.

"VV!!"

The grass beneath Su Mu's feet was pushed up, and a plump, green, soft-bodied worm poked its head out.

Su Mu leaped backward and landed steadily.

Although standing directly on the worm's head wouldn't hurt, looking at the thing that resembled a giant caterpillar, Su Mu didn't want to touch it.

If his shoes stepped on it, Su Mu would simply throw them away.

The caterpillar, its lower body buried in the soil, writhed to shake off the dirt, then faced Su Mu. Its two rows of thoracic legs began to crawl out of the soil.

Seeing the insect in its entirety made Su Mu feel even more disgusted.

It was covered in dense, black, fuzzy hairs, its body arranged in rings. It had a pair of long, thin antennae on its head, and its mouth had spider-like teeth, covered in a sticky saliva.

It was about 6 meters long, 2.5 meters wide, and 3 meters high.

Any longer and it would be as big as the Yunda Express.



Chapter 84 Insect Control


Even though it had no eyes, Su Mu felt it was looking at him with a longing gaze.

Damn, how come these cult-raised insects are just like them?

The eyeball floating beside Su Mu immediately hissed upon seeing the insect.

The fist-sized creature blocked Su Mu's view with its two pairs of wings spread wide, almost completely obscuring his vision, its eyes glaring at the insect.

*Mine!*

The insect completely ignored the eyeball's thoughts and pounced straight towards Su Mu.

It kept emitting shrill cries.

Then it was burned to death in 2 seconds by the golden light emanating from the eyeball...

Su Mu looked at the insect's corpse, now a pool of green blood, pinched his nose, and waved his hand.

This big insect smelled even worse after it died.

Seeing that the eyeball's spiritual power had increased after killing the insect, Su Mu nodded in satisfaction.

Not bad, killing so many insects will definitely make me much stronger.

Su Mu walked past the insect corpses towards the villa.

————

Inside the villa, the current head of the West family sat by a second-floor window, his face pressed against the glass, staring wide-eyed at Su Mu in the front yard.

His hands, gripping the window, trembled slightly, his mouth opening and closing several times.

He had originally planned to lie down and rest for a while; although he was a superhuman, he was getting old and his energy wasn't what it used to be.

However, he had only just reached the second floor when he suddenly sensed the insect eggs buried throughout the manor becoming restless. Forgetting all about appearances, he immediately pressed himself against the window to observe the situation.

It was dark, and he could only barely make out, by the dim moonlight, a figure that appeared to be a young man standing on the grass in the front yard.

Then, that young man instantly killed a spiritual creature that had inexplicably hatched…

What kind of monster was this?

This was the first time in his life he had ever seen someone withstand the mental pollution of a spiritual creature and instantly kill it!

Even he, a seventh-tier [Moth], possessed a rare talent for strong mental resistance.

He could enter the forest and retreat unscathed in under 15 seconds. Even so, he would experience a period of disorientation when faced with the mental interference of spiritual creatures.

And this was a spiritual creature according to the [Moth] principle, which could amplify its own mental pollution intensity when it shrieked.

Yet, the boy not only showed no signs of disorientation, but also somehow summoned a ball of light, burning the spiritual creature alive!

What kind of extraordinary being could do such a thing?

And who exactly was that boy?

Why did he suddenly appear in my manor? Did the Security Bureau finally discover my hiding place and send people here?

The old man couldn't understand, but he knew that if he didn't do something soon, he might be the one burned to death.

The old man didn't dare to delay and immediately ran towards the storage room on the third floor, where some special insect eggs were stored, each possessing strength approaching that of a seventh-tier extraordinary being.

Although there's a fair amount of skill involved—each one barely having any effect on a seventh-tier transcendent—their combined strength, including themselves, is roughly equivalent to ten seventh-tier transcendents.

Even if the bishop of the Holy Communion Church came, he wouldn't be able to escape unscathed.

Even with his high mental resistance, the boy couldn't possibly be completely unaffected by the mental interference points of a swarm of seventh-tier [Moths]!

Once he was stalled, his fate was almost certain, whether he was corrupted into a spiritual being or devoured by them.

————

In the front yard, every few steps Su Mu took, several insects would dart out from the side.

Each one looked disgusting: slugs, centipedes, woodlice, termites…

Su Mu regretted coming alone. He should have brought Alice, or at least Vania. Right now, he just wanted to rake the entire manor in with his slashing attacks.

Su Mu simply couldn't stand these insects swarming around him.

He even saw one that looked like a cockroach.

That one left a deep impression on him.

A 10-meter-long cockroach suddenly sprang out from the stables, flapping its wings as it charged towards him!

Su Mu had to admit that he felt an unprecedented fear when he saw that cockroach.

Especially since the cockroach seemed to possess an intense desire to approach and devour him.

Su Mu couldn't resist; the moment he saw the cockroach, he reacted instinctively.

He instantly conjured a 1-meter-diameter blood bead cannon, blasting the cockroach apart the moment it took flight.

Scraps of the cockroach's flesh and blood exploded on the stable floor.

Several horses, splattered with the green blood from the exploding cockroach, let out pitiful neighs.

It instantly consumed one-fifteenth of the black mist within his body.

While objectively it was somewhat of a loss—given the cockroach's strength, a few feathers from its eyeball would have been enough to kill it—

Su Mu didn't feel it was a huge loss. His main concern was that he absolutely didn't want the twin-tailed Guangdong girl near him.

Especially since these insects would explode with green blood after death.

If the cockroach got close and exploded, Su Mu was afraid he would vomit.

These sentient creatures were just too disgusting.

Su Mu even found the caterpillar that greeted him upon entering to be adorable.

Eyeball, in his mind, had now reached its peak of attractiveness.

While its appearance was a bit bizarre, at least it wasn't disgusting.

If it had been these insects that had appeared initially, Su Mu would have chopped them all to dust without hesitation.

Thinking about these random thoughts, Su Mu arrived at the villa's entrance.

The insects in the grass had all been burned away, and the once green grass was even greener… stained with green blood. The smell had changed from the damp, earthy smell of after rain to a stench like rotting corpses.

After absorbing this wave of experience, Eyeball's spiritual energy reserves had increased to the late-stage eighth rank. Considering its spiritual creature characteristics and golden light defense, it could hold its own against an early-stage seventh-rank superhuman. However, it might struggle against someone like Bruce, who was in the mid-stage; the two sides would likely remain locked in a stalemate.

Su Mu had initially planned to use this remote and luxurious place as his second base in Dex City.

But now he completely abandoned that idea.

A house that had once been crawling with insects—even if he scrubbed it until it was peeling, he wouldn't live there.

"I'll have Glenner inform the Security Bureau about this place tomorrow and let them clean it up..."

Su Mu lifted his foot, strengthened his leg muscles with black mist, and then pushed off forcefully.

The villa's front door flew backward with a bang, sliding a short distance across the living room floor before coming to a stop.

They hadn't knocked, fearing the door might have been crawled over by insects.

The living room was pitch black; no human figures were visible.

Then, footsteps echoed down the stairs.

Followed by the sound of something hitting the ground.

Su Mu looked towards the sound and saw ten insect eggs.

Unlike ordinary insect eggs, which are only the size of a thumb, these ten were each the size of a human head.

Then he sensed a spiritual fluctuation emanating from the second floor, the energy of a moth.

The ten insect eggs began to tremble.



Chapter 85 The advantage lies with me...?


The insect eggs hatched under the influence of spiritual fluctuations, and then one by one, chimeras-like insects emerged from them.

They had cockroach carapaces, dragonfly wings, mantis-like forelimbs, centipede-like bodies, and six spider-like eyes that glowed red.

Each one was about 3 meters long, and they all stared intently at Su Mu after seeing him.

“VV!!”

“Hiss—!”

They were considerably smaller than the insects outside.

Su Mu used his true form's vision to observe them; each one possessed a spiritual energy reserve at the peak of the eighth rank.

He controlled the man with a hole in his neck to walk outside.

If so many spiritual creatures corrupted the man, he would have to use some black mist to suppress the corruption, which was a bit troublesome. Although it wouldn't require much, it was better to have some insurance since he might have to meet the local evil god soon.

So this is an elite monster? Even burning all ten of their eyeballs would take some effort.

It was a slight difference in strength, and judging by the armor-like coverings of these insects, it would take some time for the flames to break through them.

Meanwhile, an old man carrying a kerosene lamp steadily descended the stairs.

The old man stood at the top of the stairs, watching Su Mu. Because the only light in the room was from the kerosene lamp, the old man couldn't see Su Mu's face clearly, but he could see Su Mu's somewhat eerie crimson eyes.

He simply stood behind the swarm of writhing spiritual creatures, and the profile illuminated by the firelight showed that the old man wasn't panicked.

With the protection of these spiritual creatures, the old man was now full of confidence.

He was joking; with a group of spiritual creatures nearing the seventh rank acting as protection, even if that bishop came, he couldn't kill him instantly.

No matter how strong his physical body was, a single bite from these spiritual creatures would corrupt and mutate him.

Although this young man had a move that could instantly burn spiritual creatures to death, it was only because the spiritual creatures in the front yard were relatively weak.

He was now unleashing all the insect eggs he had carefully nurtured for over a decade into the battle.

If his physical strength was the only factor, the mental interference from so many [moths] would drive him insane. If his mental strength was the only factor, these armored spiritual creatures could easily kill him with a single bite each.

As for being strong in both body and mind?

He didn't believe anyone could achieve that. Superhumans were generally specialized in one area; how could someone have both high magic resistance and high physical resistance?

Even if someone did have both, their attack power would be completely lacking; he could easily outlast them.

Surely no one could have high magic resistance, high physical resistance, and high attack power simultaneously, right?

He had considered all possibilities.

Even if Bruce came, he was confident he could leave him half-dead.

If this young man could kill all these spiritual creatures, he still had a backup plan.

The place he was standing on connected to the basement.

And there lay the altar his family used for sacrifices.

It allowed them to communicate with the great Seneza.

If this young man could handle so many spiritual beings, the old man planned to summon Him directly.

He didn't want to do that unless absolutely necessary.

After all, the offerings required to invoke a god's descent were simply too numerous.

Doing so would reduce the small town's population by one-fifth.

But...why are these spiritual beings he officially released so eager for this boy?

The old man has a faint connection with these spiritual beings; he can sense their emotions and give them simple commands.

This is a new ability he gained after offering himself to Senesa.

Perhaps it's the hunger of a newborn.

The old man didn't think too much about it.

Su Mu frowned and couldn't help but take a step back.

It wasn't that he was afraid...well, a little afraid, but mainly the sight of those insects huddled together, writhing, made Su Mu a little apprehensive.

Seeing a bunch of insects bigger than a person writhing and charging at you, and you can't resist running away?

Anyway, Su Mu couldn't help it.

His eyes decisively flashed with golden light again, and his feathers flew like arrows towards the insects charging at the front.

The parts of the insects grazed by the feathers began to smell burnt, and they let out painful screams.

But the insect quickly retaliated. Every time a feather was about to land on it, it twisted its body, letting the feather land on its carapace, then quickly shook it off, leaving only a small dent in the carapace after the brief contact.

The eyeball, encountering this situation for the first time, became somewhat anxious and began firing more feathers, finally succeeding in burning through the carapace and head of the foremost insect.

The old man, who had been cautiously observing from behind, showed a look of astonishment.

That eyeball...was it also a spiritual being?

He had initially thought the boy had been burned to death by a spiritual being using supernatural abilities, but it turned out the boy was a spiritual being.

This boy also had a spiritual being; was he also a follower of some deity?

Relieved that the boy wasn't from the An Yin Bureau, he then felt somewhat confused.

How did someone not from the An Yin Bureau find his way here?

Could it be that he knew someone among those he had killed before?

But I always conduct thorough investigations before sending anyone to carry out an operation, making sure everything is in order before making a move.

He decided to observe a little longer. If the boy's strength was similar to his own, he would try to negotiate; if he was weaker, he would kill him and feed him to the spiritual creatures.

He didn't think the boy could be stronger than him. Since they relied on spiritual creatures to fight, how strong could his own strength be?

He was probably a [Moth] or [Lamp]—a superhuman with enhanced mental power.

His direct combat strength couldn't be high.

Meanwhile, the headless insect still twisted its body and charged towards Su Mu. The insects behind it were also quite intelligent, hiding behind it and using it as a human shield.

As expected of insects, it's still alive.

Su Mu was somewhat astonished and decided not to hold back anymore.

He extended his right hand, which he had bitten, and pointed it at the insect. Black mist, now made invisible, flowed from the wound.

After it entered within 10 meters, it spoke in a flat, emotionless tone: "Cut."

Instantly, the insect was sliced ​​vertically in two.

It wasn't a new technique; it was just a name given to the black mist's slash.

And the two halves of its body were still writhing.

Su Mu felt disgusted just looking at it. Before the released black mist could dissipate, he gave the insect's corpse the same treatment as Bruce.

The insects that had intended to use him as a human shield to approach Su Mu didn't immediately stop when their shield was gone. Instead, they formed a circle, trapping Su Mu.

They weren't unintelligent; they had guessed that Su Mu's attack was targeted, which was why they chose to surround him from all sides.

The old man was even more astonished.

What kind of technique was that?

It had actually shredded a spiritual creature in one fell swoop!

His understanding of the young man was completely overturned.



Chapter 86 Peace Talks?


What the hell was that attack?

Is it his supernatural ability?

Judgment?

Is this guy's supernatural ability unrelated to mental power?

Then how can he remain so calm under the mental contamination of so many spiritual beings?

And can any supernatural being really do an attack of this intensity?

Judging from the attack's form, it looks like a [blade]. Does that mean his mental resistance is innate? Stronger than mine!

Calm down. With such high attack and mental resistance, his physical defense must be very low.

Just one bite from a spiritual being would contaminate his body... The advantage is mine!

The idea was beautiful, but reality was cruel.

The old man still harbored illusions, thinking that just one bite from a spiritual being would instantly reverse the battle.

But after seeing the insects swarm around Su Mu's head, his eyes floated directly above Su Mu's head and began to shed feathers.

The insects touched by the feathers were forced to wriggle and shake them off, inevitably shifting their positions.

Once an insect's head came within 10 meters of Su Mu, he would immediately use "Cut" to slice its head into pieces.

Ultimately, the insects were forced back to the old man.

Cold sweat began to trickle down the old man's forehead.

"Sir, when did I have any conflict with you?"

He chickened out.

Su Mu, with the aid of his eyeballs, easily dealt with the three spiritual creatures.

Although he could probably turn the tide if he intervened.

After all, he wasn't like those barely seventh-tier spiritual creatures; he was a pure seventh-tier [Moth]. Even if the boy had exceptionally strong innate mental resistance, he couldn't possibly survive his seventh-tier skills unscathed. Even the slightest lapse in concentration could reverse the situation.

However, he wasn't sure if the boy could withstand a strike like the one he had just delivered.

The boy might be able to withstand a mental attack, but he himself had virtually no chance against the boy's strikes.

Judging from the power of that "Cut" move, even spiritual creatures couldn't withstand it; how could he, a fragile superhuman without any physical enhancements, possibly withstand it?

Damn it, where did those two go…?

The old man began to regret sending his son and grandson out.

Now he was alone facing this strange boy.

He'd try to stall him with conversation first, and then the three of them could surround him for a better chance of victory once the other two returned.

"I hate bugs."

Su Mu simply replied with a nonsensical statement to the old man's question.

A vein throbbed on the old man's forehead.

You hate bugs, yet you came?

Are you just looking for trouble?

The old man realized this boy was here to cause trouble.

"Oh, by the way, your grandson wanted to blow up my house."

?

What does that mean? Has this guy seen Henry?

That brat told him to find someone from the Holy Communion Church, but he went to find someone else instead.

Knowing his grandson as he did, the old man was almost certain that Henry had seen this boy's face on the way and immediately forgot the task he had given him.

The old man tensed instantly, tentatively asking, "It's my fault for failing to discipline him properly. Our West family will offer you compensation far exceeding the value of your house. If you wish, we can even buy you a house you like and gift it to you... But what about my grandson?"

Even if he's crippled, it's fine, just don't die.

"Dead."

The old man's breath hitched, and the image of Seneza killing him in a fit of rage immediately flashed through his mind.

Not necessarily. Lady Seneza lays dozens of insect eggs at a time; these children probably aren't important to her...

"Is that so? Then he deserves it. Don't worry, our West family won't have any objections."

"Who asked you? Now it's my objection."

The old man stared at the boy's profile, illuminated by the golden light of his eyes, lost in thought.

Clearly, the boy came intending to wipe them out.

He's quite handsome, but there's no possibility of negotiation now.

"Sir, you'd better think this through."

"If you think it through, I'm going to rake this entire insect hive in and out."

The old man's gaze met Su Mu's blood-red eyes, a fleeting, pale gray light flashing within them.

......

Old Man: ......

Su Mu: ......

Su Mu blinked.

What's this old man thinking about after staring at me for so long?

The old man's cold sweat deepened.

What's going on? His ability didn't work?!

He had been secretly using his ability while he was staring at the boy.

One of his abilities was to induce temporary amnesia in his enemies through eye contact; the longer the eye contact, the stronger the amnesia effect.

Generally, an eighth-tier superhuman only needs 10 seconds of eye contact to completely forget the memories of the previous 24 hours for the next 30 minutes.

Even a seventh-tier superhuman only needs 1 minute of accumulated eye contact, although it only causes 15 minutes of forgetting for them.

This move's strength lies in its surprise; aside from [Lamp] and [Activate], no one else could detect it.

Judging from the boy's reaction, he clearly hadn't noticed either, but... a full three minutes had passed, why was he completely unharmed?!

The old man instinctively took a step back.

"If you won't make a move, then it's my turn. Eyeball, burn him."

Su Mu reminded him, and the old man snapped out of his shock.

Seeing Eyeball shoot out feathers, he immediately retreated several steps while simultaneously instructing the spiritual creature to disperse its attacks.

As for why Su Mu didn't take a few steps forward to use "Cut,"

mainly because he didn't want to get close to the swarm of wriggling insects in front of the old man.

Furthermore, he had brought Eyeball along to help it level up; removing it himself would negate that effect.

Su Mu had actually noticed the old man's subtle movement as he activated his ability.

He had been using his 360-degree field of vision to prevent any sudden attacks from behind, so when the old man activated his ability, he clearly saw a fluctuation in the old man's spiritual energy.

However, since he himself was unaffected, Su Mu assumed it was the old man suppressing the effects of the potion and paid no attention.

The dispersed insects hissed again, splitting into three groups of two or three and charging towards Su Mu from three different directions.

Their thinking was clear; upon seeing Su Mu activate "Cut," they immediately pointed to the target with their right hand, assuming the attack required aiming.

Furthermore, Su Mu only used his ability when they were within 10 meters, clearly due to distance limitations.

Therefore, they used this as a breakthrough point, simultaneously entering the 10-meter radius from three directions to spread the risk of being attacked.

In the brief exchange just now, they had determined that only Su Mu's slashes posed a threat to them.

While the eyeball feathers could also cause damage, their effect was too slow; they could simply withstand the attack.

The old man, after dodging the feather attack, also glared fiercely at Su Mu.



Chapter 87 Completely Ineffective Mental Interference


"Vvv!"

"Hiss—!"

As the insects swarmed and attacked, the old man, unwilling to give up, unleashed his ability on Su Mu again.

The old man opened his palm, and invisible spiritual energy gathered within it.

Chaotic patterns appeared on his hand.

As the spiritual energy reached its limit, the old man clenched his fist.

The [Moth's] spiritual energy expanded outwards, quickly enveloping Su Mu.

The old man looked at Su Mu, his eyes filled with anticipation.

This was a full-force attack from a seventh-tier being. Even the bishop of the Holy Communion Church wouldn't dare to take it directly. Even if you were a seventh-tier being, you would still be affected.

The ability he had just used could distort the perception of creatures within the spiritual energy's coverage area.

The distortion included perception of distance, direction, body control, and time.

What he had applied to Su Mu and his eyes was to reduce their perception of one second to 0.1 seconds, meaning that 10 seconds of normal time flow would be equivalent to 1 second in their physical sense.

This alters the perception of time in spiritual beings, making one second feel like ten seconds, creating a 100-fold difference in time perception between them.

While it doesn't actually accelerate the spiritual beings, it gives them 100 times more time to think than Su Mu, effectively increasing their speed.

Furthermore, it increases the distance between Su Mu and his eyes by 1 meter, which his brain perceives as 10 meters; it also reverses his vertical and horizontal perception, and interferes with his limb control.

Now, the boy's viewing distance is 10 times greater than before, his perspective is reversed in four directions, and when he tries to control his right hand, he finds himself controlling his left leg.

Even if Su Mu possesses the "Judgment," capable of instantly killing spiritual beings, the 100-fold difference in reaction speed allows them to evade before Su Mu can act.

However, under these interferences, the boy is probably unable to use "Judgment" properly…

"Judgment."

The old man's heart had just warmed with pride when he heard the boy's clear voice.

Then, the two spiritual creatures in front of him instantly turned into chunks of flesh and fell to the ground.

The two insects that Su Mu had pointed at, aided by the old man's abilities, had actually reacted, but their bodies couldn't keep up.

"How is this possible!"

The old man exclaimed in shock.

He looked at Su Mu as if he were a monster.

How could he still move normally under such interference?!

The old man was certain that Su Mu had been interfered with. He could sense that his own spirituality hadn't been hindered; he had made normal contact with Su Mu.

But Su Mu seemed completely unaffected, appearing utterly nonchalant.

He had never heard of anyone being born with such strong mental resistance that they could directly resist the influence of a seventh-tier transcendent!

This was utterly unbelievable!

The old man's worldview was severely shaken, and he stood there, stunned.

Could this young man be hiding some kind of transcendent item or even a sealed artifact that he didn't know about?

Was it a mental defense mechanism?

The old man's initial idea was correct; the spiritual energy he released acted directly on Su Mu's perception without any hindrance.

But it had no effect whatsoever. Su Mu's powerful mental strength completely ignored the influence carried by those spiritual entities, not even considering it an attack. Those influences couldn't even make Su Mu feel anything.

Su Mu simply raised his right hand, spun his body counter-clockwise, and snapped his fingers, saying, "Return."

The entire movement was fluid and seamless.

*Sizzle! Sizzle! Sizzle!*

Countless sounds of shredding flesh rang out simultaneously after the snap of his fingers.

Then came the splattering sound of chunks of flesh hitting the ground.

The floor where the insects had been standing was also affected by the aftershocks of the attack, cracking into countless tiny fissures.

In an instant, all the insects were completely shredded by Su Mu.

Green blood spread across the floor, and then, in the blink of an eye, the old man saw Su Mu standing on the shoe cabinet in the entryway, holding one of the eyeball's wings in his hand.

Su Mu looked at the center of the encirclement where he had been standing, now soaked in green blood, and a look of disgust crossed his face.

He almost touched something filthy.

His previous attack was simply using solidified black mist to slash at people; the spin was to maximize damage.

The black mist on his body could only leave his body for 10 seconds. Allowing the mist to fly from the wound on his finger to the insects would waste some time.

To ensure maximum output, Su Mu chose to spin to shorten the time the black mist spent traveling.

Although waiting for the insects to get closer would be the same, Su Mu didn't want them to get any closer, so he attacked as soon as they entered within 10 meters.

As for why he suddenly chose "Hui" as his name…

Although the essence of the attack hadn't changed, it gave the impression that he had many skills. Even ninth-tier extraordinary beings had three skills; how could he, as a god, only have the abilities to control black mist and spirituality!

However, using the Black Mist Slash consumes a lot of Black Mist; one-fifth has already been used up.

But against group attacks, the all-around "Turning" is still more effective, especially against these insects that can still move even after suffering fatal wounds.

Using Blood Beads doesn't immediately incapacitate them like a slash.

After all, Blood Beads primarily inflict single-target penetrating wounds through compression and kinetic energy, but these insects are basically immune to penetrating wounds.

He raised his right hand, looking at the eyeball he held in his hand, its eyes now vacant.

Its four wings seemed to have a mind of their own, flapping in four different directions.

Has it been affected?

Can spiritual beings be affected by other spiritual beings?

Since he hadn't sensed the old man's attack, Su Mu initially thought the eyeball was affected by the insects.

However, Su Mu quickly dismissed this idea. If it could be affected, it would have done so immediately during the initial attack; how could it only be affecting it now?

Blood-red eyes swept over the still-astonished old man.

It must be this old man's extraordinary ability, but why wasn't I affected?

Although Su Mu could roughly determine from Bruce's memories that the old man's strength was about the same as Bruce's,

Bruce with the cane was stronger than the old man in his normal state, but with so many spiritual beings assisting him, the old man could still easily overpower Bruce as long as he wasn't allowed to get close.

But he sensed the old man was no different from an ordinary person; in fact, Su Mu felt a bit like he was bullying a lonely old man…

Su Mu shook his right hand.

The eyeball followed his movement, swaying from side to side, but its gaze remained dull.

After a moment's thought, Su Mu injected a wisp of black mist into the eyeball.

Instantly, the eyeball regained its clarity, and its four wings stopped darting around.

Su Mu released his hand, and the eyeball flew up again.

It seemed that by imbuing the eyeball with black mist, he could directly absorb the mental influence it had suffered.

Su Mu turned to look at the old man.

The old man, startled by Su Mu's gaze, took two steps back.

This guy was a monster!



Chapter 88 The Door Opens


Not only does it possess overpowered attack methods like "Cut" and "Return," and a sealed artifact seemingly capable of withstanding seventh-tier mental attacks, but it also has a spiritual creature as an ally…

Can a human even fight this?!

The old man didn't hesitate any longer, pulling a fingernail-sized insect egg from his robes and tossing it into the air.

The egg rapidly expanded, bursting forth as a sphere with a radius of 5 meters.

It had no armor, and the flesh looked excellent; even a red-sharp blade wouldn't deflect its attack.

Su Mu watched curiously as the old man prepared to do something, then saw the insect slam into the ground in front of him, creating a deep crater.

The old man leaped down after him; although he couldn't see it, Su Mu could hear that the old man landed on the fleshy ball.

Su Mu wasn't in a hurry, leisurely walking to the edge of the hole and jumping down.

He chose to land directly without stepping on the insect, while his eyes burned it to death a few meters after landing.

Below was a long corridor, the only visible light coming from the kerosene lamp held by the old man.

Su Mu was immediately hit by a strong stench of blood.

Then he saw glass jars filled with human skin placed at intervals along both sides of the basement, some broken, indicating their contents had just been removed.

However, Su Mu was starting to get used to it.

————

The old man, clutching several intact human skins and one piece stitched together from different people's skins, ran frantically, the steady footsteps of the boy following behind.

He gritted his teeth and quickened his pace, finally reaching the end of the corridor.

The old man paused to catch his breath before pushing open a door at the end of the corridor. Inside was a large altar, candles lit at the four corners, their pale gray flames barely illuminating the dim room.

He covered the candles with several intact human skins, letting the flames scorch them. He then placed the stitched-up skins in the center of the altar, before pulling out a small knife and slicing open his own palm, dripping blood onto the seams.

The old man uttered hoarse, low murmurs, as if chanting something, but it wasn't a common language.

As he recited a prayer, his blood began to flow along the stitches to various parts of the skins, and the surrounding candlelight grew brighter.

The patterns drawn in blood beneath the altar began to twist and deform, eventually revealing a spider.

The space before him began to distort, then cracked.

The space revealed a forest-like scene, but the colors were so vibrant they seemed unreal, like a dream.

A strong mental pollution emanated from the cracked space. The old man coughed up blood, but the blood turned a dark green after he coughed it up.

His bleeding right palm also began to deform, his fingers becoming long and thin, and developing several additional joints.

The old man wasn't surprised. This was spiritual pollution from the woods; it wouldn't have been this severe in the past, but this time he'd gone all out, practically emptying his entire fortune. The stitched human skin used as a magical artifact was sourced from various social classes.

Unlike the usual ten-second or so sessions, this time the door would remain open for half an hour.

He dared not imagine what might emerge from within.

He dared not even think about what would happen to himself.

Just then, the boy's footsteps reached the doorway.

The old man turned and glared at Su Mu with a ferocious expression.

His lips curled into an exaggerated grin as he laughed, “Prepare to face my lord!”

“Die! You can’t possibly survive under the radiance of Lady Silesa. After you die, I will skin you alive as the main material for my next magical artifact. Thank me properly, for I will let you bask in the radiance of my lord and become nourishment for her!”

Even if this youth was a seventh-tier transcendent being with strong mental resistance, he couldn’t possibly stand against the great Silesa!

Under the guidance of the god, he would utterly kill this arrogant youth!

He wanted to savor the look of despair on the youth’s face, and personally dismember him as the freshest sacrifice for Lady Silesa!

Su Mu frowned as he looked at the old man’s crazed state.

Five eyes, a forked tongue, a mouth stretched to his ears, and the fingers of his bleeding hand had transformed into organs resembling insect legs.

"The potion in your body has completely ruined you. I estimate that by tomorrow you'll only be half human."

Su Mu kindly reminded him, but the old man didn't listen at all.

"Heh, is this your last words!"

"Only regretting it now that you're about to die, trying to curry favor with me? It's too late!"

"I gave you a chance, but a foolish, ignorant commoner like you, even if you become a superhuman, will only be a rat that doesn't know its own limitations!"

"Don't worry, I will cut off your limbs in the most painful way, making your fear and despair the most delicious seasoning for Lady Senesa!"

"I will keep your skull as a collection, give your spine to my son, and make a ring from your finger bones for my grandson!"

The old man had begun to ramble incoherently, his words uttered incoherently.

"Are you out of your mind? Your grandson and your son have already been killed by me."

The old man's frenzied words stopped abruptly, and he froze in place, his reason returning at Su Mu's words.

*That was close! I was actually affected by the potion just now…*

"Wait, what do you mean? My son?"

Didn't this guy only kill Henry?

"Yes, your son is dead too. Your family is extinct."

A sudden feeling of impending doom made the old man's breath catch in his throat.

His grandson didn't matter, he wasn't important, but this son was his only remaining bloodline.

The spiritual energy within him began to surge with his emotions.

"What did you say!"

But Su Mu ignored his anger and continued to mock, "Hard-hearing? Your grandson is dead, your son is dead, I did it all. You're literally childless."

"You will pay for this!"

The old man's roar subsided, and the space behind him stabilized. Then, a sharp insect-like thoracic leg emerged from it.

The old man turned around at the sound and a cruel smile appeared on his face.

"Die! You—!"

Before he could finish speaking, the old man's body was violently thrown to the side.

His body slammed solidly against the wall, spitting out more green blood.

It was the spider leg that did it, seemingly annoyed by the old man's loud voice.

And its eyes tensed up after the leg stepped out, the golden light on its surface flickering.

*Strong*

*Danger*

Then more spider legs emerged from the space, followed by a head.

The entire head was densely packed with eyes, devoid of pupils or whites; the eyes were pure crimson.

As soon as the head appeared, those eyes immediately locked onto Su Mu, who was standing in the doorway.

Meanwhile, the old man slammed against the wall sat slumped on the ground, coughing up blood and leering maliciously.

"Heh, heh heh, die, you lowly commoner."

He felt his spine was broken, but he didn't care about that now; he only wanted the boy before him dead.



Chapter 89 Can't Beat, Can't Run Away


Su Mu raised an eyebrow at the head covered in eyes.

Is this the local evil god?

It doesn't seem any different from other spiritual beings, not even as special as the eyeballs floating beside him.

It doesn't seem very strong either.

Judging by its spiritual energy alone, it's only at the late seventh-tier level, slightly stronger than Bruce.

Su Mu glanced at the old man whose body had turned half green.

This guy probably doesn't recognize some spiritual being as an evil god, does he?

Perhaps its main strength lies in its own mental pollution. Su Mu, completely unaffected, wasn't sure if it was different from other insects.

However, judging from the old man's transformation, he should be much stronger.

The spider only extended four legs and a head, but these alone almost filled the entire basement space.

Su Mu had to crane his neck to see the spider's head, which was reaching towards the ceiling.

The part of its head that was sticking out was 5 meters long and wide.

And the spider's first reaction upon seeing Su Mu was to eat this human.

Then one of its eyes noticed Su Mu's right hand, which was still emitting black mist.

The spider's eye instantly shrank to half its size, and its legs began to twitch uneasily.

It was afraid.

The old man noticed the twitching spider legs and suddenly burst into laughter.

"Ha, ha, you're finished! Lady Senesa is already excited..."

The old man didn't know why the spider legs were twitching, but in his understanding, it couldn't be a sign of fear.

How could a god fear a human?

It must be the excitement of seeing prey!

Then he saw the spider's head abruptly retract into the doorway, its movement so fast that it bumped against the doorframe.

Following that, its four legs also frantically retreated into the doorway.

Su Mu: ......

The old man: ......

The old man stared at this scene, mouth slightly agape, unable to utter a sound.

The eye, which had mutated into something resembling insect tentacles, also straightened up, its pupil widening in disbelief as it stared at the shattered spatial passage.

Su Mu looked at the old man and asked, "You believe in this stuff?"

"No, impossible! Lady Senesa only thinks you're too weak for her, so she's sending her children to eat you!"

He started to deceive himself, but then several insects actually crawled out of the door, and the old man's eyes lit up instantly.

"Hahahaha! See that, you lowly commoner? You don't even deserve Lady Senesa's personal attention."

Su Mu glanced at the insects crawling out.

There's a mysterious feeding opening?

Su Mu met the eyeball's gaze.

The eyeball immediately ran up and started molting feathers at the doorway; every insect that came out died.

The old man's laughter subsided over time.

He realized his thinking might be wrong; these insects seemed to be just other spiritual creatures from the forest...

"You, you shouldn't be so happy yet! It's just that Lady Senesa's children haven't come out yet!"

In just 30 seconds, he went from arrogant to stubborn.

"Really? Then I'll go say hello to them."

After finding nothing coming out of the door, Su Mu moved closer, looking into the space while avoiding the insect corpses.

The vibrant grassland within the space was completely bare except for some unhatched insect eggs.

But the trees were enormous, stretching as far as the eye could see.

Looking into the distance, a massive black shadow was frantically fleeing—it was the spider.

Su Mu stepped into the woods without hesitation, his eyes following closely behind.

"I came here to collect you; it would be a huge loss of face if you got away."

Entering the woods, Su Mu felt a sense of homecoming.

It was similar to his own space, yet different.

Su Mu tried to stop the black mist from flowing from his wound, allowing it to flow out more.

He then discovered that the black mist had no distance limit in the woods, and its duration also increased.

Su Mu's eyes lit up. He reached out from outside the space and used the knife the old man had used to cut his palm, slashing it directly at the carotid artery in his arm.

Instantly, a torrent of black mist surged out, spreading rapidly like ink dropped into water.

The spider, frantically sprinting on its twelve legs in the distance, sensed something and glanced back. Its dozen or so scarlet eyes instantly shrank, leaving only a cluster of red dots.

It accelerated again, running for its life.

Behind it, a black mist, utterly out of place with the surrounding forest, began to spread from the point of the spatial rift, instantly obscuring the sky.

And it continued to approach its location.

It now felt deep regret.

If it had known this would happen, it would never have chosen to build its nest in the place where the spatial rift appeared!

Originally, it had chosen that area because food appeared intermittently, and it was far from the dangerous central area of ​​the forest, so it wouldn't have to worry about being attacked by other spiritual creatures.

A human had even come out of the spatial rift to offer its blood.

It could smell the human's scent—the one who had occasionally brought food to the forest—so it drank the blood and granted him a small ability to control its offspring.

The rift was unusually large today, and no food emerged. So it poked its head out to see what was going on.

The first glance was pleasant; although the first human was noisy, the other looked incredibly delicious.

Then it saw black mist slowly drifting out of the human's right hand.

Just one glance, and an inexplicable fear filled its mind. It didn't know what it was, but a primal, instinctive fear told it that if it didn't run, it would die.

So it ran, waking all its surviving offspring before it fled, hoping they would buy it some time.

But it had only run a short distance when it saw the billowing black mist rushing towards it!

It felt like it was seeing a ghost.

Based on the previous events, there shouldn't have been anything powerful on the other side of the spatial rift; the most powerful one would have difficulty even stepping into the forest. Now, suddenly, this terrifying thing had appeared!

Tears even welled in its crimson eyes. Its dozen or so legs blurred as it darted forward, expelling all the spider eggs from its body to speed things up.

Then, it raised its spinneret, aiming it at a tree twice its body thickness, intending to swing across.

Once it entered the forest, there was still a chance to escape. Perhaps it would even encounter other sentient beings, sparking a fight…

Then he saw a strong gust of wind sweep by, and the giant tree, 28 meters in diameter, was snapped in half and fell to the ground.

With a dull thud, the wind whipped up by the falling trunk lifted him up and sent him flying backward, his spiderwebs billowing wildly through the air, completely obscuring his vision.

He couldn't see anything.

Where did I go?



Chapter 90 again?


It took the spider a long time to regain control of its body. It wobbled to its feet, trying to see where it had been blown by the shockwave of the falling tree.

Hopefully, it could get away from that spatial rift...

Then it saw an eyeball floating in front of it.

The two creatures stared at each other in silence.

“V—!”

The spider let out a sharp shriek, its fangs lashing out at the eyeball.

Golden light flashed across the eyeball's surface, and feathers flew directly towards the spider's eyes, completely ignoring the spider's attack.

The golden light momentarily halted the spider's attack, but it was quickly pierced through.

The spider's fangs, defying the golden light's defense, pierced directly at the eyeball, emitting a sound that seemed like disdain.

I can't defeat that black mist, but I can certainly defeat a spiritual creature that appeared out of nowhere.

The feathers fired from the eyeball only created a wisp of smoke around the spider, completely failing to harm it.

Just as the spider was gloating, black mist surged from within its eyeball.

The tips of its two fangs, aimed at the eyeball, were instantly swallowed upon contact with the black mist, leaving only the rear halves.

"No?

How come you have it too!"

Before it could even react, the spider immediately stopped and turned to run.

But it was too late; its escape route was blocked.

Then, the black mist from afar spread to this spot, completely enveloping the spider in its ominous aura.

The spider let out a piercing scream, trying to beg for mercy.

But it was no use; the black mist lifted the spider, which was enveloped within it.
