The spider's cries abruptly ceased; only a dozen or so legs flailing outside the black mist, but quickly those legs severed and fell to the grass.

After a while, the black mist began to dissipate, and the spider's dying body fell from mid-air.

Its body was covered in dense, intricate cuts, severed in two, yet a sliver of life remained.

*You've collected the head.*

Eyeball, receiving the command, delivered the final blow to the spider.

Just as Su Mu had guessed, as long as Eyeball collected the head, it could absorb the spirituality of spiritual creatures. However, because the spirituality of spiritual creatures needed to be converted into its own unconditional spirituality, the conversion efficiency was average.

Adding the experience gained from feeding it before, it was now probably at the late stage of the seventh rank.

Not bad; excluding itself, it was its strongest combat force.

Su Mu's black mist had been spreading for nearly five minutes and was gradually dissipating without seeing any other creatures, except for the insects that often hid in the grass.

Was there too few spiritual creatures in the forest, or was this place too remote?

Su Mu blocked the main artery to prevent the black mist from flowing out further and used the black mist to suture the wound.

Finding nothing of use inside, Su Mu jumped out through the spatial rift.

The old man lay there, barely alive.

His previously relieved eyes widened again when he saw Su Mu emerge.

"You...you're not dead?"

Su Mu glanced at him.

"Your faith isn't any better. You died in two hits. Choose a better faith in your next life."

The old man felt like the sky had fallen.

Wait, a human entered the forest for almost seven minutes and was completely unharmed?

The old man scanned Su Mu from head to toe a dozen times before confirming one fact.

This guy isn't human.

What human could withstand the mental pollution of the forest for seven minutes without showing any signs of abnormality?!

This guy isn't human!

He's a complete monster. Could he, like Henry, actually be a spiritual being?

Just then, footsteps echoed down the corridor.

The old man turned his gaze.

Any accomplices...?

Then he saw his son walk in carrying a secret manual and a potion, muttering to himself.

"So poor. All you have left is a bottle of eighth-tier potion and a secret manual. No wonder there's no record of you in the An Yin Bureau's files."

"You...you...what's going on?!"

He was utterly confused.

Wasn't he supposed to be dead?

Who is this?

Could it be that his son wanted to seize the throne early and hired someone to kill him?

But that's just plain crazy! He's the only heir left; the entire family will be his sooner or later!

The hole in his neck was still bleeding—was he dead or alive?

The man had been rummaging through the house while Su Mu was in the woods, and now he'd found the few useful items.

Besides these two, the West house was full of insect eggs; Su Mu didn't want to touch any of them.

"Surprised? Your son isn't completely dead, just half-dead. Don't worry, I won't let you bury him. Aren't I kind?"

The old man glared at Su Mu with murderous intent.

"I'm going to kill you..."

Without hesitation, Su Mu severed half of his body.

"Aaaaah!!"

The old man screamed in agony.

His mutated insect-like half of his body lay curled up and writhing on the ground.

"Alright, anyone can talk tough. If you want to kill me, you'd better be able to run 10 meters in the next 10 seconds while barely alive, maintain supersonic speed after that, possess an insect stronger than that spider in the forest, and withstand the mental pollution of a late-stage seventh-tier spiritual creature..."

The old man was already delirious, his ears ringing.

"Okay, enough of that. Goodnight."

With those words, the old man's body turned into a pile of minced meat.

————

The next morning.

Glenner crawled out of bed, looking exhausted.

He had narrowly escaped death last night, and hadn't even had to do the subsequent paperwork.

The captain had told him, Edric, and Vivian to go home early and rest, giving each of them three days off.

The reason given was that he had played a significant role in the battle, so he didn't need to help with the subsequent trivial matters.

Although he found the reason ridiculous—he was practically useless throughout the entire battle except for throwing out a smokescreen, which served a negligible purpose—he was essentially a mere accessory.

He was immediately blasted away by Bruce's Boiling Blood attack upon emerging from the fray. If it weren't for the mysterious figure within him preemptively protecting the sealed artifact, he would likely have died on the spot.

His subsequent attempt to retreat with the captain failed, and they were rescued by the mysterious figure...

To be honest, he felt a little guilty about giving him a break.

Did this performance really warrant a three-day leave?

Edric and Vivian, on the other hand, were due to injuries.

Although both were actually fine with the help of the mysterious man's teammates,

Vivian's mild anemia was even cured.

So versatile...

Glenner couldn't imagine how there could be such a superhuman with such high damage output and rapid healing.

According to the An Yin Bureau's data, very few superhumans possessed healing abilities.

Even the healing of the [Heart] only produced a healing effect by accelerating the body's metabolic rate.

But that boy could heal wounds with just a touch.

Even a severed finger could grow new flesh!

Just as Glenner was preparing breakfast and recalling last night's experiences, the mysterious man within him spoke again.

*To celebrate the successful completion of your first mission, I have brought you a gift.*

...

Again?



Chapter 91: Continuing to Work Overtime


Glenner felt numb when he heard Su Mu's voice.

Wait, this efficiency, is it for real?

One report a day?!

Glenner felt the difference between people.

More accurately, the difference between organizations.

The An Yin Bureau, where he had worked for years, hadn't managed to gather any useful intelligence.

While this mysterious organization he was forced to work for had big news every day…

The An Yin Bureau was really useless…

But he couldn't think of it that way. Maybe the organization behind the mysterious man had actually been established in Dex City for many years, which was why they had this kind of intelligence efficiency…

*Please tell me, sir. I'll remember.*

Su Mu briefly explained the relevant information about the West family to Glenner. He found the rest about the woodlands and the West family history too troublesome to explain, so he told Glenner to pass the information to the An Yin Bureau and let them investigate themselves.

Glenner listened intently, not even noticing the pork chops in the pot were burning, until he smelled the burning and turned them over.

*That's about it. Tell the people at the Security Bureau to clean up the scene as soon as possible. Although they don't have any servants at the residence right now, they might come to work later, and if they see the scene, they could be directly contaminated and mutated.*

*Okay, I'm on my way!*

————

At the same time, inside the Security Bureau.

Blake slumped in his office chair, motionless. The matter was finally resolved, and Blake wanted to take a break.

Browter, who came from next door, had also returned, but he still looked distraught. He had telegraphed Browter's captain about last night's events, hoping Browter wouldn't be punished.

After working through the night, the intelligence on the Holy Communion Church was finally compiled, mainly the distribution of the Holy Communion Church in other regions and the approximate strength of each region.

It's hard to say whether it's lucky or unlucky.

Judging from the correspondence between the bishops of these Holy Communion branch churches, most bishops in the region didn't possess the overwhelming strength of a seventh-tier opponent like Bruce. The vast majority didn't have any extraordinary items or sealed artifacts.

Overall, it was fortunate. Although his Security Bureau was unfortunate to encounter such a powerful bishop, other regions, with the support of intelligence, should have an easier time than his side.

Most importantly, although the Bishop of An he faced was overwhelmingly stronger, the entire battle ended with an astonishing zero casualties!

The only one who died was Zobert, who was suspected of betraying the Holy Communion Church…

The other members were mostly unharmed, and even the three who were injured were miraculously healed and recovered completely by the mysterious youth…

Blake picked up his now-cold coffee, took another sip, and let out a long sigh.

The headquarters' instructions regarding the youth, who was suspected of belonging to another mysterious society, were to observe, attempt an investigation but not delve too deeply. From what they seemed to be friendly, they could try cooperation.

Even if cooperation is not possible, it is best to maintain a friendly relationship.

There are so many secret organizations; even if the Security Bureau expanded tenfold, it couldn't eradicate them all.

Especially considering that boy's strength—at the very least, he could easily defeat the Security Bureau captain himself. If headquarters dared to give him a mission to eliminate them, Blake would immediately quit.

Are you kidding? Sending someone to eliminate someone with that kind of strength would be tantamount to suicide.

Fortunately, the people at headquarters aren't fools. Headquarters doesn't have that many high-level combatants to send right now, and they understand the power gap between the two sides. Cooperating with a local neutral organization is naturally the best option.

Like the armored man at the ferry crossing; although they haven't reached a cooperation agreement yet, at least they maintain a neutral stance.

Besides, the ferry crossing merely provides a trading platform for local superhumans; they haven't done anything too heinous, so the Security Bureau turns a blind eye.

"This Dex City is truly a place where hidden talents abound..."

Blake remarked, then a series of urgent knocks sounded at the door.

"Please come in."

Blake looked at the newcomer with surprise.

"Glenner? Didn't I give you three days off? Don't push yourself too hard, you need to balance work and rest..."

Blake advised, after all, he was just a newcomer, and after such an intense battle, he needed to rest properly. Too much mental pressure in their line of work could lead to problems.

"Captain, I have a new discovery!"

"..."

Blake, who was about to say a few more words of advice, shut his mouth and instead looked Glenner up and down with a look of astonishment.

No, not again!

Damn it, calm down for a bit. With the current strength of the Security Bureau, going to fight Bruce again would really result in a total wipeout!

"Ahem, ahem, Glenner, what is it?"

Although he felt astonished and confused, as a superior, Blake maintained basic composure.

Glenner began to relay the information Su Mu had given him to Blake.

He only mentioned the location and situation of West Manor, claiming he discovered it while taking a walk.

Upon hearing the details, Blake breathed a sigh of relief.

Thank goodness. It seems it was just two groups of superhumans fighting. The Security Bureau will probably only be responsible for cleaning up the mess...

Wait, why are there other superhuman organizations in Dex City?

Blake's relaxed mood returned.

He now had a pessimistic thought: "If I had known Dex City would have so much trouble, I wouldn't have come here in the first place."

But it was too late to say anything now. He'd been doing this for years, and he could only grit his teeth and continue.

Hopefully, these other superhuman organizations wouldn't display the absurd fighting power of the Holy Communion Church...

————

Morning, West Manor.

Blake arrived at West Manor with several field agents from the Security Bureau, accompanied by three people who should have been on vacation.

Unfortunately, although they were very generous in approving the leave yesterday, they had no choice but to call them back due to unforeseen circumstances.

However, neither Vivian nor Edric showed any dissatisfaction.

They didn't really need the leave; their biggest problem was probably insomnia last night.



Chapter 92: A dense mass of corpses


Edric felt incredibly insecure because the boy had taken his knife, leaving him with only a pistol and a regular machete until the Bureau issued new weapons.

Vivian, on the other hand, had spent the entire night thinking about the boy.

Her right hand clenched unconsciously.

Even after a night, she still found it unbelievable.

Her finger bones had been melted...

Unbelievable.

If she hadn't witnessed firsthand the boy's ability—that invisible slash—she would have definitely considered him a high-level [Heart], no, even a [Heart] couldn't perform such healing.

She had never even heard of such miraculous healing.

Such power could only come from sealed artifacts, but how many sealed artifacts must that boy be carrying?

The ability to transform a superhuman's corpse into a spiritual sealing artifact, to convert a [cup] into a blood bead and grant it immense speed, coupled with the sealing artifact displayed while healing himself…

Moreover, she hadn't seen anything resembling a sealing artifact in the boy's hands, and his clothes didn't seem like they would contain one.

Vivian considered a possibility: the boy didn't rely on sealing artifacts but genuinely possessed these abilities…

But she quickly dismissed this idea.

How could any superhuman possibly possess both high damage output and high healing power?

It's impossible for everyone's superhuman abilities to be specialized while he's the only one with well-rounded skills, right?

Suppressing her chaotic thoughts, Vivian began her work.

But as soon as she pushed open the door, everyone's expression changed.

The ground in the manor was covered in a lush green.

But it wasn't weeds, because the plants had all withered, revealing the color of the bare soil.

But the soil wasn't brown; it was a deep, dark green.

The numerous corpses of spiritual creatures, large and small, lying in the front yard hinted at the strange color of the soil.

The blood left behind by those dead spiritual creatures had stained the entire area!

Blake's expression turned grave. So many spiritual creatures—had he wandered into a woodland?

"Be careful and on guard. Run immediately if you spot anything wrong."

What kind of battle had taken place with so many corpses of spiritual creatures?

This thought involuntarily crossed everyone's minds.

Mainly because the area was densely packed with the corpses of insects. They couldn't imagine how terrifying the mental pollution these insects must have caused when they were alive.

Led by Blake, Edric brought up the rear as the others slowly explored further in.

Every few steps, Blake had to stop and confirm with Vivian that there were no spiritual fluctuations, and then use his ability on the corpses to ensure they were truly dead before daring to continue.

They didn't have anyone with innate mental resistance strong enough to fight against the mental pollution of spiritual creatures, so they had to be extremely cautious.

Unlike the others' tension, Glenner remained remarkably calm.

He was the only one among them who was certain there was no longer any danger.

This was not only due to the mysterious man's words, but also because his spiritual alarm hadn't gone off.

After being fed information several times by the mysterious man, he had fully digested the potion, and his spiritual alarm had become even more accurate.

He'd sensed a major problem within the manor when he was at the entrance, but it wasn't dangerous. He just hadn't expected it to be filled with the corpses of insects...

And each one was astonishingly large; compared to them, he felt more like an insect.

As the group ventured deeper, they discovered that these spiritual creatures seemed to have been burned to death by a dense barrage of sparks.

The insects' bodies were covered in black cratered pits.

Was it [Forging]?

Blake wondered, then witnessed a different scene.

Ahead of the group was a 10-meter-long, cockroach-like spiritual creature.

Its body lay sprawled on the muddy ground, its legs curled up.

It looked much like a dead cockroach.

But the horrifying thing was that its body had been pierced through.

A hole about 1 meter in diameter pierced through the cockroach's body, starting from its head.

Blake could see its internal organs and countless tiny green spheres, like insect eggs.

Upon seeing those spheres, Blake immediately became alert.

"Could this guy still have offspring alive?"

He then looked at Vivian.

"Vivian, can you sense any spiritual fluctuations?"

Glenner's abilities excel at finding hidden traces of the supernatural, while Vivian can directly sense spiritual fluctuations.

This isn't very useful when searching for hidden supernatural beings, because supernatural beings are generally very cautious and don't use their abilities recklessly, which means Vivian's abilities are mostly useless.

But spiritual beings are different; their very existence constantly releases spiritual energy, so Vivian's abilities are extremely useful in such situations.

Vivian shook her head and then nodded.

“Captain, in my perception, none of these spiritual creatures show any spiritual fluctuations. This one… has, but it's the fluctuations of a normal spiritual creature's corpse.”

Vivian composed herself and spoke again, “This is very abnormal. The others seem to have had their spirituality completely stolen. Logically, a spiritual creature's corpse should still retain some spirituality, but these spiritual creatures have none…”

Meanwhile, Su Mu, who was eavesdropping inside Glenner's body and on patrol, felt relieved upon hearing this.

Luckily, back at the Security Bureau, the eyeball hadn't fully exerted its power because it wasn't entirely willing; it had only absorbed a portion of the insect's spirituality.

Otherwise, if Vivian connected the eyeball with this matter, Glenner would probably be in a bad mood.

Blake frowned upon hearing this.

“Stripped away? Who could do something like this…”

Blake suddenly remembered the boy's actions last night, converting the superhuman into a spirit.

Could it be that boy?

No, that's unlikely. Even if he had a sealing artifact capable of directly extracting spiritual energy, it's impossible for him to directly strip a spiritual being of its spiritual essence, right?

Blake thought it was more likely that another spiritual being did it, since some spiritual beings gain power by killing other spiritual beings.

Besides, the transformation method the boy displayed wouldn't leave a corpse.

Blake turned his gaze back to the cockroach's corpse.

He quickly noticed that it was different from the other insect corpses.

Blood was seeping from the hole piercing its body, not the corrosive green blood of a spiritual creature, but a dark red, almost human-like blood!

Moreover, Blake now noticed that looking back through that hole, he could see circular gaps in the stable roof, surrounded by dark red bloodstains.

Blake immediately thought of the communion-style blood-bead bullets the boy had used in front of them last night.

Could it really have been that boy?!



Chapter 93 A Powerful Mysterious Organization?


Blake had already concluded that Su Mu was responsible for the cockroach's corpse.

But this realization terrified him even more.

Wait, a boy who looked a full twelve years younger than him could kill so many spiritual creatures?

But he quickly considered a more likely scenario.

Their entire organization must have attacked together.

Even so, it was still absurd, but it was more convincing than the boy single-handedly slaughtering them all.

After all, no matter how strong a superhuman is, encountering spiritual creatures is a headache.

For superhumans, encountering spiritual creatures is like encountering their natural enemy; they not only suffer from mental pollution but also have to suppress their magical potions.

Their combat power is reduced by 30%.

Even if the boy was very strong, strong enough to easily kill a seventh-tier being,

it's impossible for him to have the courage to face so many spiritual creatures at once… probably?

Moreover, just looking at this, we can see two different attack methods. Could it be that the boy has some other sealed artifact capable of harming spiritual creatures?

How powerful must the organization behind him be?

Shaking his head, Blake said to the others, "Let's move on."

Let's see what's inside first.

Carefully avoiding the corpses of the insects, the group arrived at the entrance of the manor villa.

The villa door was open, and the scene inside confirmed Blake's suspicion that the boy from last night had come.

He saw the insects chopped into pieces.

Vivian spoke up, "Captain, some of these still have residual spiritual energy."

Blake nodded.

It seemed, as he suspected, that the boy had come with other members of his organization.

Those that appeared to have been stripped of their spirituality were likely the work of the boy's companions.

The group began exploring the villa, returning to the living room only after gathering all the useful information.

From what they currently understood, the West family, from top to bottom, were followers of the evil god named Seneza.

They engaged in constant sacrifices, large and small, making them a truly fanatical family.

Furthermore, Blake discovered that he had seen this family head again in the headquarters' records.

They were a small family that had been attacked incidentally during the siege of the Holy Communion Church. However, they hadn't used the West surname then, and according to the records, they were quite weak, with their strongest member only reaching the ninth rank. The Security Bureau was preoccupied with clearing out the remnants of the Holy Communion Church and hadn't had time to deal with them, resulting in them escaping here.

And yet, after arriving here, they thrived.

Not only did the family head possess the strength to confront a Holy Communion Church bishop, but they also mastered a ritual capable of opening a portal to the woodland.

This answered Blake's previous questions.

The spiritual creature exposed by the Security Bureau was the work of this family; they had even cooperated with the Holy Communion Church, giving Bruce several insect eggs.

Blake looked at Glenner and exclaimed, "Glenner, you're truly remarkable! When did you realize that the 'Moth' was the West family's butler?"

"Ah, oh, right, uh, when I saw that old butler, I had a feeling of déjà vu. Then I remembered seeing him during a police call. This morning I came here hoping to find some clues, and then I saw what was happening outside the manor..."

After a moment of stunned silence, Glenner quickly realized what had happened and fabricated a non-existent experience.

He had no idea that the man he'd met earlier was the West family's butler; everything the mysterious figure had told him was true.

He was a complete novice; his knowledge of the supernatural was entirely from the Security Bureau. How could he possibly know members of such a cult?

But with more experience, Glenner's ability to lie improved, and he was no longer as flustered as before.

Blake felt a pang of melancholy. Thankfully, the family had been wiped out by that mysterious organization; otherwise, if they had also performed some kind of ritual to summon an evil god, it would have been disastrous.

But this didn't lift Blake's spirits much.

While the annihilation of a hidden cult was a good thing, the thought that its destruction had absolutely nothing to do with their Security Bureau was still a concern…

The mysterious organization that destroyed the cult possessed the combined strength to withstand the mental pollution and attacks of a group of spiritual beings, roughly equivalent to two seventh-tier superhumans, and had killed them all…

And they would most likely still have to contend with new spiritual beings emerging from the forest, and might even have to face the so-called evil god, Sinessa…

This kind of setup is only found in security bureaus in large cities…

Such a powerful organization, how come he'd never heard of them before?

If he'd known this small place had so many experts, he would definitely have requested more resources from headquarters.

This level of strength is nothing like that of a small city.

Blake made up his mind that he would definitely have a serious discussion with headquarters about the amount of resources they had been allocated.

It had to be increased; if it wasn't, they might not even be able to participate in battles between local superhumans in the future.

—

Meanwhile, Su Mu, the leader of the mysterious organization, sat on the sofa, drinking milk and holding a book.

Why milk? Because the coffee here was really awful; he always drank milk with sugar.

*The Dream Ranger's Adventures*

The name sounds like a fairy tale book.

His eyeballs lay comfortably on his head, while Lorina, sitting beside him, would occasionally glance at them with displeasure.

Noticing this, Eyeball defiantly grabbed a strand of Su Mu's hair with its wing.

"You mere human, can you do that?"

Lorrina gritted her teeth, then watched as Su Mu grabbed Eyeball by the wing and flung it aside.

"Don't disturb me while I'm reading."

Lorina immediately gave a mocking look, and smugly hummed twice.

Heh, that mere eyeball was just taking advantage of its size to cling to the Lord of the Mist's head, and now it's been chased away!

The eyeball felt a little embarrassed.

It had originally wanted to boast that its relationship with Su Mu was better than this human's.

After all, they were practically the same kind, while these two humans were just pets!

And then, they were ruthlessly pushed away, accused of being in the way...

Eyeball felt wronged.

It wasn't like this yesterday when it called me to fight!

It kept putting its paws on me to make me more comfortable, and now it won't let me do that for such a small thing.

*Bad...*

Su Mu ignored it and continued reading.

It's just a pet; it shouldn't disturb his reading of the secret manual.

Seeing Su Mu's heartlessness, Eyeball felt even sadder.

Then it turned and glared at Lorina.

It's all that human's fault!

Otherwise, I'd definitely be lying here comfortably. Why did I have to grab my hair like that...?

Lorna, not to be outdone, glared back and even made a face, taking advantage of her good looks.

Eyeball could only glare back even harder, spreading its wings to make itself look more intimidating.

Today, I'll show you, human, how powerful I am!

Although that's what he thought, because Su Mu had ordered him not to use his abilities on them, Eyeball could only fly over and bump Lorina's head with its wings.



Chapter 94 The Daily Life of a Certain Cult


"Ouch, that hurts."

Caught off guard, Lorina was bumped, her forehead slightly red, tears welling in her eyes. She reached out to grab the eyeball.

But the despicable eyeball simply flew high, and Lorina jumped up and down but couldn't touch a single feather.

"Damn it, come down if you dare!"

The eyeball rolled its eyes at her, letting out a mocking sound.

In the living room, Alice watched the two—one person and one...eyeball—chasing and playing around the sofa, a relieved smile on her face.

That's good, Lorina is much more lively than before.

Then Alice continued reciting.

"One times one equals one..."

She hadn't made Alice recite yesterday when they went out on missions, but since she had nothing to do today, she definitely wouldn't be able to escape.

Su Mu had also finished reading the secret manual.

It was quite interesting.

A story about changing the minds of bad people and robbing the rich to help the poor by infiltrating dreams.

According to the story, everyone's dreams reside in a larger space outside of reality.

These dreams are connected to the subconscious.

Therefore, by finding and entering a person's dream cocoon within this space, one can indirectly alter their personality by changing their thoughts within the dream.

However, although everyone's dreams reside in this space, leaving one's own dream cocoon and personally entering that space is extremely difficult.

Only those with strong mental power can do it; otherwise, the inherent influence of entering that space might render them mentally impaired.

This is because the woodland is also part of that space, and most people's dreams are confined within its boundaries.

This is also described in the book.

The protagonist witnesses the spiritual creatures in the woodland feeding upon their first entry into the woodland.

They are eating dream cocoons.

However, it doesn't have a significant impact on the person being eaten; the main effects are nightmares and several weeks of mental exhaustion.

Of course, a strong spirit alone isn't enough to enter that space; one also needs to master a specific language and recite it in a dream ritual.

The protagonist in the book mastered the language of birds, allowing him to enter the forest in bird form.

The book also included tutorials on how to have lucid dreams and the pronunciation of the so-called bird language.

After reading the secret manual, Su Mu gained a significant amount of [Moth] points, reaching the mid-stage of the seventh rank.

Su Mu immediately decided to try it out.

Then he discovered he couldn't dream at all…

Even hibernation was only possible after he acquired a human body; without a human form, he couldn't even lose consciousness.

And even with a body, he could only achieve a hibernation state similar to sleep; he couldn't dream at all.

I'll have Alice try it tonight…

Just as Su Mu took a sip of milk, looking troubled, Alice noticed him put down the book and immediately leaned closer to his head again.

After reading the secret manual, Su Mu didn't shoo Eyeball away, letting it stay there.

Once Eyeball was sure Su Mu didn't intend to send it away, it looked at Lorina triumphantly again.

Lorina's cheeks, already flushed from the chase, turned even redder.

"That's so despicable!"

Lorina yelled, then simply pounced into Su Mu's arms, hugging his waist and rubbing her head against his stomach.

"Lord of the Mist, Eyeball is bullying me!"

Lorina looked at Su Mu with teary eyes.

Su Mu could sense her displeasure and gently ruffled her hair.

After all, she was just a child seeking help from an adult when she was upset, even though he had no idea what had happened to Lorina.

Her head felt nice to touch.

Su Mu was always easygoing. Although he didn't know why Lorina was upset, coaxing her wouldn't be difficult. After all, he had accepted the deal, and he couldn't ignore the person he was protecting.

But Eyeball was unhappy. Seeing its privilege, which should have been its own, being taken away, it immediately flapped its wings, pointed at Lorina, and made some noises.

It sounded like it was cursing. How could Eyeball make noises? Do you even have that organ?

Su Mu looked at Eyeball, which was in front of him, sometimes pointing at Lorina and making noises, sometimes looking at him with a wronged expression, with some confusion.

Lorina, on the other hand, wore a proud expression, her previous grievances completely gone, replaced by an air of triumph.

It felt like two children fighting over a toy…

*Why…won’t…help me!*

Eyeball’s aggrieved cry echoed through the room. Su Mu sighed, put down his cup, reached out and took Eyeball in his hand, gently stroking its feathers with his other hand.

After all, it was his capable assistant, and it didn’t eat or cause trouble; its only problem was that it was a bit clingy. Such an excellent employee certainly couldn’t be mistreated.

Eyeball immediately calmed down after being stroked by Su Mu’s hands. Its pupils narrowed into slits, radiating a sense of contentment.

Lorina, deprived of her head pats, could only sullenly bury her head in Su Mu’s arms.

Both Lorina and Eyeball thought to themselves:

Just a pet!

Just as he had finished comforting the two, another phone call came in.

*Lord Su Mu, I’ve finished memorizing!*

Vanya's excited voice echoed in Su Mu's mind.

Her words carried a hint of exhaustion and heartache.

Su Mu was startled, then instinctively asked telepathically.

*You haven't been sleeping all day, have you?*

*Yes! Because it was Lord Su Mu's order, and...and I can't come to see Lord Su Mu until I've memorized it...*

Vanya's voice sounded both proud and shy.

Su Mu was exasperated. How could this child be so stubborn? It was also his fault; he was only focused on the battle last night and hadn't taken good care of her...

His body was already weak, and then he stayed up all night...

*Have you had breakfast?*

*Yes, the Sister brought me some.*

In the dormitory of the Church of the Holy Light of Salvation, Vanya ate breakfast with dark circles under her eyes. Although she was exhausted from staying up all night, her heart was warmed.

Lord Su Mu cares about me!

So happy...

The Sister stood behind her, tenderly patting her head.

"You're such a lazy child, staying up all night is one thing, but not even eating is a problem. Don't do this again next time."

Vanya mumbled between bites of bread, "I don't think I'll do it next time."

The elder sister gently tapped Vanya on the head.

"Not 'should,' 'definitely.'"

Vanya didn't answer, just chuckled twice.

"That depends on Lord Su Mu's orders. As a candidate for Saintess, everything is up to Lord Su Mu!"

The Sister High Priestess, seeing Vania's silly grin without a direct answer, could only sigh.

"You need to take good care of your health. I'll give you another half-day off today. Get some sleep."

"After all, he's already taken a liking to your body. If anything goes wrong, he won't be happy..."

The Sister High Priestess said a few more words before leaving, while Vania waited for Su Mu's next instructions.

*What does Lord Su Mu need me to do next?*

*Go to sleep!*



Chapter 95 Dirty Cooperation


*Hmm, okay...*

I thought I could talk to Lord Su Mu a little longer...

Although it was a pity, Vanya, having received instructions, didn't say anything. Already malnourished, she was now not only mentally exhausted but also physically weak.

Vanya obediently lay back down on the bed.

*Goodnight, Lord Su Mu...*

————

On the other side, the Sister entered the prayer room. Inside sat a middle-aged man wearing a police uniform.

The Sister bowed respectfully upon seeing the man and said, "Chief."

The man didn't look at her, continuing to flip through the newspaper in his hands.

[The renowned philanthropist, Ms. Viahi, recently visited Dexterity and expressed her intention to adopt several more children!]

[Ms. Viahi has never married and dedicated her life to charity. To date, she has adopted over 100 orphans, many of whom have achieved upward social mobility under her loving care and through their own talents!]

The newspaper carried this news in its largest section, with a black-and-white photograph in the upper right corner.

The photograph showed a woman who appeared to be in her early thirties, her hands resting on the shoulders of two children, gazing at them with compassionate eyes.

“How is she?”

“Everything is fine. Her spirits are high, even better than before. It’s just that her body is frail; she might not be able to withstand the enthusiasm of the noble gentlemen.”

“It’s alright, as long as she’s in good spirits. This buyer only values ​​her spirits; they won’t shortchange you.”

“Thank you for your generosity.”

This wasn’t the first time the two had exchanged such conversations.

The elder sister left quickly without saying anything more.

She knew very well that she couldn't ask too many questions in this kind of deal.

Just knowing the middleman's identity was dangerous enough.

Especially since the middleman was the police chief.

The elder sister was involved in human trafficking.

This had started when she was in charge of local orphan adoptions.

She herself had been an orphan adopted by the Church of Holy Light. Unlike other orphans, she had been sold by her parents, but managed to escape and was taken in by the church's elder sister.

From that moment on, she had only one thought in her mind.

All the suffering she had endured stemmed from her lack of money.

So she had to earn money.

Under the guidance of her former elder sister, she took over this position and began to oversee the church's adoption work.

In fact, she was no longer short of money after taking over as elder sister; the church provided a monthly allowance to the clergy in the name of Holy Light.

Although it wasn't as much as a middle-class person's monthly salary, they lived and ate at the church, so their lives were actually much better than those people's; at least they didn't have to worry about being evicted for not being able to pay rent.

But she wasn't satisfied; even so, she felt it wasn't enough.

Then she met the police chief.

She was on holiday and happened to witness the chief making a deal with a nobleman.

She saw the nobleman's payment to the chief.

And then she was tempted.

A 15-year-old boy could be exchanged for nearly 70,000 rupees.

So after their meeting, she approached the chief and struck a deal.

Because of his position, the police chief was often hesitant and cautious, and the children he recruited were mostly from impoverished areas.

He couldn't find many decent-looking children throughout the year.

She could solve this problem. While the children adopted by the church were also mostly from impoverished areas, under the church's care, they were generally good-looking, if not exceptionally beautiful.

Furthermore, because Dexterity was relatively remote, the church's supervision was lax, and they didn't check on the whereabouts of the orphans, giving her an opportunity to exploit the situation.

Although a nun who worked with her at the orphanage once noticed something amiss due to negligence,

with the police chief's help, they quickly fabricated a scene of her attempting suicide due to excessive pressure.

She also used this as a pretext to discourage others from wanting to manage the orphanage.

Why would anyone want a job that could drive someone to suicide?

After solving the problem, she provided the supply, and the police chief provided the clients.

Everything went so smoothly, and she quickly obtained a huge fortune.

But she was still not satisfied, or rather, she would never be satisfied…

This was just another ordinary transaction.

The only strange thing was that the chief always gave her food to give to Vanya.

She didn't know what the food was, but the chief had told her very seriously not to try it, so she neither touched it nor asked.

Although Vanya didn't seem to react after eating it.

This breakfast was the same; Vanya acted as if she were eating ordinary bread.

And this time, her reaction was even more normal than before when she fed Vanya. Usually, Vanya would pause for a moment after eating, but this time she didn't pause at all.

After the elder sister left, the man finished reading the newspaper and also left.

The man left with a slight sigh in his heart.

A senior nun from the Church of the Holy Light had gone so far as to assist him in human trafficking…

At first, he thought the senior nun was being used as bait, but he later discovered that wasn't the case.

The senior nun simply wanted money.

But he couldn't understand why, given the Church of the Holy Light's treatment of her, this was necessary.

However, it was also quite good; having someone with considerable power within the church to assist him in his transactions with those nobles would make things much smoother.

Especially after he recently had a formidable subordinate like Glenner.

His case-solving speed was like he had built-in answers, which terrified him, fearing that he might be investigated someday.

Therefore, he hadn't really relied on Glenner. Fortunately, Glenner resigned a few days ago, albeit by directly joining the royal organization…

And now he didn't even know what Glenner was doing.

Logically, he should have stopped. A police officer who had previously demonstrated exceptional detective talent under his command had suddenly joined the royal organization and become someone he absolutely couldn't afford to offend.

And this happened at such a sensitive time, right before he was about to complete the transaction.

It's hard not to suspect that he might have given himself away and been discovered.

This deal is incredibly lucrative; if it goes through, he'll never have to work again, and even his future children might not need to.

He's been working on this for over two years, finally finding a child who meets the buyer's requirements.

Now that it's almost done, wouldn't all his efforts over the past two years be wasted if he gives up now?

Although the buyer's deposit alone is 500,000, he'd still make a profit even if he ended the deal immediately.

But would a nobleman who can casually pull out that much money let him end the deal so easily?



Chapter 96 Dreaming


Inside the only inn in the western district of Dexterity.

A woman in an elegant dress sat by the window, cutting her steak with a knife and fork. Opposite her sat a handsome man who looked to be in his twenties.

The man was also dressed expensively, but not as brightly as the woman.

Just then, an elderly man approached with a smile, carrying a plate of steak.

The steak was drizzled with a thick black pepper sauce.

He placed the steak in front of the man.

"Sir, your steak, please enjoy."

The man nodded and smiled kindly at him.

"Okay, thank you, this is your tip."

The man said, taking out a wallet from his pocket and pulling out two large bills.

The old man quickly waved his hand.

“It’s my honor that you two are staying at my humble inn. How could I possibly ask for your money? Please don’t hesitate to call me if you need anything.”

The old man then seemed to suddenly remember something and said, “Oh, by the way, my name is Charno, and I’m the owner of this inn. It’s truly the greatest blessing of my life that I can personally entertain the kind Lady Visia before I pass away.”

While praising Visia’s generosity and kindness, the old man subtly tried to make himself known.

Upon hearing this, Visia put down her knife and fork, clasped her hands together in front of her chest, and made a gesture of prayer in the Church of the Holy Light.

“I’m merely practicing the teachings of Our Lady.”

Seeing this, the old man quickly made the same gesture. Although he wasn’t a believer in Our Lady, he knew he had to cater to her preferences to build a relationship.

“Your piety will surely move Our Lady.”

After chatting for a while, the old man took his leave, fearing that continuing the conversation would leave a bad impression on Visia.

The man began his meal after the old man left, and the two remained silent.

*Mother, won't this high-profile publicity attract attention? After all, many of our brothers and sisters have already… What if the An Yin Bureau discovers something amiss?*

*It's alright. According to the information I received from the Church of the End of All Things, the An Yin Bureau here is nothing but a bunch of good-for-nothings. There's nothing to worry about. I heard that after several years, they've only managed to get tickets to the local superhuman gathering.*

The two are communicating telepathically.

*Is that so? It seems the An Yin Bureau here really is just a bunch of good-for-nothings. But Mother, why did you come personally this time? Didn't you usually send other nobles?*

The man, relieved by Visia's confident reply, cut his steak more quickly, while asking with some doubt.

*This time is different. She's very talented.*

Hearing Visia's words of appreciation, the man couldn't help but feel a little displeased. Visia noticed this and chuckled, affectionately wiping the sauce from the corner of his mouth with a napkin.

*Relax, my child. When I said "talented," I meant as raw material. You know I've been changing faster and faster lately; a young heart alone can't keep up. Now I desperately need a powerful enough brain to provide the tools to suppress the potion.*

As if in response to the woman's words, dense wrinkles and age spots suddenly appeared at the corner of her left eye, but quickly returned to smooth skin as if it had all been an illusion.

The man, upon seeing this, showed pity and heartache in his eyes.

*Mother…I will go find the Dream Cocoon for you now!*

The man quickly finished his food and returned to his room on the second floor, lying on his bed to prepare to enter a dream.

Visia smiled as she watched the man go upstairs before preparing to leave as well.

As a benevolent noblewoman within the empire, she needed to properly promote her image.

If she encountered a promising talent, she could truly adopt them and raise them by her side, just like this son.

Which slum should she visit next?

Upstairs, the man lay on his bed and quickly entered a dream.

When he awoke again, he was fully conscious in his dream.

Without much time, he opened his mouth and whispered.

As he whispered, his form began to change. First, his limbs transformed into four animal legs, then a canine head took shape, with sharp teeth and fierce eyes.

After the whisper ended, he had completely transformed into a wolf.

He shook his head, then deliberately tore open his dream cocoon, peering out to observe the woodland environment.

Good, there are no spiritual creatures now; he can explore properly.

Although it's still midday and he probably won't find any dream cocoons, he doesn't have anything else to do and naturally wants to share the burden with his mother.

————

At noon, the An Yin Bureau returned with everyone carrying bags and looking exhausted.

The entire An Yin Bureau was bustling with activity. Half of them were examining the corpses of the spiritual creatures, collecting anything they couldn't understand and handing it over to headquarters later.

There were also arrangements made regarding the servants hired by the West family.

Those with inside information were all arrested; those who were just employees were dealt with depending on the circumstances.

The other half were examining the records found at the West family's residence, especially those concerning Senesa.

The An Yin Bureau had records of this evil god, but the details differed from the West family's records. In the An Yin Bureau's records, it was a pale, humanoid evil god, its only non-human feature being its legs.

Its legs were long, thin, and smooth, resembling a golden metal.

But looking at the West family's records, this Seneza seemed to be a spider with many legs and eyes?

Could there be a problem with the An Yin Bureau's files?

This thought was quickly dismissed by Blake. It was all because of recent setbacks; he'd become somewhat insecure about the An Yin Bureau...

Rubbing his temples, he noticed the exhausted Vivian.

"Vivian, you've worked so hard. Are you really alright now? Why don't you go to the lounge and get some sleep?"

Vivian weakly hummed in agreement and then swayed towards the lounge.

Blake watched her retreating figure and sighed.

Sigh, young people these days are just too overworked.

Vivian's work efficiency was frighteningly high when she was at West's house; she collected more than half of the information.

Blake could understand; she was clearly provoked by Glenner...

"Captain, want to go eat together?"

Glenner's voice came from behind, startling Blake, before he realized what was happening.

He'd only invited her to the cafeteria; she'd almost thought Glenn had sent her some new message again...

Blake turned and nodded to Glenn and Edric as they walked towards him.

————

Inside the church dormitory, Vania, asleep, found herself in a strange place.

Vania opened her eyes. What greeted her was a vibrant color unlike anything in the church, like something out of a children's picture book, with highly saturated hues.

Vania rubbed her eyes and got out of bed.

"Where am I?"



Chapter 97 I got scammed again...


Staring at the familiar yet slightly different church ceiling, Vanya stood there stunned for a long time before suddenly realizing what was happening.

"Am I dreaming?"

She'd had lucid dreams like this a few times before, so it was nothing new to her.

Then, an absurd thought popped into her head.

"If it's just a dream... it should be okay, right?"

She felt her cheeks burning, but she closed her eyes and mentally sketched Su Mu's face.

When she opened her eyes again, she found Su Mu standing in front of her, curiously looking around.

Vanya felt a surge of excitement. She swallowed hard and then poked Su Mu's cheek with a finger.

Seeing Su Mu's skin indent slightly from her touch, a strange sense of satisfaction welled up inside her.

She murmured, "It's a pity there's no physical contact in the dream..."

"Pity what?"

"Huh?"

Vanya watched as Su Mu turned his head to look at her after she poked him, feeling a little confused.

*Wait, something's not right.*

Am I dreaming? Why does Lord Su Mu look so real?

Su Mu saw Vanya suddenly freeze and waved his hand in front of her eyes.

*Hello?*

*Ah, I'm here!*

Vanya instinctively withdrew her hand, immediately clasped her hands together in front of her chest, and then knelt down before Su Mu.

*I hope Lord Su Mu can forgive my blasphemous behavior just now…*

Or perhaps divine punishment would be acceptable…

Su Mu waved his hand, his tone relaxed, "It's alright, I'm used to it. Get up."

Vanya breathed a sigh of relief and stood up after receiving Su Mu's instruction, but a few doubts lingered in her heart.

What does Lord Su Mu mean by saying she's used to it?

"So, are you dreaming?"

"Ah, yes, Lord Su Mu. I used to have lucid dreams like this from time to time."

"Impressive, quite gifted."

"Eh, really? Hehehe..."

Vanya scratched her head somewhat embarrassedly.

Su Mu hadn't expected the little nun he picked up on the roadside to have such a talent.

He was eating when he suddenly noticed that Vanya's soul was in a strange state, half-asleep and half-awake.

And she kept calling to him, so he decided to see what was going on.

Then he descended into Vanya's dream.

Su Mu hadn't expected to be able to enter dreams in this way. He had thought that in the future, he would only be able to enter the woods through spatial rifts like the one at the West's house.

"Lord Su Mu, how did you get here?"

Vanya asked nervously.

Did Lord Su Mu know what I was thinking just now?

"I came to take a look. Since you have this talent, you can accompany me in my experiment."

"Okay! I'll follow Lord Su Mu's arrangements."

Seeing Vanya's enthusiastic attitude, Su Mu tried to get her to master the language of the birds.

Good news: Vanya grasped languages ​​very quickly and learned it in no time.

Bad news: This isn't the so-called language of the birds at all!

Looking at Vanya, who had turned into a green snake on the table, Su Mu fell into deep thought.

The difference between this and the language of the birds is too great. Isn't the author of that secret manual afraid that someone will discover the discrepancy and come knocking on his door?

Vania, now a green snake, was also intrigued. She examined her body in the mirror, then turned to Su Mu and flicked her tongue.

*Lord Su Mu, how amazing!*

The green snake cautiously poked Su Mu's finger with its tail.

Seeing no reaction from Su Mu, Vania became bolder.

Since Lord Su Mu didn't stop her, that meant he agreed!

The green snake's tail coiled around Su Mu's ring finger.

"Fine, a snake it is then. Vania, try to open an opening here."

Su Mu held Vania in his hand and dragged her to the edge of her dream cocoon.

Vania felt a stiffness as she was held like this, but quickly recovered after hearing Su Mu's command.

*I'll try.*

The green snake first tried to bump its head against the opening, but to no avail. Then it tried to bite, but still to no avail.

Su Mu, watching, had his answer.

It seems that even these mimicry-like creatures can't leave their dream cocoons if they're not strong enough.

Su Mu placed his other hand on the inner wall of the dream cocoon to feel it.

It wasn't very thick, but if he were to attack, it would affect Vanya's mind, potentially causing her to become mentally weak for a period of time.

Su Mu was somewhat troubled. He still couldn't enter the forest… wait a minute.

“Vanya, change back first, I'll teach you something else.”

Vanya, who was still biting the wall with her teeth, immediately stopped upon hearing Su Mu's words and transformed back into human form.

“Now repeat after me.”

Vanya obediently nodded, then awkwardly repeated, “In the name of Su Mu, the Lord of the Mist and God of the Extraterrestrial Realms, I am granted a human body…”

————

Inside the forest, a black wolf was running aimlessly.

While it was safer at midday due to fewer spiritual creatures, there were also fewer dream cocoons.

Dreams capable of forming dream cocoons are few and far between, and given the lack of naps and dreaming at midday, he hadn't found a single one to harvest after wandering for so long.

Moreover, not only were there no dream cocoons, but he also discovered something truly terrifying.

The corpse of a spiritual creature.

While wandering the woods, he found the carcass of a gigantic spider.

The spider's legs were all severed and scattered on the ground, its body, cut in half, covered in a dense network of wounds.

Furthermore, on the path where he found the spider, he had also seen a giant tree that had been cleaved in half.

The clean cuts left him bewildered; what kind of power could have accomplished that?

Especially the spider's corpse. Even with his mimicry ability to help him resist some of the woods' mental pollution, seeing the spider's carcass still made him feel dizzy.

Although he didn't know the spider's exact strength, judging from the level of pollution in the corpse, its spiritual energy in life was roughly equivalent to his own, a [Moth] about to advance.

With mental pollution added, it would easily overpower him.

And this spiritual creature had actually died here.

And its death was so gruesome; he couldn't even imagine how powerful the being that killed the spider must have been…

So he only glanced at it from afar before turning and running away.

If he had accidentally run into it, he would have died eight hundred times over.

After wandering aimlessly for a long time, he was about to give up when he discovered a dream cocoon.

The dream cocoon, hanging on a tree branch, looked like a ripe apple.

Finally found it!

The black wolf excitedly ran to the tree and climbed upwards on all fours, finally reaching the dream cocoon, which was as large as himself.

He opened his mouth and bit down on the point where the dream cocoon was attached to the branch. The dream cocoon then fell to the ground and exploded into countless tiny, luminous orbs.



Chapter 98 Owl


Black Wolf quickly devoured the overflowing spiritual energy, then felt a pang of doubt.

Besides the [Moth's] spiritual energy, there was also [Awakening]—it seemed this was a dream cocoon of a transcendent being.

Black Wolf licked his lips.

A substantial harvest; this alone was enough to provide his mother with a week's worth of youth.

Black Wolf had achieved his goal and was about to return to his dream cocoon and leave the forest when he suddenly heard a sound.

There seemed to be movement in the bushes nearby.

He immediately assumed a fighting stance, staring at the rustling bushes, and spoke in human language.

"Who's there?"

The sound from the bushes suddenly stopped. After a moment, a wing emerged, swaying from side to side as if greeting someone.

"Y-you, hello?"

The voice was followed by the appearance of an owl's head.

"This is the first time I've encountered another superhuman like myself in the woods, I'm a little nervous..."

The owl was sweating profusely.

Today was her first time entering the woods under her mother's guidance.

Her mother had specifically instructed her that if she encountered any spiritual creatures, she should hide and run.

She did just that; while exploring westward, she had seen the corpse of a spiritual creature.

It was so large it felt like it could use her as a toothpick. Although it was completely dead, she didn't hesitate and ran back.

However, due to the mental pollution, her head was dizzy, and while flying, she didn't see the road clearly and crashed into a tree...

Then she landed in the bushes and lay there for a while.

After finally recovering, she decided to explore in other directions, and when she peeked out, she saw a black wolf had eaten a person's dream cocoon...

Her first reaction was that she had encountered a spiritual creature; she was so frightened that she didn't dare to make a sound and immediately shrank into the grass, cosplaying as an ostrich.

Then she heard the black wolf speak in human language.

She breathed a sigh of relief upon hearing the familiar Plit language, but then felt even more terrified.

Speaking in human language proved it was a superhuman, and eating dream cocoons proved it wasn't a good person either.

Most spiritual beings aren't very intelligent; with a little effort, she might be able to evade them, but how could she hide from a superhuman?

And she'd already been discovered…

And not close at all. Should she try flying away?

But the trees here are so dense, she couldn't fly fast at all…

What if this guy chased her and discovered her dream cocoon?

I've only just started; am I going to be bedridden for weeks on my first forest exploration?

The owl was brainstorming, while the black wolf was stunned for a moment, then overjoyed.

An unexpected gain?

She'd actually encountered a superhuman who also possessed the ability to mimic dream entry.

Besides the [moth] spirit of the dream cocoon, the most valuable resource in the forest was fellow superhumans.

As for the native spiritual creatures of the woodland… it's a miracle they're not eaten, let alone killed for resources?

The efficiency of obtaining spiritual energy by hunting extraordinary beings is far greater than consuming Dream Cocoons.

A fully digested ninth-tier potion might yield the equivalent of a newly acquired ninth-tier potion's worth of spiritual energy, which is already incredibly lucky. Let alone the fact that an ordinary person might need a dozen or more Dream Cocoons to produce even one ninth-tier potion's worth.

Superhumans, however, can produce quantities corresponding to their rank, though what they can produce is primarily [Moth] essence and a small amount of their own principle's spirituality.

Through special rituals, this spirituality can be brought into reality and attached to objects. Some [Moth] principle potions require materials from dreams.

Judging by the owl's size, it seems to be only rank nine; I can easily handle it.

The rank of a superhuman is relatively easy to distinguish in dreams, unlike in reality, where almost no one knows unless you use your abilities.

The size of the mimicked creature in the dream is directly linked to the amount of spirituality within the superhuman.

Rank nine beings are generally 2 to 3 meters long, rank eight rank 3 to 4 meters, and so on. However, I'm unsure what ranks above six are like.

I've heard that some rank five superhumans can reach 20 meters in length, depending on the creature they mimic.

While such a size would be exaggerated in reality, in the forest, it would still look like an insect.

The woods were filled with towering trees, making the black wolf's 4.5-meter length appear minuscule in comparison, and the owl, only 2 meters tall, even more so.

Otherwise, she wouldn't have been unable to fly out of the undergrowth for so long.

The black wolf chuckled.

"Hello, Miss Owl, this is my first time meeting another of my kind in the woods. Would you like to join me?"

"Ahaha... thank you for the invitation, but my mother is urging me to go home for dinner. Maybe next time..."

The owl spoke while subtly flapping her other wing, still entangled in the bushes.

Hurry up!

If you don't hurry, you'll be eaten!

"What's the rush? Speaking of food, how about I take you to find some Dream Cocoons to try? They're quite delicious..."

The black wolf spoke as she slowly approached the owl.

"No, really no! Thank you for your generosity and kindness, but I really have to go home!"

Tears welled in the owl's eyes. Seeing the approaching black wolf, she steeled herself and forcefully pulled her wing, stuck in the bushes, out.

The wing cut several gashes in the sharp branches of the bushes, from which a few traces of her own life force slowly seeped.

Ignoring the pain, the owl immediately flapped her wings and fled.

The black wolf immediately gave chase, inwardly scoffing.

With so many obstacles around, flying is slower than running on the ground, and you've even injured yourself. I don't even need to worry about you hiding anymore.

"Miss Owl, why are you running?"

"Then why are you chasing me!"

"I was trying to invite you to lunch."

"You want to make me your lunch?!"

The owl cried out as she flew, enduring the pain in her wing, constantly watching her surroundings to avoid crashing into obstacles.

"Mommy, help me!"

*Snap!*

A crisp sound of a branch breaking reached their ears.

The two stopped abruptly.

The black wolf swallowed hard, looking to his right in the direction the sound came from. The owl, perched on a branch, also watched nervously.

Could it be…?

The black wolf sensed something was wrong.

Could it be a spiritual creature…?

Aren't spiritual creatures usually only active at night?

If it is a spiritual creature, should he fight or run?

Although he was actually a [Blade] transcendent, his ability to enter dreams was due to a ritual taught to him by his mother.

However, he wasn't sure if he could defeat the spiritual creature. If he lost, all the spiritual energy he had just collected would be wasted.

The owl perched on a branch, its body shrinking further behind the leaves.

Perhaps this was a good opportunity? If that black wolf transcendent could be restrained by the spiritual creature, could I take the chance to escape back to my dream cocoon?

The two, each with their own thoughts, looked behind the tree trunk from which the sound had come.

Then they saw a hand appear in their field of vision.

A human hand.



Chapter 99 Vanya's Wonderland


Vania found it amazing. She had had lucid dreams a few times before, but this time she realized that her dreams had boundaries, which she had never paid attention to in her previous lucid dreams, as if they had been subconsciously ignored.

Although the thought of Su Mu suddenly appearing in her dream and almost discovering her somewhat impure thoughts...

she was still very happy.

Lord Su Mu came to my dream; he cares about me!

Especially afterward, she was transformed into a small snake by the spell Lord Su Mu taught her.

That wasn't the point; the point was that Lord Su Mu actually let her stay in his hand!

To be touched by the god she served—for a saintess, such a blessing was... was...

Too shameful; Vania herself didn't want to admit her own feelings.

At that moment, she only had one feeling:

Lord Su Mu's hand was so soft...

Afterward, Vania's head was spinning when responding to Su Mu's commands; she responded entirely on instinct.

Then Lord Su Mu taught her another spell.

Although the incantation was shorter than the previous one, the pronunciation difficulty was vastly different; it felt incredibly awkward and difficult to pronounce the first time.

She also noticed that after Su Mu finished speaking, he covered his mouth with his hand, lowered his head, and trembled slightly, as if trying to suppress a laugh.

As the one being laughed at, Vania should have felt somewhat ashamed, but besides a slight embarrassment, she felt mostly a sense of satisfaction.

Su Mu smiled because of me... This is the duty of a saintess!

To convey divine pronouncements and please the gods.

However, Vania misunderstood Su Mu here. While his accent played a part, the real reason Su Mu was suppressing his laughter was because he had actually managed to create an Alice-in-Waiting outfit for her by adding keywords to the Chinese text.

A light blue puff-sleeved dress paired with a white apron, a blue bow in her hair, traditional Mary Jane shoes, and white socks on her calves—the only difference being her hair color.

Su Mu felt like a genius. Although this design was originally meant for Alice's dream journey through the woods, since the inspiration struck, he used it on Vanya.

Afterwards, Vanya, now back in human form, successfully pushed open the door at the boundary and entered the woods.

After emerging from her dream cocoon, Vanya didn't wander around but turned back to ask Su Mu what she should do.

Then she saw Su Mu stuck inside the doorway.

"Lord Su Mu? What's wrong?"

Su Mu tried a few more times to extend his fingers, then gave up.

This body of his was merely created by Vanya using her impressions in a dream; naturally, he couldn't leave Vanya's dream.

"It's alright, go look around."

"But..."

Vanya hesitated, her eyes filled with reluctance as she looked at Su Mu.

“I’ve always been by your side. This body is merely a projection you created in your dream.”

As she spoke, Vania saw a cloud of black mist appear beside her, just like the one she had seen before.

“This black mist is my true form; the human body is just for convenience.”

“I see.”

Vanya nodded admiringly, then couldn't resist poking the ball of black mist in her hands.

This black mist was Lord Su Mu's true form...

That means, I... I'm...!

Su Mu suddenly looked at Vanya.

"What were you thinking about just now?"

"Nothing! I... I'm going to look around first, thank you Lord Su Mu!"

Before Su Mu could ask any further questions, Vanya ran off, steaming from her head, still clutching the ball of black mist.

...

Su Mu was somewhat speechless. He had suddenly felt that Vanya seemed to have some rather morbid emotions...

"It must be my imagination, right? What could a child like that possibly be thinking?"

As Vanya left, the space within her dream cocoon gradually turned into a pure white space. Su Mu's figure disappeared, his main focus shifting to the black mist held in Vanya's arms.

To be safe, Su Mu placed himself on Vanya's head, blurring her face to prevent any potential superhumans from seeing her, and then began wandering aimlessly through the woods.

This time, Su Mu had made some minor adjustments to ensure Vanya could see what was happening outside while remaining completely invisible to anyone from the outside.

In reality, Su Mu continued eating.

————

Back to the present.

Black Wolf's slow retreat halted, his entire body frozen in place.

A human... a human hand?

Humans in the woods?

Black Wolf felt like he was dreaming… he felt like he really was dreaming…

No, no.

He suddenly remembered that it was indeed possible to enter the woods physically using rituals and unstable space.

Has someone actually entered the forest?

The owl was stunned for a long time after seeing the hand.

Could there be a human in this forest?

Then they saw the figure emerge from behind a tree, supporting itself on the trunk.

It was a young girl, wearing a blue and white dress, with long brown hair cascading to her waist, and a blue bow in her hair.

While a girl appearing in this place was already eye-catching enough, what intrigued the two was her face.

A black mist obscured her entire face, not even her eyes.

At first glance, there were no facial features whatsoever.

The black mist on her head, as if it were alive, tidied her bow and brushed away a few leaves.

Is this really human?

Black Wolf was somewhat doubtful.

He had never seen any superhuman enter the forest wearing such inconvenient clothing. Did they want to die even faster?

"The trees here are so big..."

The girl looked up at the leaves that almost completely blocked out the sunlight and exclaimed.

*Someone's here.*

Su Mu's telepathic message reached Vania's ears, and she turned to look.

A giant black wolf and an owl, taller than herself, perched on a distant branch.

The girl's mouth gaped open.

"Are all the animals in my dreams this big?"

Unlike Vania's novel and relaxed mood,

the black wolf seemed hesitant.

It seemed this girl was a superhuman who had physically entered the forest.

But she appeared completely unaffected by the forest's mental pollution.

Either this isn't human, or it's a transcendent being of rank five or higher…

Both ways, Black Wolf felt immense pressure.

But she just mentioned dreams.

So, did this guy also enter through a dream?

Then why is he in human form?

A human couldn't possibly break through the inner walls of the dream cocoon, could he?

Suddenly, a third possibility occurred to him.

Is this guy using an ancient language?



Chapter 100 Stirring up the situation


Under Visia's tutelage, he had read some ancient histories. These histories supposedly depicted a distant past where human civilization existed, and almost all humans were extraordinary beings. However, a cataclysmic event wiped out all civilizations, and those who rebuilt later lost their ancient history. Only some of these histories described the state of the ancient civilizations.

He hadn't finished reading that particular history, because like secret traditions, it was considered corrupting. Prolonged study could lead to fear or "obsession," eventually affecting an extraordinary being's resistance to their own magical incantations. Therefore, his mother always had him read it in installments.

Now, after more than a decade, he had only read half of it.

If the girl before him was reciting incantations in an ancient language, then her entering the forest in human form, and seemingly unaffected by its spiritual corruption, would be a reasonable explanation.

While he was somewhat surprised that the girl appeared to possess an ancient language, it was still plausible.

She was probably, like himself, guided by a senior extraordinary being.

As for the black mist adjusting the girl's clothes and bow, it must be a characteristic of ancient people, right?

After all, people in ancient civilizations were all extraordinary beings, so it's normal for their mimicry to have some strange features.

Thinking this, Black Wolf's tense muscles relaxed a bit, and he began to observe the girl.

1.5 meters tall, doesn't even look like she's at the ninth rank. Did she become an extraordinary being by accumulating spiritual energy?

"This human lady, it's a pleasure to meet you."

————

Up in the tree, the owl thought she might be hallucinating from blood loss.

But then she suddenly remembered that in the forest, she was using mimicry, which at most involved the release of spiritual energy, not blood.

Wow, how come there's a human in the forest?

Didn't Mom say that humans entering the forest are courting death?

Why does this little girl look so relaxed and carefree?

The owl's worldview was shattered. Even in a dream, this was too absurd!

"Am I dreaming?"

Still reeling from the shock, the owl heard the black wolf below begin talking to the girl, slowly approaching her.

"That person is dangerous!"

The owl wanted to warn her, but the pain in her wing told her she should take this opportunity to escape.

Although she didn't know what was going on with this girl, the fact that she could stay in the forest so easily suggested that her human body was probably a mimicry, right?

And that wisp of black mist didn't look like something a human would possess.

"If that's the case, even if the girl was really eaten, she'd probably only be bedridden for a few weeks..."

—

Vanya looked up at the sound and saw the black wolf approaching her.

*Lord Su Mu, the wolf is talking!*

*It should be a superhuman.*

*A superhuman?*

*In any case, not a good person.*

*So what should I do next?*

*Watch my actions.*

The black mist on Vanya's head stopped fiddling with its bow, and the transparent black mist began to gradually spread out.

I don't know what kind of combat power this mimic superhuman possesses. I'll play it safe and spread the black mist until it enters my attack range…

"...Wait, I don't think I have any distance restrictions in the forest?"

"The black mist's intensity will increase in all aspects. Alice's ninth-tier black mist allows me to easily kill seventh-tier spiders. Even an ordinary person like Vanya, I could at least fight a seventh-tier spider in the forest."

Thinking this, Su Mu, controlling Vanya, relaxed.

He crossed his arms and looked at the black wolf with extreme arrogance.

"Where did this wild dog come from?"

Everyone froze upon hearing this. The owl looked at the girl, wondering if he had misheard.

So arrogant?

Vanya had lost control of the dream mimicry, her consciousness observing everything from within her own consciousness space.

Upon hearing Su Mu's words, she clapped excitedly, exclaiming, "Lord Su Mu is so cool!"

The black wolf paused, staring at Vanya in astonishment.

"Wait, how dare you, someone who isn't even ninth-tier, be so arrogant?"

"Look at my size! I'm 4.5 meters tall, a seventh-tier expert! How dare you talk to me like that?"

"Are you dreaming while drunk?"

Black Wolf was momentarily confused, unsure whether he should continue forward.

"You…"

Black Wolf opened his mouth but didn't know what to say, finally closing it again. His eyes flashed fiercely at Vanya, then he charged towards her with all his might.

"Whatever," he thought, "what strength could a superhuman whose mimicry is only tiny possibly have?"

Su Mu pointed at Black Wolf, a wisp of black mist gathering at her fingertip, faintly glowing with a pale red light.

Black Wolf noticed the black mist at the girl's fingertip, but after a quick assessment, he decided to ignore it.

"A self-taught superhuman who hasn't even reached the ninth tier, even if she has attack capabilities, couldn't possibly kill me instantly."

"Since I can't kill her instantly, inflicting wounds on myself is just a way to strengthen my own stats. I can use this girl's attacks to boost my attributes."

Just as the human and the wolf were facing off, a dark shadow flew in from the other side.

Before the wolf could reach Vania, the owl arrived first, grabbing Vania's shoulders and lifting her into the air.

The wolf's attack missed, and it glared at the owl in the sky, clearly annoyed.

"Miss Owl, was it really necessary to come back with such a good opportunity?"

Even if you ran, I could still track you down using your spiritual energy.

The owl ignored him and began flying off in one direction with Vania.

She couldn't bear to use Vania as bait, especially seeing that Vania was still in a human body.

Running away now would feel like abandoning her companion.

"Hey, your Dream Cocoon shouldn't be in this direction, right?"

The owl asked softly as it flew.

Vania, or rather, Su Mu, whom she was holding, didn't reply.

The owl's talons didn't grip Vanya's shoulder, but instead seized the invisible layer of black mist covering her body.

Su Mu had covered Vanya's body with a layer of defensive black mist to prevent the black wolf from suddenly unleashing some kind of laser attack.

Although it was made from black mist that had descended using Vanya as an anchor point, it was still sufficient to provide the same defensive effect as an eyeball in the semi-advantageous environment of the woodland.

That's why Su Mu arrogantly chose to charge up his attack in place.

But he was interrupted by this owl.

Su Mu did react; he could have dodged the owl's claws if he wanted to.

However, since he didn't sense any hostility from the owl, Su Mu didn't choose to dodge. Instead, he let himself be grabbed to see what the owl wanted to do.

Su Mu looked at the black wolf running below and found this experience quite novel. This was the first time he had ever been chased…

Usually, he was the one chasing others…
